```
          → Define whether to enable:
            • Caching only
            • Caching AND compression

  Step 2: Create an ACCELERATION RESOURCE
          → Binds (attaches) the policy to a specific load balancer

  One policy can be bound to MULTIPLE load balancers,
  but each requires its own separate acceleration resource.

  ┌──────────────────┐     attached via      ┌──────────────┐
  │  WAA Policy      │◄──────────────────────│ Acceleration │──► Load Balancer 1
  │  (caching +      │                        │ Resource 1   │
  │   compression)   │◄──────────────────────│ Acceleration │──► Load Balancer 2
  └──────────────────┘                        │ Resource 2   │
                                              └──────────────┘
```

**Caching:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Caching Behavior                                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  How it works:
  • Backend server responds → load balancer CACHES the response
  • Subsequent requests for same content → served from CACHE (not backend)

  Benefits:
    1. Reduces LOAD on backend servers (requests served from LB cache)
    2. Reduces LATENCY (faster response from cached data vs backend)

  Caching rules:
  • Only HTTP status code 200 responses are cached
  • Only GET and HEAD requests are cached
  • Responses with Set-Cookie header are NOT cached
  • Dynamic pages should NOT be cached (risk of leaking secrets
    like OAuth state parameters)
  • Default cache duration: 10 minutes (configurable)
    - Static content → configure longer cache duration
    - Frequently changing content → configure shorter cache duration
  • Maximum cache size: 100 MB
    - When 100 MB is reached, resources accessed the FEWEST times
      are evicted first (LFU — Least Frequently Used eviction)
  • Cached content is available even if the file is removed from
    the backend server (until expiry or manual purge)
```

**Cache Purge:**

```
  Cache purge = manually removing cached data from the load balancer

  When to use:
  • Backend content has changed but cached version is outdated
  • Need to force fresh content to be served from backend

  ⚠️  Cache purge is an IRREVERSIBLE action — cannot be restored
      after purging
```

**Compression:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Compression Behavior                                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  How it works:
  • Backend server responses are COMPRESSED before being returned
    to the web application user

  Benefits:
    1. Reduces BANDWIDTH required to transmit responses
    2. Increases SPEED of server responses over the network

  Supported algorithm: gzip ONLY (currently)

  Only applies alongside caching — you cannot enable compression
  without caching in the WAA policy
```

**Impact of WAA (Metrics Overview):**

```
  ┌─────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Metric                         │  Effect after enabling WAA     │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │  Client bytes received          │  Decreases — due to            │
  │                                 │  compression (smaller payload) │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │  Load balancer bytes sent       │  Decreases — due to            │
  │                                 │  compression                   │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
  │  Backend bytes sent             │  Significant decrease — due to │
  │                                 │  caching (most requests served │
  │                                 │  from LB cache, not backend)   │
  └─────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key Points:**

- WAA speeds up Layer 7 HTTP load balancers using **caching** (reduces backend load and latency) and **compression** (reduces bandwidth and improves speed)

- Two-step setup: create a **WAA Policy** (define caching/compression) → create an **Acceleration Resource** (binds policy to a specific LB)

- One policy can be applied to multiple load balancers via separate acceleration resources

- **Caching** applies only to HTTP **200** responses to **GET and HEAD** requests; responses with **Set-Cookie header** are not cached

- **Do not cache dynamic pages** — risk of leaking secrets (e.g., OAuth state parameters)

- Default cache TTL: **10 minutes**; max cache size: **100 MB** (LFU eviction when full)

- **Cache purge** removes cached data and forces backend to serve fresh content — **irreversible**

- **Compression** uses **gzip only**; can only be enabled together with caching (not standalone)

================================================================================


**DNS (Domain Name System):**

**What is DNS:**

- DNS translates easy-to-remember symbolic domain names into IP addresses that machines understand

- Similar to saving phone numbers with names in contacts - you remember the name (domain), machine uses the number (IP address)

- Associates human-friendly domain names with numerical IP addresses

**DNS Query Flow:**

- User enters www.example.com in browser → Machine queries DNS server (recursive/non-authoritative)

- DNS server queries Root Servers (.) asking for IP of www.example.com → Root servers respond "ask .com"

- DNS server queries TLD (Top Level Domain) servers for .com → TLD servers respond "ask example.com"

- DNS server queries Customer DNS servers (authoritative) for example.com → Authoritative DNS servers return actual IP address

- DNS server returns IP address to browser → Browser displays website content

**DNS Server Types:**

- Authoritative DNS Servers: Actually hold the DNS records (e.g., customer DNS servers for example.com)

- Recursive/Non-Authoritative DNS Servers: Query other DNS servers on behalf of clients (e.g., central DNS server in query flow)

**OCI DNS Capabilities:**

- Create and manage DNS zones that your company owns

- Add records to zones (e.g., Name Server records, Start of Authority records)

- Zone types supported: Primary zones

- Allow OCI's edge network to handle domain's DNS queries

- Import zone files in standard BIND zone data format (industry preferred format)

**OCI Public DNS Benefits:**

- Consistently lowest query latency performance

- Queries served via anycast from OCI's global commercial regions

- Built-in DDoS protection - highly resilient against outages and attacks

- Industry-leading propagation time for fast response to DNS changes

================================================================================

**DNS Service Components:**

- Domain: Specific location or group of locations on internet as a whole, complete part of DNS tree delegated to user's control (e.g., oracle.com)

- Zone: Part of DNS namespace that contains Start of Authority (SOA) record and nameserver record when created

- Label: Prepended to zone name or separated by period to form subdomain name (e.g., "www" in www.oracle.com is the label)

- Child Zone: Independent subdomain with its own SOA and nameserver records - parent zone must contain nameserver record that refers DNS queries to nameservers responsible for child zone

- Resource Records: Records containing specific domain information for particular zone - A record contains IP address for domain name, MX records contain mail server information for domain, AAAA record for IPv6 addresses

================================================================================

**DNS Zones:**

- DNS Zone is portion of DNS namespace managed by specific organization or administrator - acts as administrative space allowing granular control of DNS components like authoritative nameservers

- Holds trusted DNS records that reside on OCI nameservers (A record, AAAA record, MX record, CNAME record, etc.)

- When zone created, two records automatically generated: Nameserver record and Start of Authority (SOA) record

- Two types of zones in OCI: Public Zone and Private Zone

- Public Zone: Holds trusted DNS records residing on OCI nameservers for public internet access

- Private Zone: Contains domain names that resolve DNS queries for private addresses within Virtual Cloud Network (VCN)

================================================================================

**Public DNS Zone:**

**What is Public DNS Zone:**

- Used to resolve hostnames on internet (public accessibility)

- Hosts trusted DNS records that reside on OCI nameservers

**Three Steps to Implement Public DNS Zone:**

**Step 1 - Create Public DNS Zone:**

- Create public DNS zone in OCI

- On successful creation, automatically generates two records: Nameserver (NS) record and Start of Authority (SOA) record

- Must create public DNS zone with same name as your purchased domain name (e.g., if domain is abc.com, create zone named abc.com)

**Step 2 - Delegate the Zone:**

- Purpose: Makes hosted zone accessible from internet

- Copy nameserver records generated when public DNS zone was created in OCI

- Update/replace nameserver records in domain registrar's DNS panel (e.g., GoDaddy) with OCI nameserver records

- By default, domain registrar provides their own nameservers when you purchase domain - replace those with OCI nameservers

- This step performed in registrar DNS panel, not in OCI

**Step 3 - Add Records to Zone:**

- Add different types of DNS records to zone (A record, AAAA record, CNAME record, MX record, etc.)

- Each record type contains rdata (record data) - e.g., A record contains IP address as rdata

- Records can be added only after zone is created and delegated

================================================================================

**Private DNS Zone:**

**DNS Options in VCN (3 Choices):**

**Option 1 - Internet and VCN Resolver (Default):**

- Internet Resolver: Lets instances resolve hostnames publicly published on internet

- VCN Resolver: Lets instances resolve hostnames of other instances within same VCN using fully qualified domain name (FQDN)

**Option 2 - Custom Resolver:**

- Select DNS server of your choice with multiple deployment options: DNS server available over internet, Custom DNS server in your VCN, Custom DNS server in on-premises network

**Option 3 - Private DNS:**

- Provides hostname resolution within VCN (like VCN resolver)

- Provides hostname resolution across VCNs (between multiple VCNs)

- Provides hostname resolution between VCN and on-premises network or any other private network

- Previously required custom solution for cross-VCN hostname resolution, now handled by Private DNS service

**Private DNS Zone Components/Resources:**

**Private DNS Zone:**

- Contains the DNS data

- Each zone can belong to single view only

**Private DNS View:**

- Collection of private DNS zones

**Private DNS Resolver:**

- Contains configuration that serves responses to DNS queries within VCN

- Structure: Resolver contains Views → Views contain Zones → Zones contain Record data

- Views on resolver decide zone and record data applicable for response

**Private DNS Records:**

- A record: Hostname to IPv4 mapping

- AAAA (Quad A) record: Hostname to IPv6 mapping

- Pointer (PTR) record: Reverse DNS lookup

- Private DNS zones support multiple DNS record types

**Private DNS Resolver Endpoints:**

**Purpose and Configuration:**

- Sets up ingress and egress in VCN

- Consumes private IP addresses from subnet

- Corresponding VNIC created for each resolver endpoint

**Two Types of Resolver Endpoints:**

**Listening Resolver Endpoint:**

- Responds to DNS queries coming from outside VCN or outside OCI (e.g., from on-premises network)

- Handles inbound DNS queries

**Forwarding Resolver Endpoint:**

- Uses rules to redirect/forward queries to different DNS servers

- Handles outbound DNS queries from VCN to external DNS servers

**Example Flow:**

- On-premises VM wants to resolve OCI VCN VM hostname → Listener endpoint responds to query from outside

- OCI VCN VM wants to resolve on-premises VM hostname → Forwarder endpoint redirects query to on-premises DNS server

================================================================================

**Private Views:**

- Private view is collection of private DNS zones - allows logical grouping of set of private DNS zones

- Each DNS zone can only belong to single view - cannot belong to multiple views simultaneously

- When VCN created, it has resolver with default view that can contain zones

- Can create and attach additional custom views to resolver (in addition to default view which always exists)

- Purpose of creating and attaching views to resolver: Make zones resolvable in VCN and share private DNS data across VCNs

- One particular view can be used by multiple resolvers, but each zone limited to single view only

================================================================================

**HTTP Redirects:**

- DNS service allows redirection of HTTP traffic from one URL to another URL

- One-to-one mapping between source and target domains

**Four HTTP Redirect Scenarios:**

- Scenario 1: Redirect all HTTP traffic for entire zone to another zone (e.g., redirect all traffic from test.net to test.com)

- Scenario 2: Redirect specific subdomain to HTTP URL (e.g., subdomain.test.net to different URL)

- Scenario 3: Redirect subdomain to URL with port number (e.g., example.test.net to http://sample.test.net:8080)

- Scenario 4: Permanently redirect domain name using 301 response code - used when domain deprecated, informs search engines and browsers what to do with information

**Response Codes:**

- 301: Moved Permanently (permanent redirect)

- 302: Temporary redirect

**Important Requirement:**

- Must create relevant DNS record (CNAME record) and add it to DNS zone for redirect to work successfully

- Redirect configuration includes: Zone selection, Domain and subdomain information, Target (protocol, host, port), Optional path and query parameters

================================================================================

**Traffic Management:**

**Overview:**

- DNS service component that provides intelligent responses to DNS queries based on defined conditions

- Sits between user DNS query and endpoints, routing traffic based on logic/rules defined in steering policies

- Conditions can include: geographical location of user, health of endpoints, traffic distribution requirements

- DNS responses are constructed from steering policy logic, not from DNS zone records directly

**Traffic Management Components:**

- **Steering Policies:** Framework containing rules/logic to intelligently serve DNS answers; defines traffic management behavior for public DNS zones

- **Attachment:** Links steering policy to public DNS zone; when attached, DNS responses are constructed from steering policy rather than domain records in the zone

- **Rules:** Guidelines used by steering policies to filter traffic based on DNS request properties (geolocation, endpoint health, etc.)

- **Answer Pools:** Contain the endpoint records that will be provided as answers to DNS queries

**Steering Policy Types:**

- **Failover Policy:** Route traffic to primary endpoint; automatically switch to backup endpoint if primary is unavailable

- **Load Balancer Policy:** Distribute traffic among multiple endpoints for load distribution

- **Geolocation Steering Policy:** Route traffic based on user's geographical location

- **ASN Steering Policy:** Route traffic based on Autonomous System Number

- **IP Prefix Steering Policy:** Route traffic based on IP address prefixes

================================================================================

**Traffic Management Policy Types:**

**1. Failover Policy:**

- Prioritize order in which answers are served (primary and secondary endpoints)

- OCI health checks monitor all endpoints continuously

- DNS traffic steered to primary when both endpoints are healthy

- Automatic failover to secondary endpoint when primary becomes unhealthy or unavailable

**2. Load Balancer Policy:**

- Distributes DNS traffic across multiple endpoints

- Supports weighted distribution: even distribution (50/50) or custom ratios (10/90, etc.)

- Flexible weight configuration per endpoint

- Automatic traffic redirection to healthy endpoints when one becomes unavailable

**3. Geolocation Steering Policy:**

- Distributes DNS traffic based on end user's geographical location

- Can configure routing at country level or region level

- Different locations can be directed to different endpoints

- Global user base can be divided into geographically defined regions

**4. ASN Steering Policy:**

- Routes traffic based on Autonomous System Numbers (ASN)

- DNS queries originating from specific ASN or set of ASNs can be steered to specific endpoints

- Useful for enterprise or mobile operator-based routing

**5. IP Prefix Steering Policy:**

- Steers DNS traffic based on IP prefix of originating query

- Enables differentiation between internal and external users

- Useful for canary testing and selective routing

**Traffic Management Use Cases:**

- **Automated Failover:** Automatic switching between primary and secondary servers for high availability

- **Cloud Migration:** Use weighted load balancing to gradually migrate traffic (start with 10%, then increase ratios until full migration)

- **Multi-Cloud & Hybrid Support:** Service-agnostic - works with OCI resources, other cloud providers, and on-premise data centers (must be publicly exposed/resolvable)

- **Worldwide Geolocation Steering:** Divide global users into regions and steer to specified resources based on physical location

- **Canary Testing:** Use IP prefix steering to serve different responses for internal vs external users

- **Zero-Rating Services:** Use ASN steering to direct preferred ASNs to free resources and other traffic to paid resources

================================================================================

**Network Command Center:**

- Unified experience for all virtual network monitoring needs - access all tools from a single place

- Provides tools for visualization, monitoring, and troubleshooting of virtual networks

**Network Command Center Tools:**

- **Network Visualizer:** Provides interactive visualization of virtual network topology; shows resource interconnections and dependencies

- **Network Path Analyzer:** Identifies virtual network configuration issues by analyzing configured connectivity; helps with troubleshooting connectivity problems

- **Flow Logs:** Captures network traffic information to support monitoring and security needs

- **Inter-Region Latency Dashboard:** Provides view of network health and metrics for network service elements across all regions

- **Virtual Test Access Points (VTAP):** Mirrors all traffic to/from designated source and sends it to selected target; facilitates troubleshooting, security analysis, and data monitoring

- **Capture Filter:** Contains set of rules governing what traffic is captured by VTAP or Flow Log

================================================================================

**Inter-Region Latency:**

**OCI Backbone Network:**

- Global private IP network that Oracle Cloud workloads use when communicating between different regions

- Inter-region latency reflects traffic flowing through this backbone network

**Inter-Region Latency Dashboard:**

- Displays average inter-region round-trip latency for all pairs of regions in an OCI realm

- Helps customers make intelligent region selections for business expansion

- Not available in realms with only one region

- Latency information is not specific to your tenancy's workloads - provides general visibility into latency between all regions

**Two Chart Types:**

- **Current Inter-Region Round-Trip Time:** Real-time snapshot expressed in milliseconds; average of values over last 5 minutes; view updates every minute; displays round-trip time in each cell based on origin-destination pair

- **Inter-Region Round-Trip Time (Historical):** Shows historical view of last 30 days; allows selection of source and destination regions; helps validate network performance over time

**Use Cases:**

- **Network Planning:** Determine performance impact of different regional selections; helps make optimal selection considering disaster recovery, compliance, data residency, and performance factors

- **Troubleshoot Performance Issues:** Validate both real-time and historic network performance using the dashboard

- **Monitoring Integration:** Utilize latency information through monitoring API; integrate metrics into on-premise monitoring platforms; receive notifications when defined latency thresholds are exceeded

================================================================================

**Network Path Analyzer:**

**Overview:**

- Network Command Center tool that identifies connectivity problems using automated configuration analysis

- Analyzes routing rules and security rules by querying networking constructs to check reachability

- Provides hop-by-hop path visualization for troubleshooting

- Supports both bi-directional (forward and return path) and uni-directional analysis

**Key Features:**

- Precisely points out routing and security configuration issues

- Supports connectivity to Oracle Services Network via service gateway or private endpoint

- No actual traffic is sent - only configuration is examined to confirm reachability

- Free service - does not incur any cost regardless of number of analyses run

**Supported Scenarios:**

- Source and destination both within OCI (e.g., VCN to VCN via DRG)

- OCI to on-premises connectivity

- OCI to internet connectivity

**Example Issues Detected:**

- VCN to VCN: Security list misconfiguration causing traffic denial despite proper DRG and VCN attachments

- OCI to Internet: Missing route configuration and egress rule denying access to public DNS servers

**Use Cases:**

- **Troubleshoot Misconfigurations:** Identify routing and security issues causing connectivity problems; significantly reduces mean time to resolution

- **On-Demand Validation:** Verify logical network paths match intent before onboarding production applications; avoids delays in application deployment

- **Proactive Verification:** Validate network routing and security policy configurations before issues occur

================================================================================

**Network Visualizer:**

**Overview:**

- Service that helps visualize entire network of any complexity very easily

- As more VCNs, subnets, and gateways are added to architecture, visualization becomes difficult - Network Visualizer solves this challenge

- Provides diagram of implemented topology of all VCNs in selected region and tenancy

- Start with regional topology and drill down to subnet topology to get complete network information

**Three Topology Views:**

- **Regional Topology Map:** High-level layout of entire virtual network configuration; shows DRGs, VCNs, customer premises equipment, and different gateway types; provides bird's-eye view of whole infrastructure

- **VCN Topology Map:** Organization of single VCN including its subnets and routing configuration; detailed view of individual VCN components

- **Subnet Topology:** Information about instances, load balancers, and services like OKE clusters within the subnet; most granular view

**Use Cases:**

- **Visualize and Troubleshoot Security:** See relationships between security lists and NSGs with other virtual network resources

- **Deeper Insights:** Visualize relationships between resources and understand impact of any architectural changes

- **DRG Troubleshooting:** Enhanced view of dynamic routing gateway attachments to easily troubleshoot transit routing configuration issues

================================================================================

**Capture Filters:**

**Overview:**

- Filter used with Flow Logs and Virtual Test Access Points (VTAP) to select which traffic to include

- Prevents capturing all traffic which can cause performance degradation

- Allows selection of only required traffic based on defined criteria

**Key Characteristics:**

- Capture filter is associated with VTAP configuration and defines what type of traffic to capture

- VTAP must have a capture filter associated with it (mandatory)

- Each capture filter must have at least 1 rule and can have up to 10 rules maximum

- If rule doesn't specify CIDR block/prefix or IP protocol, all IP addresses or IP protocols are accepted for that rule

**Rules Configuration:**

- Can create rules with include or exclude actions

- Rule parameters: Source and destination CIDR, Protocol, Source and destination port

- Traffic direction setting: Captures traffic based on ingress or egress criteria

- Example: Ingress HTTP traffic from internet (source 0.0.0.0/0) to subnet A, TCP protocol, destination port 80

**Rule Evaluation Order (CRITICAL):**

- Capture filter rules are examined in sequential order

- Rules are executed when matched

- When a match is found, remaining rules are NOT examined or executed on that packet

- If you reorder the rules, capture filter behavior changes - ordering is critical

**Rule Order Example:**

- Goal: Capture all traffic from 10.1.0.0/16 except 10.1.1.1

- **Correct Order:** Rule 1: Exclude 10.1.1.1/32 → Rule 2: Include 10.1.0.0/16 (works correctly - excludes 10.1.1.1 first, then includes rest)

- **Incorrect Order:** Rule 1: Include 10.1.0.0/16 → Rule 3: Exclude 10.1.1.1/32 (doesn't work - traffic from 10.1.1.1 matches first rule and gets included, third rule never evaluated)

================================================================================

**Virtual Test Access Points (VTAP):**

**Overview:**

- Provides way to mirror traffic from designated source to selected target for troubleshooting, security analysis, and data monitoring

- VTAP source and target must be hosted in the same VCN

- If multiple VTAPs have overlapping source configuration, traffic from VTAP with more specific source will be mirrored

**Three VTAP Components:**

- **VTAP Source:** Resource that VTAP monitors; traffic on this resource is mirrored and sent to chosen target

- **VTAP Target:** Receives mirrored traffic from selected VTAP source; must be network load balancer with UDP listener on port 4789

- **Capture Filter Rules:** Select what traffic is included in mirrored traffic from source to target

**VTAP Source Options:**

- Single compute instance VNIC in a subnet

- Load balancer

- Database system

- Exadata VM cluster

- Autonomous database

**Traffic Flow:**

- Traffic is encapsulated in VxLAN format

- Sent to VTAP target (network load balancer) via UDP port 4789

- Backend set of network load balancer performs appropriate analysis on mirrored traffic

**VTAP Target Requirements:**

- Must be a network load balancer with UDP listener on port 4789

- Must be in same VCN as VTAP source

- Backend set performs analysis on received mirrored traffic

**Use Cases:**

- **Deep Packet Inspection:** Detect anomalous behavior in network traffic

- **Compliance:** Log and monitor traffic to meet regulatory mandates

- **Troubleshooting:** Identify issues impacting application performance

================================================================================

**Compute Service Overview:**

- OCI Compute Service lets you provision and manage compute hosts (also known as instances)

- Serves as foundation for other OCI services

- Offers variety of shapes designed to meet range of compute and application requirements

- Shape is template that defines number of OCPUs, amount of memory, and other resources allocated to an instance

**Instance Types:**

- **Bare Metal Instances:** Provides dedicated physical server access

- **Virtual Machine Instances:** Independent computing environment that runs on top of physical bare metal hardware

**Data Persistence:**

- Changes to instance local drives are lost when instance is terminated

- Changes saved to volumes attached to instance are retained after termination

================================================================================

**Platform and Custom Images:**

**Instance Images Overview:**

- When creating compute instance, must specify shape and image

- Image determines operating system and other software for instance

- Image is template of virtual hard drive

**Image Sources:**

- **Platform Images:** Pre-built operating systems provided by Oracle for OCI (Oracle Linux, Ubuntu, CentOS, Microsoft Windows, Oracle Autonomous Linux)

- **Oracle Images:** Oracle Enterprise images and solutions enabled for OCI

- **Custom Images:** Create custom image from existing instances running in OCI; includes customizations, configurations, and software installed when image was created

- **Partner Images:** Trusted third-party images published in marketplace by Oracle partners

- **Import Custom Images (BYOI):** Bring your own custom images into OCI environment

**Custom Images Behavior:**

- When creating image of running instance, instance shuts down and remains unavailable for several minutes

- Instance restarts automatically when image creation process completes

- Instances launched from custom image include all customizations, configurations, and software installed at time of image creation

**Bring-Your-Own-Image (BYOI):**

- Enables bringing your own OS versions to cloud (as long as underlying hardware supports it)

- Limitation: Maximum image size that can be imported is 400 GB

**Windows Custom Images:**

- **Generalized Images:** Images cleaned of instance-specific information

- **Specialized Images:** Point-in-time snapshots of boot disk of running instance; useful for creating backups

================================================================================

**Image Import/Export and BYOI:**

**Image Import/Export Overview:**

- Can share custom images across tenancies and regions using image import/export

- Leverages OCI Object Storage for replicating images across regions

**Three Import Launch Modes:**

- **Emulation Mode:** For virtual machines that don't support paravirtualized drivers created outside of OCI (older on-premise physical or virtual machines)

- **Paravirtualized Mode:** For virtual machines that support paravirtualized drivers but created outside of OCI environment

- **Native Mode:** For images that were exported from Oracle Cloud Infrastructure

**Steps to Replicate Image Across Regions:**

- Step 1: Export image to object storage bucket in same region as the image

- Step 2: Copy image to object storage bucket in destination region

- Step 3: Obtain URL path to the image object

- Step 4: Import image in destination region using the URL path

**Bring Your Own Image (BYOI) Details:**

- Export virtual machines from existing virtualization environment and import directly to OCI as custom image

- Supported import formats: qcow2 and VMDK formats

- Once image is imported, can create instance from custom image

**BYOI Benefits:**

- Enables virtual machine cloud migration projects

- Supports both old and new operating systems

- Encourages experimentation with different OS versions

- Increases infrastructure flexibility

- **Important:** Must comply with all licensing requirements

================================================================================

**Compute Shapes:**

**Shape Definition:**

- Template that determines number of OCPUs, amount of memory, and other resources allocated to an instance

- Compute shapes available with AMD processors, Intel processors, and ARM-based processors

**Two Shape Options:**

**1. Fixed Shape:**

- Cannot customize number of OCPUs and amount of memory

- Can create both bare metal instances and virtual machine instances using fixed shapes

**2. Flexible Shape:**

- Can customize number of OCPUs and amount of memory

- Can only create virtual machine instances (not bare metal) using flexible shapes

- Maximum OCPUs and memory vary based on processor type

**Flexible Shapes Characteristics:**

- Example naming: VM.Standard.E3.Flex, VM.Standard3.Flex

- Customize number of OCPUs and memory when launching or resizing VM

- Network bandwidth and number of vNICs scale proportionately with number of OCPUs

- Maximum memory: 64 GB per OCPU (e.g., 4 OCPUs = 256 GB max memory)

**Benefits of Flexible Shapes:**

- Build VMs that match your specific workload requirements

- Optimize performance for applications

- Minimize costs by selecting only needed resources

- Example: VM.Standard3.Flex allows 1-32 OCPUs with up to 64 GB memory per CPU

================================================================================

**Compute GPU Shapes:**

**Overview:**

- GPU shapes designed for hardware-accelerated workloads

- Hardware acceleration: Process where applications offload certain tasks to hardware in system to accelerate that task

- OCI offers highest value and performance for bare metal and VM compute instances powered by NVIDIA GPUs

**NVIDIA GPU Models:**

- Available GPU types: A100, A10, V100, P100

- VM GPU options: NVIDIA Ampere, Volta, and Pascal GPUs

**Use Cases:**

- Mainstream graphics and video processing

- Most demanding AI training workloads

- High performance compute (HPC) workloads

**GPU Shapes Specifications:**

- GPU shapes include Intel or AMD CPUs combined with NVIDIA graphics processors

- Some bare metal GPU shapes support cluster networking

**Cluster Networking Capabilities:**

- Ultra-low latency cluster networking based on RDMA (Remote Direct Memory Access)

- Provides microsecond-level latency

- Can host more than 500 GPUs in a cluster without compromising performance

**Instance Options:**

- Can choose from variety of virtual machine and bare metal compute instances with GPUs

- Both VM GPU series and bare metal GPU series available

================================================================================

**Bare Metal, Virtual Machines, and Dedicated Hosts:**

**Bare Metal Instances:**

- Direct access to underlying hardware

- Provides dedicated physical server for highest performance and strong isolation

**Bare Metal Use Cases:**

- Workloads that are not virtualized

- Performance-intensive workloads

- Workloads requiring bring your own license (BYOL)

- Workloads requiring specific hypervisor

**Virtual Machine Instances:**

- Runs on top of bare metal hardware

- Hypervisor sits on top of bare metal server to virtualize it into smaller VMs

- Ideal for applications that do not require performance and resources of entire physical machine

**Dedicated Hosts:**

- Combination of bare metal and virtual machines

- Multiple VMs running on bare metal, with whole server dedicated to single customer

- Meets compliance and regulatory requirements for isolation

- Prevents use of shared infrastructure when required by compliance/regulations

================================================================================

**Preemptible Instances:**

**Overview:**

- Same as on-demand instances with one exception: OCI can terminate them if compute capacity is needed elsewhere

- Run on unused on-demand capacity which enables OCI to offer 50% discount compared to on-demand instances

- Designed for short-term usage - not suitable for long-running workloads

- Can be terminated at any time

**Pricing:**

- Always 50% cheaper than on-demand rates

- Simple and consistent pricing model

**Limitations and Restrictions:**

- When editing preemptible instance, only instance name can be changed

- Cannot change shape after instance is launched

- Cannot convert preemptible instance to on-demand instance

- Does not support bare-metal instances

- Cannot be used to create instance configurations

- Cannot be used in instance pools

**Capacity Reclamation:**

- OCI can reclaim capacity when requests for on-demand instances increase

- OCI sends termination notice through event service before terminating preemptible instance

- Instance preemption action event sent 30 seconds before instance termination begins

**Use Cases:**

- Batch jobs (e.g., video encoding)

- Short-term, interruptible workloads

- Cost-sensitive workloads that can tolerate interruptions

**Creating Preemptible Instances:**

- Uses same management experience as on-demand instances

- In console: select "preemptible capacity" instead of "on-demand capacity" in placement section under capacity type

================================================================================

**Capacity Reservations:**

**Overview:**

- Allows you to reserve compute capacity in advance and use this capacity when creating instances against the reservation

- When instances using reserved capacity are terminated, capacity is returned to the reservation

- Provides assurance that you have capacity necessary to manage your workload

- Can create reservation with as little or as much capacity as needed - no size or time constraints

- Can delete reservation at any time to stop paying for it

**Pricing:**

- Reserved capacity incurs charges even when capacity is unused (reservations consume resources)

- Unused reserved capacity billed at 85%

- Instances created against capacity reservation billed at 100%

**Configuration:**

- Specify availability domain where you want to reserve capacity when creating reservation

- Specify capacity configuration (e.g., VM.Standard.E3.Flex)

- Can view used capacity vs. total reserved capacity

**Use Cases:**

- **Large Capacity Requirements:** Ensures capacity available for migrations or new project launches

- **Disaster Recovery:** Ensures capacity available when you need to failover to secondary location

- **Seasonal Usage:** Provides needed capacity when maintenance or seasonal adjustments cause usage to vary

- **Workload Spikes:** Enables reserving capacity as buffer for unexpected workload spikes

**Support and Limitations:**

- Must specify availability domain when creating reservation - cannot be shared between availability domains

- Cannot move capacity reservation from one AD to another

- Cannot move capacity reservation from one tenancy to another

- Cannot be used with dedicated virtual machine host feature

- Capacity allocated when reservation is created - if insufficient capacity available, reservation created with as many instances as possible (e.g., request 20 instances but only 10 available = reservation with 10 instances created)

- Instance shape limited to shape defined in capacity configuration

================================================================================

**Dedicated Virtual Machine Host:**

**Overview:**

- Gives ability to run compute VM instances on dedicated server that is single tenant and not shared with other customers

- Billed for dedicated VM host as soon as you create it

- NOT billed for individual VM instances placed on the host

**Host Configuration:**

- Select shape for host when creating - shape determines how much capacity is available and what type of instances can be launched on host

- Some OCPUs reserved for VM management - difference between billed OCPUs and available OCPUs

**Use Cases:**

- Meet compliance and regulatory isolation requirements (not using shared infrastructure)

- Meet host-based licensing requirements that require licensing entire server

**Dedicated VM Host Shapes:**

- Usable OCPUs and usable memory depend on selected shape

- Example: DVH.Standard3.64 provides 60 usable OCPUs and 960 GB usable memory

- Each shape supports specific VM series - Example: DVH.Standard2.52 supports VM.Standard2 series

**Limitations:**

- Most compute features for VM instances are supported on dedicated VM hosts, but following features are NOT supported:

- Autoscaling

- Capacity reservations

- Instance configurations

- Instance pools

- Burstable instances

- Remote migration

================================================================================

**Burstable Instances:**

**Overview:**

- Virtual machine instance that provides baseline level of CPU performance with ability to burst to higher level to support occasional spikes in usage

- Designed for scenarios where instance is typically idle or has low CPU utilization with occasional spikes

- Cost less than regular instances with same total OCPU count

- Charged according to baseline OCPU (not burst OCPU)

**Configuration:**

- When creating burstable instance, specify total OCPU count and baseline CPU utilization

- Baseline utilization options: 12.5% or 50% of each CPU core

- Baseline provides minimum CPUs that can be used constantly

**Bursting Behavior:**

- No guarantee that instance will be able to burst if physical VM host is oversubscribed

- System allows bursting only if average CPU utilization for instance is below baseline over last 24 hours

- **Important:** Memory doesn't burst - only CPU bursts

**Supported Shapes:**

- Not all shapes support burstable instances

- Supported shapes: VM.Standard3.Flex, VM.Standard.E3.Flex, VM.Standard.E4.Flex

**Use Cases:**

- Workloads running at fraction of CPUs most of the time, then burst up to full CPUs for limited time

- Microservices

- Dev and test environments

- CI/CD tools

- Static websites

- Monitoring systems

**Creating Burstable Instances:**

- Process similar to creating on-demand instance

- Change shape to one of supported shapes

- Specify number of OCPUs (maximum OCPU)

- Select burstable option

- Set baseline (12.5% or 50%)

**Pricing:**

- Billed according to baseline OCPU

- Even if instance bursts above baseline, you still pay only for baseline OCPU

================================================================================

**Compute Vertical Scaling:**

**Overview:**

- Vertical scaling means adding additional resources to instance to meet demand

- Also referred to as scale up and scale down

- Can change shape of VM instance without rebuilding instances or redeploying applications

- **Scale Up:** Increase performance by adding more resources

- **Scale Down:** Save costs by reducing resources

**Shape Selection:**

- Shape series and image of original shape determine which shapes can be selected as target for new shape

- Option available in console to edit shape of instance

**What Remains Unchanged During Vertical Scaling:**

- IP addresses (both public and private) remain unchanged

- Volume attachments not affected

- VNIC attachments not affected

**What Changes During Vertical Scaling:**

- Number of OCPUs changes

- Memory amount changes

- Network bandwidth changes

- Maximum number of VNICs changes

**Important Considerations:**

- If instance is running when shape is changed, it is rebooted as part of change shape operation

- Scaling a virtual machine will cause a reboot

**Automation:**

- Can use Monitoring service to monitor via metrics and raise alarm when particular metric is met

- Alarm notification can call a function to adjust shape of VM as needed

- Enables automated vertical scaling based on metrics

================================================================================

**Compute Autoscaling:**

**Instance Configuration:**

- Template that defines settings to use when creating compute instances

- Can be used to create one or more instances in instance pool or create individual instances

- Does NOT include contents of any block volumes attached to the instance

- Can launch instances and create instance pools from instance configuration

- To modify existing instance configuration, must create new instance configuration with desired settings

**Instance Configuration Creation:**

- Can use existing compute instance as template

- Can provide list of configuration settings

- Contains: Operating system image, Metadata, Shape, VNICs, Storage, and Subnet information

**Instance Pool:**

- Used to create and manage multiple compute instances within same region as a group

- Can use same instance configuration for multiple instance pools

- Each instance pool can have only one instance configuration associated with it

**Instance Pool Management:**

- After creation, can update size of pool

- Can add and remove existing instances from pool

- Can attach or detach load balancers

- Can adjust number of instances running using metrics or schedule

- Instances can be deployed in different availability domains and placed behind load balancer

**Two Types of Autoscaling Policies:**

**1. Metric-Based Autoscaling:**

- Autoscaling action triggered when performance metric meets or exceeds threshold

- Benefits: Provides consistent performance for end users during high demand periods; Reduces costs during low demand periods

- Example: Add/remove instances when CPU or memory utilization exceeds threshold

**2. Schedule-Based Autoscaling:**

- Autoscaling event that takes place at specific times that you schedule

- Useful for predictable workload patterns

**Steps to Configure Autoscaling:**

- Step 1: Create instance configuration

- Step 2: Create instance pool from instance configuration

- Step 3: Configure instance pool (update size, add/remove instances, attach/detach load balancer)

- Step 4: Define policy or rule using autoscaling configuration

**Autoscaling Example:**

- Initial pool size: 2 instances

- Scaling rule: Add 2 instances if CPU or memory utilization > 70%; Remove 2 instances if < 70%

- Minimum size: 1 instance

- Maximum size: 4 instances

- When scale-out event happens, pool scales from 2 to 4 instances

================================================================================

**Oracle Cloud Agent:**

**Overview:**

- Lightweight process that manages plugins running on compute instances

- Installed by default on current platform images

- Plugins are installed as part of Oracle Cloud Agent

**Plugin Functions:**

- Collect performance metrics

- Install operating system updates

- Perform other instance management tasks

**Requirements to Use Plugins:**

- Oracle Cloud Agent software must be installed on instance

- Plugins must be enabled

- Plugins must be running

**Plugin Examples:**

**1. Bastion Plugin:**

- Required to use OCI Bastion service

- OCI Bastion provides restricted and time-limited secure access to resources that don't have public endpoint

- Must be enabled and running on instance to use Bastion service

**2. OSMS Agent (Operating System Management Service):**

- Required for OS Management Service to manage patches and updates for operating system environment

- Must be enabled and running on instance

**3. Compute Instance Run Command:**

- Facilitates running scripts within instance using run command feature

**Plugin Management:**

- Can enable or disable individual plugins

- Multiple plugins available beyond Bastion and OSMS examples

**Installation:**

- If instance created using current platform image: Oracle Cloud Agent installed by default

- Can manually install Oracle Cloud Agent software on instance using another supported image

================================================================================

**OS Management Hub Service:**

**Overview:**

- Fully managed service designed to simplify OS and patch management across organization

- Management as a service solution that centralizes management across entire environment

- Helps streamline day-to-day tasks and maintain high standards for security and compliance

**Common Challenges for IT Administrators:**

**Small Scale (Few Instances):**

- Software management and patching challenging due to different update cycles

- Oracle OS critical patch updates released quarterly

- Linux security patches released frequently as vulnerabilities discovered (unpredictable timing)

- Requires constant attention to stay on top of updates

- Reporting, security, and compliance: Must ensure SLAs are met, generate reports, maintain regulatory standards

- Monitoring and assessment: Need tools to monitor performance, identify issues, and resolve them

**Large Scale (Hundreds of Instances):**

- Manually managing patches and ensuring compliance becomes time-consuming, error-prone, and drains resources

- Team spends time on repetitive tasks instead of important projects

- Missing single patch or vulnerability can expose entire system to attack

**Multi-Environment Complexity:**

- Growing number of systems

- Diverse environments: On-premises setups, multiple cloud providers, different operating systems

- Maintaining security compliance across all systems

- Lifecycle management: Ensuring every system is up-to-date and running smoothly

- Centralized management solution becomes essential for unified view and automation

**OS Management Hub Key Features:**

**1. Centralized Management:**

- Gain complete control over updates throughout entire environment

- Reduces administrative overhead and improves overall efficiency

- Single unified view for managing systems across OCI, third-party clouds, and on-premises

**2. Enhanced Security and Compliance:**

- Clear, consolidated dashboard to monitor compliance

- Track patch statuses and review reports

- Stay proactive and aligned with security standards

**3. Zero Downtime Updates:**

- Ksplice integration allows applying critical Oracle Linux updates without system reboot

- Minimizes downtime and disruption

**4. Multi-Environment Support:**

- Manages infrastructure across on-premises data centers, OCI, and third-party clouds (Microsoft Azure, AWS)

- Provides tools and resources for Oracle Linux management

- Streamlines operations and makes management simpler and more efficient

================================================================================

**OS Management Hub Workflow:**

**Instance Grouping:**

- Group instances together that share same OS type, release, and architecture

- Grouping simplifies management tasks, applying updates, and maintaining consistency

- OS Management Hub provides centralized, unified management across all grouped systems

- Automates tasks: patching, monitoring compliance, ensuring systems stay secure and up-to-date

**Management Agent Deployment:**

- Management agent deployed on each managed instance

- Agent gathers important data: patch status, compliance information

- Sends data back to OS Management Hub

- Enables easy automation of updates and patch management across all systems while maintaining consistency

**Workflow Diagram:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        OS Management Hub (OCI)                          │
│                    - Centralized Console                                │
│                    - Monitoring & Compliance                            │
│                    - Patch Management                                   │
└──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┬───────────────────┘
                           │                          │
                           │                          │
        ┌──────────────────▼───────┐    ┌────────────▼─────────────┐
        │   OCI Environment        │    │  On-Premises / 3rd Party │
        │                          │    │      Cloud Environment    │
        │  ┌──────────────────┐    │    │                          │
        │  │  Instance Group  │    │    │   ┌──────────────────┐   │
        │  │                  │    │    │   │ Management       │   │
        │  │  ┌────────────┐  │    │    │   │ Station          │   │
        │  │  │ Instance 1 │  │    │    │   │ (Bridge)         │   │
        │  │  │ [Agent]    │──┼────┼────┼───│ [Agent]          │   │
        │  │  └────────────┘  │    │    │   │ - Mirrors Repo   │   │
        │  │                  │    │    │   │ - Local Updates  │   │
        │  │  ┌────────────┐  │    │    │   └─────────┬────────┘   │
        │  │  │ Instance 2 │  │    │    │             │            │
        │  │  │ [Agent]    │──┼────┤    │   ┌─────────▼────────┐   │
        │  │  └────────────┘  │    │    │   │  Instance Group  │   │
        │  │                  │    │    │   │                  │   │
        │  │  ┌────────────┐  │    │    │   │  ┌────────────┐ │   │
        │  │  │ Instance 3 │  │    │    │   │  │ Instance A │ │   │
        │  │  │ [Agent]    │──┼────┘    │   │  │ [Agent]    │ │   │
        │  │  └────────────┘  │         │   │  └────────────┘ │   │
        │  └──────────────────┘         │   │                  │   │
        │                               │   │  ┌────────────┐ │   │
        └───────────────────────────────┘   │  │ Instance B │ │   │
                                            │  │ [Agent]    │ │   │
          Direct Connection                 │  └────────────┘ │   │
          to OS Management Hub              │  └──────────────┘   │
                                            └─────────────────────┘
                                            
                                            Connection via
                                            Management Station

Data Flow:
- Agents collect: Patch status, compliance data, system info
- Management Hub sends: Updates, patches, security advisories
- Management Station: Reduces bandwidth, local distribution
```

**Multi-Environment Architecture:**

**For OCI Instances:**

- Instances grouped and managed directly by OS Management Hub

- Management agents report directly to OS Management Hub service

**For On-Premises or Third-Party Cloud Instances:**

- Deploy one or more management stations as bridge between instances and OS Management Hub in OCI

- Management stations mirror software repository and locally distribute updates

- Reduces bandwidth usage for patching

- Only management stations need to communicate directly with OCI service

- Instances connect to management station to receive updates (no direct access to OCI/OSMH service needed)

- Management agents deployed on both managed instances and management station

- Agents report back to OS Management Hub via management station

**Console Monitoring and Reporting:**

- Monitor status of all instances from OS Management Hub console (on-premises, third-party cloud, or OCI)

- Clear view of available security and bug updates not yet applied across managed environment

- Status check to verify all managed instances properly communicating with service

- Real-time reports providing comprehensive view of security patch compliance status

- Detailed security advisory information to quickly identify and address vulnerabilities

- Can apply updates immediately or schedule for later time

- Real-time reports can be downloaded anytime for further review

**Four Key Benefits:**

**1. Enhanced Security:**

- Ensures systems stay up-to-date with latest security patches and bug fixes

- Minimizes vulnerabilities keeping OS environment secure and reliable

**2. Time Savings:**

- Automation for patching policies and scheduling updates

- Streamlines processes, especially valuable for large-scale deployments

**3. Cost Reduction:**

- Fully managed service reduces need for additional administrative resources

- Eliminates cost associated with third-party management tools

- Achieve more with fewer resources

**4. Simplified Management:**

- Centralized, simplified console to manage instances at scale

- Grouping for fleet management and automated updates

- Makes large-scale operations easier to handle

**Summary:**

- Simplifies OS management across diverse environments (on-premises, OCI, third-party clouds)

- Improves security, saves time, lowers costs, reduces management complexity

- Powerful automation with centralized console ensures systems stay secure and up-to-date

- Makes large-scale operations more efficient and manageable

================================================================================

**OS Management Hub Components and IAM Policies:**

**Key Components:**

**1. Management Station:**

- Central hub located in data center or third-party cloud environment

- Main role: Mirroring and distributing software

- Acts as bridge between managed instances and OS Management Hub service

**2. Profiles:**

- Blueprints for managing systems

- Define configurations instances need

- Makes management consistent and streamlined

**3. Software Sources:**

- Specify which repositories instances should access

- Allows control and standardization of software across environment

**4. Agents:**

- Software components installed on each instance to be managed with OSMH

- Enable communication between instances and the hub

**5. Network Configuration:**

- Ensures proper connectivity and firewall settings

- Management station can communicate with both OSMH and managed instances

**6. Ksplice:**

- Tool that applies security patches to Linux kernel without reboot

- Enhances system availability and security

**7. Reports:**

- Software update reports: Track update status across instances

- Security vulnerabilities: Identify and resolve potential risks

- System health: Visibility into performance and overall system status

**IAM Configuration:**

**User Groups:**

- Define which users/administrators can access and manage OS Management Hub components

- Ensures right people have appropriate permissions

- Create user group (e.g., "OS Management Hub Admins") for users who will manage OSMH

- Group has necessary permissions to administer service across tenancy

- Can create additional user groups with more restrictive policies for specific resources or functions

**Dynamic Groups:**

- Define specific resources that OS Management Hub will manage

- Includes OCI instances and non-OCI instances (on-premises or third-party clouds)

- Include managed instances based on rule statements defining compartments or subcompartments

- Group automatically grows or shrinks as instances added or retired within specified compartments

- For non-OCI instances: Each rule statement should include management agent resource in designated compartment

**Required IAM Policies:**

**Policy 1 - Dynamic Group (Managed Instance Access):**

- Allows agents on managed instances to interact with OS Management Hub service

- Provides agents the access they need

**Policy 2 - User Group (Manage All OSMH Resources):**

- Grants user group permission to manage all OS Management Hub resources across tenancy

**Policy 3 - User Group (Management Agent Access):**

- Allows users to manage management agents within tenancy

**Policy 4 - User Group (Agent Key Access):**

- Allows users to manage agent keys within tenancy

**Policy Scoping:**

- Can scope permissions at compartment level

- If scoped at compartment level: Must grant read access to profiles and software sources at tenancy level

- Read access at tenancy level needed to replicate software sources and use service-provided profiles

**Supported OS Platforms:**

**For OCI:**

- Oracle Linux

- Specific Windows Server versions (within OCI only)

**For On-Premises and Third-Party Clouds:**

- Oracle Linux versions 7, 8, and 9

================================================================================

**Configuring OS Management Hub for OCI Instances:**

**Prerequisites:**

- OSMH service enabled in tenancy

- Required IAM policies in place

- Dynamic groups configured

**Setup Workflow:**

```
Step 1: Verify Network Configuration
         ↓
Step 2: Set Up Software Sources and Profiles
         ↓
Step 3: Create and Register Compute Instances
```

**Step 1: Network Configuration:**

**For Private Subnet Instances:**

```
Option 1: Service Gateway
┌─────────────────────────┐
│  Private Subnet         │
│  ┌─────────────────┐    │       ┌──────────────────┐
│  │  OCI Instance   │────┼──────▶│ Service Gateway  │──▶ Oracle Services Network
│  └─────────────────┘    │       └──────────────────┘    (All region label)
└─────────────────────────┘

Option 2: NAT Gateway
┌─────────────────────────┐
│  Private Subnet         │
│  ┌─────────────────┐    │       ┌──────────────────┐
│  │  OCI Instance   │────┼──────▶│   NAT Gateway    │──▶ Internet (Outbound)
│  └─────────────────┘    │       └──────────────────┘
└─────────────────────────┘
```

**For Public Subnet Instances:**

```
┌─────────────────────────┐
│  Public Subnet          │
│  ┌─────────────────┐    │       ┌──────────────────┐
│  │  OCI Instance   │────┼──────▶│ Internet Gateway │──▶ Internet
│  └─────────────────┘    │       └──────────────────┘
└─────────────────────────┘
```

**For Windows Instances:**

- Set up security lists or network rules allowing access to Windows Update servers

- Open necessary ports and IP addresses in security lists or NSGs for Windows updates

**Step 2: Software Sources:**

**Overview:**

- First level of control over content available to managed instances

- Must add at least one vendor software source before registering instances

- Collection of packages and modules (similar to repositories)

- Once added, available across all compartments, regions, and management stations

- Administrators control which packages can be installed without manual repository setup

**Three Types of Software Sources:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    1. Vendor Software Source                        │
│                                                                     │
│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  All software from OS vendor (Foundation for all others)  │    │
│  │  Required: At least one before registering instances      │    │
│  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    2. Custom Software Source                        │
│                                                                     │
│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  Vendor Source + Filters (Include/Exclude packages)       │    │
│  │  Flexibility: Tailor repositories to specific needs       │    │
│  │  Control exactly what's available to instances            │    │
│  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                              ↓
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 3. Versioned Custom Software Source                 │
│                                                                     │
│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  Custom Source + Version Number (Immutable)               │    │
│  │  Cannot modify contents once created                      │    │
│  │  Perfect for lifecycle environments (Dev/Test/Prod)       │    │
│  │  Ensures consistency across stages                        │    │
│  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Step 3: Configuring Profiles:**

**Profile Purpose:**

- Templates that simplify and standardize instance registration process

- Automatically apply same settings to multiple instances at once

- Only impact instances during registration process (not after)

- Location-specific: Profile created for OCI can be used on on-premises instances

**Profile Components:**

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                        Profile                               │
│                                                              │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  1. Software Sources                                │    │
│  │     - Locations for software updates and packages   │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                              │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  2. Lifecycle Stage Membership                      │    │
│  │     - Development / Testing / Production            │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                              │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  3. Group Membership                                │    │
│  │     - Assign instances to groups for easier mgmt    │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Instance Registration Workflow:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    Instance Registration Process                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Step 1: Create Instance
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Create instance with required config │
│  • Configure network with:              │
│    - Service Gateway (private subnet)   │
│    - Internet Gateway (public subnet)   │
│    - NAT Gateway (outbound access)      │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 2: Enable OS Management Hub Agent
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Access Oracle Cloud Agent            │
│  • Activate OSMH agent plugin           │
│  • Plugin enables communication between │
│    instance and OSMH service            │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 3: Select Profile
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • During plugin activation, select     │
│    profile for instance                 │
│  • Profile automatically applies:       │
│    - Software sources                   │
│    - Lifecycle stage membership         │
│    - Group membership                   │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Result: Instance Ready for Management
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Instance appears as managed instance │
│    in OSMH service                      │
│  • Ready for centralized management     │
│  • Within few minutes after activation  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Verifying Agent Version:**

- Run command to verify Oracle Cloud Agent version

- Install or update to latest version for compatibility

- Useful for registering existing OCI instances with OSMH service

- After confirming version, enable OSMH agent plugin

- Instance appears in Managed Instances list within few minutes

**Key Points:**

- Profiles only affect instances during registration (not after registration)

- Must have at least one vendor software source before registering instances

- Network configuration crucial for proper communication with OSMH service

- Windows instances require additional security list/NSG configuration for Windows Update servers

================================================================================

**Run Command:**

**Overview:**

- Remotely configure, manage, and troubleshoot compute instances by running scripts within the instance

- Can run commands on instance even without SSH access or open inbound ports

- Enables remote execution without traditional connectivity requirements

**Supported Platform Images:**

- Oracle Autonomous Linux

- Oracle Linux

- CentOS

- Windows Server

- **NOT supported:** Ubuntu

**Script Execution:**

- **Linux instances:** Script runs in bash shell by default

- **Windows instances:** Script runs as batch file

**Script Size Limitations:**

- **Maximum size for direct upload (plain text):** 4 KB

- **For larger files:** Save file in object storage location and reference it

**Output Limitations:**

- **Plain text output:** Limited to last 1 KB

- **For larger responses:** Save output to object storage location

**Use Cases:**

- Automate tasks such as configuring secondary virtual network interface cards (vNICs)

- Troubleshoot SSH connectivity issues

- Remote instance configuration and management without direct access

================================================================================

**Instance Console Connection:**

**Overview:**

- Provides display of what is happening during boot process

- Used for remotely troubleshooting malfunctioning instances

- Special case for troubleshooting, not for normal operations

**Two Connection Options:**

**1. Cloud Shell Connection:**

- Cloud Shell integration automatically creates instance console connection

- Automatically creates temporary SSH key

- Simplifies connection process

**2. Local Connection:**

- Generate SSH key pair, or

- Upload your own public key

- Requires manual SSH key management

**Two Types of Console Connections:**

**1. Serial Console Connection:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│             Serial Console Connection                       │
│                                                             │
│  Primary Use: Linux servers and CLI-based operating systems │
│                                                             │
│  Connection Method:                                         │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
│  │  Create console connection → Connect via SSH        │   │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
│                                                             │
│  ✓ Supports Cloud Shell                                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- Mainly used for Linux servers and CLI-based operating systems

- After creating console connection, connect via Secure Shell (SSH)

- Can use Cloud Shell for connection

**2. VNC Console Connection:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              VNC Console Connection                         │
│                                                             │
│  Primary Use: Servers with graphical user interface (GUI)  │
│                                                             │
│  Connection Method:                                         │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
│  │  Set up secure tunnel to VNC server on instance    │   │
│  │               ↓                                     │   │
│  │  Connect with VNC client                           │   │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
│                                                             │
│  ✗ Cannot use Cloud Shell (local connection only)         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

- Commonly used for servers with graphical user interface

- Set up secure tunnel to VNC server on instance

- Connect with VNC client

- **Important:** Cannot use Cloud Shell for VNC console connections (only for serial console)

**Use Cases:**

- **Edit System Configuration Files:** Make configuration changes when normal access unavailable

- **Add or Reset SSH Keys:** Manage SSH keys for OPC user when locked out

- **Troubleshoot Boot Issues:** Debug imported/customized images not booting successfully

- **Troubleshoot Unresponsive Instances:** Diagnose instances that were working fine but suddenly stopped responding

**When to Use Instance Console Connection:**

- Instance not booting successfully (imported/custom images)

- Instance stopped responding and normal access methods fail

- Need to access instance for troubleshooting without SSH access

- Need to see boot process diagnostics

================================================================================

**Infrastructure Maintenance:**

**Overview:**

- Routine maintenance performed on physical infrastructure for virtual machine instances

- OCI offers different options to relocate VMs during maintenance to minimize inconvenience

**Three Migration Options:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Infrastructure Maintenance Options                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

                   Infrastructure Maintenance Needed
                              ↓
                   ┌──────────┴──────────┐
                   │                     │
         ┌─────────▼──────────┐    ┌────▼──────────────────┐
         │ Supports Live      │    │ Does NOT Support      │
         │ Migration?         │    │ Live Migration        │
         └─────────┬──────────┘    └────┬──────────────────┘
                   │                     │
         ┌─────────▼──────────┐    ┌────▼──────────────────┐
         │   Live Migration   │    │  Reboot Migration     │
         │                    │    │  (14-16 days notice)  │
         │  - Minimal         │    │                       │
         │    disruption      │    │  - Short downtime     │
         │  - No downtime     │    │  - Proactive control  │
         │  - Linux OS only   │    │    possible           │
         │  - Specific shapes │    └────┬──────────────────┘
         └────────────────────┘         │
                                        │ (If auto-migrate
                                        │  not possible)
                                        │
                                  ┌─────▼─────────────────┐
                                  │  Manual Migration     │
                                  │                       │
                                  │  - Terminate instance │
                                  │  - Recreate instance  │
                                  │  - Preserve boot vol  │
                                  └───────────────────────┘
```

**1. Live Migration:**

- OCI live migrates supported VM instances from physical host needing maintenance to healthy host

- Minimal disruption to running instances

- Infrastructure maintenance event emitted when live migration begins and ends

- **Supported:** Linux operating systems only

- **Restrictions:** Limited to specific shapes

- **Downtime:** None (seamless migration)

**2. Reboot Migration:**

- Used when VM instance does not support live migration

- OCI schedules due date for reboot migration within 14-16 days

- OCI sends notification about upcoming maintenance

**Reboot Migration Behavior:**

- If you do not proactively reboot instance before due date: Instance is stopped → Migrated to healthy host → Restarted

- Short downtime occurs during migration

- You can control when downtime occurs by proactively reboot migrating instance before maintenance due date

**Important Distinction:**

- **Rebooting operating system ≠ Rebooting instance from Console/CLI/API**

- Operating system reboot does NOT trigger the migration

- Must reboot instance from OCI Console, CLI, or API to trigger migration

**3. Manual Migration:**

- Required for instances without date in reboot maintenance field

- Instance must be terminated and recreated

**Manual Migration Steps:**

- Terminate instance

- **Critical:** Preserve boot volume before termination

- Recreate instance from preserved boot volume

**Automatic Instance Recovery:**

**For Standard Virtual Machine Instances:**

- When underlying infrastructure fails, OCI automatically attempts to recover instance

- Recovery performed using reboot migration

- Automatically restores VM on healthy host

**For Dense I/O Virtual Machines:**

- Recovered by rebooting instance on same physical host

- If recovery on same physical host not possible:

  - OCI notifies you to delete or terminate instance within 14 days
  
  - If instance not deleted before deadline: OCI disables instance on deadline
  
  - OCI deletes disabled instance within next 7 days
  
  - **Important:** Boot volume and remote-attached data volumes are preserved

**Key Points:**

- Live migration has no downtime but limited to Linux and specific shapes

- Reboot migration gives 14-16 days notice with short downtime window

- Manual migration requires instance termination and recreation

- Always preserve boot volumes during manual migration

- Dense I/O VMs have different recovery process than standard VMs

================================================================================

**Shielded Instances:**

**Overview:**

- Protect virtual machines and bare metal instances against low-level threats

- Target threats: Rootkits and Bootkits with kernel-level privileges that can infect firmware and operating system

- These threats are very difficult to detect using traditional security measures

- Shielded instances prevent unauthorized code from running during boot process

**Enabling Shielded Instances:**

- Follow standard instance deployment flow

- Images and shapes that support shielded instances have **shield icon** next to them

- When choosing supported image and shape, enable features in advanced options:

  - Enable Secure Boot
  
  - Enable Measured Boot (for VMs)

- Enabling Measured Boot for virtual machines automatically enables Trusted Platform Module

**Three Security Components:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Shielded Instance Security Components                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

                        Boot Process Security
                                 │
                ┌────────────────┼────────────────┐
                │                │                │
        ┌───────▼────────┐  ┌───▼──────────┐  ┌─▼──────────────┐
        │  Secure Boot   │  │ Measured Boot│  │ Trusted Platform│
        │                │  │              │  │  Module (TPM)   │
        │ - Prevents     │  │ - Stores     │  │                │
        │   unauthorized │  │   measurements│  │ - Security chip│
        │   bootloaders  │  │ - Verifies   │  │ - Stores boot  │
        │ - Blocks non-  │  │   boot hasn't│  │   measurements │
        │   signed boot  │  │   changed    │  │ - Contains PCRs│
        │   components   │  │ - VM only    │  │                │
        └────────────────┘  └──────┬───────┘  └────────┬───────┘
                                   │                   │
                                   │   Measurements    │
                                   │     stored in     │
                                   └──────────►────────┘
                                        
        Available on:          Available on:       Available on:
        - All bare metal       - VM instances      - All bare metal
        - All VM instances       only              - All VM instances
                                                    (auto-enabled with
                                                     Measured Boot)
```

**Component Details:**

**1. Secure Boot:**

- Unified Extensible Firmware Interface (UEFI) feature

- Prevents unauthorized bootloaders and operating systems from booting

- Boot components that are not properly signed are not allowed to run

- System prevented from booting if unauthorized components detected

- **Availability:** All supported bare metal and virtual machine instances

**2. Measured Boot:**

- Complements and enhances Secure Boot

- Stores measurements of boot components: bootloaders, drivers, and operating systems

- Verifies that measurements don't change from one boot to the next

- Detects tampering by comparing current boot measurements with previous boots

- **Availability:** VM instances only (not available on bare metal)

**3. Trusted Platform Module (TPM):**

- Specialized security chip used by Measured Boot

- Stores boot measurements securely
