- Virtual TPM automatically enabled when Measured Boot is enabled for VMs

- Contains Platform Configuration Registers (PCRs)

- **Availability:** All supported bare metal and virtual machine instances

**Platform Configuration Registers (PCRs):**

- Memory location inside TPM

- Holds value that summarizes all measurement results

- Measurements taken by Measured Boot are stored in PCRs

- Provides secure storage for boot integrity verification

**Availability Matrix:**

```
┌──────────────────────┬─────────────┬──────────────┐
│     Component        │ Bare Metal  │  VM Instance │
├──────────────────────┼─────────────┼──────────────┤
│  Secure Boot         │      ✓      │      ✓       │
│  Measured Boot       │      ✗      │      ✓       │
│  Trusted Platform    │      ✓      │      ✓       │
│  Module (TPM)        │             │  (auto with  │
│                      │             │ Measured Boot)│
└──────────────────────┴─────────────┴──────────────┘
```

**Pricing:**

- All shielded instance services provided for OCI platform at **no charge**

- No additional cost for Secure Boot, Measured Boot, or TPM features

**Key Benefits:**

- Protection against firmware-level attacks (Rootkits, Bootkits)

- Ensures only authorized, properly signed boot components can run

- Detects tampering or changes in boot process

- Secure storage of boot measurements

- Defense-in-depth security approach with multiple layers of protection

================================================================================

**Confidential Computing:**

**Overview:**

- Secures data while loaded in memory (not just at rest or in transit)

- Prior to confidential computing, all data running in memory was accessible as clear text

- Range of processor families now enable data to be encrypted while running in memory

- Takes encryption to next level by protecting data in use

**Key Security Features:**

- Minimizes list of trusted parties (operating system, ecosystem partners, administrators)

- Reduces risk of data exposure

- Protects data in use at hardware level

- Both data and application processing the data are encrypted and isolated while application processes data

- Prevents unauthorized access or modification of either data or application

**Technology Foundation:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Confidential Computing Architecture                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

        AMD EPYC Processors (2nd & 3rd Generation)
                         │
        ┌────────────────┴────────────────┐
        │                                 │
┌───────▼─────────────┐        ┌──────────▼──────────────┐
│  For Virtual        │        │  For Bare Metal         │
│  Machines           │        │  Servers                │
│                     │        │                         │
│  AMD Infinity Guard │        │  AMD Infinity Guard     │
│  Features:          │        │  Features:              │
│                     │        │                         │
│  • SEV (Secure      │        │  • SME (Secure Memory   │
│    Encrypted        │        │    Encryption)          │
│    Virtualization)  │        │                         │
│                     │        │  Encrypts all system    │
│  Encrypts VM memory │        │  memory                 │
│  with per-VM key    │        │                         │
└─────────────────────┘        └─────────────────────────┘

        Available in E3 and E4 Shapes
```

**Powered by AMD Technology:**

- AMD EPYC processors enable confidential computing

- **For Confidential VMs:** AMD Infinity Guard with Secure Encrypted Virtualization (SEV)

- **For Confidential Bare Metal:** Secure Memory Encryption (SME)

- Features leverage security components in 2nd and 3rd generation AMD EPYC processors

- Available in all OCI E3 and E4 shapes

**Supported Compute Shapes:**

**Virtual Machine Shapes:**

- VM.Standard.E4.Flex

- VM.Standard.E3.Flex

- **Supported on:** Oracle Linux 7.x or 8.x platform images

**Bare Metal Shapes:**

- BM.DenseIO.E4.128

- BM.Standard.E4.128

- BM.Standard.E3.128

- **Supported on:** Any platform image

**Key Benefits:**

**1. Improved Isolation Using Real-Time Encryption:**

- Data and applications encrypted using per-VM encryption key

- Encryption key generated during VM creation

- Key resides solely in AMD secure processor (part of CPU)

- Key not accessible from any application, VM, instance, hypervisor, or Oracle Cloud Infrastructure

**2. No Application Changes Required:**

- Enabling confidential VMs does not require any changes to applications

- Seamless enablement without code modifications

- Works with existing applications as-is

**3. Minimal Performance Impact:**

- Applications experience little to no performance impact with confidential computing enabled

- Hardware-level encryption ensures high performance

- Real-time encryption without significant overhead

**Security Architecture:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 Encryption Key Security                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

                  Per-VM Encryption Key
                          │
                   Generated at
                   VM Creation
                          │
                          ▼
              ┌───────────────────────┐
              │  AMD Secure Processor │
              │      (Part of CPU)    │
              └───────────────────────┘
                          │
              Key Isolated From:
              • Applications
              • Virtual Machine
              • Hypervisor
              • OCI Platform
              • Administrators
                          
         Hardware-Level Protection
```

**Summary:**

- Confidential computing protects data in memory during processing

- Uses AMD EPYC processor security features (SEV for VMs, SME for bare metal)

- Available in E3 and E4 shapes

- No application changes required

- Minimal performance impact

- Encryption keys isolated in AMD secure processor

- Reduces trusted parties and minimizes data exposure risk

**Object Storage Overview:**

**OCI Storage Services Summary:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              OCI Storage Services for Unstructured Data             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────┐
│   Object Storage         │        │   Archive Storage        │
│                          │        │                          │
│  • Unlimited capacity    │        │  • Unlimited capacity    │
│  • High durability       │        │  • High durability       │
│  • Objects up to 10 TiB  │        │  • Objects up to 10 TiB  │
│  • Unstructured data     │        │  • Long-term archival    │
│  • Frequent access       │        │  • Infrequent access     │
│  • Multiple tiers        │        │  • Lowest cost           │
└──────────────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────┘

Note: 1 TiB = approximately 1.1 TB
```

**Object Storage Key Characteristics:**

**Performance & Scalability:**

- Highly durable, scalable, secure, cost-efficient storage platform

- Internet-scale and high-performance

- Designed for 99.9% availability

- Highly reliable service

**Capacity:**

- Store unlimited amount of data

- Create thousands of buckets per account

- Each bucket can host unlimited number of objects

- Objects can be as small as 0 bytes or as large as 10 TiB

**Data Type:**

- Designed for unstructured data

- Examples: Images, videos, analytic data, logs, documents

**Regional Service:**

- Data stored in specific OCI region

- Can access data from anywhere using object storage endpoints

- Global accessibility with regional data residency

**Access Methods:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Storage Access Patterns                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────┐
│  Public Access          │        │  Private Access          │
│                         │        │                          │
│  Internet               │        │  VCN (Private Subnet)    │
│      │                  │        │      │                   │
│      ▼                  │        │      ▼                   │
│  Public Endpoints       │        │  Service Gateway         │
│      │                  │        │      │                   │
│      ▼                  │        │      ▼                   │
│  Object Storage         │        │  Object Storage          │
│                         │        │                          │
│  • Accessible from      │        │  • Private IP addresses  │
│    anywhere             │        │  • No internet exposure  │
│  • Internet connectivity│        │  • Secure VCN routing    │
└─────────────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────┘
```

**Private Access via Service Gateway:**

- Supports private access from OCI resources in VCN through Service Gateway

- Connect to object storage public endpoints using private IP addresses in private subnet

- No internet gateway required for object storage access from VCN

- Traffic stays within Oracle network

**Storage Tiers:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Three Object Storage Tiers                                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────┐
│   Standard Tier          │
│                          │
│  • Hot data              │
│  • Frequent access       │
│  • Highest cost          │
│  • Instant retrieval     │
└────────────┬─────────────┘
             │
             ▼
┌──────────────────────────┐
│   Infrequent Access Tier │
│                          │
│  • Warm data             │
│  • Less frequent access  │
│  • Lower cost            │
│  • Instant retrieval     │
└────────────┬─────────────┘
             │
             ▼
┌──────────────────────────┐
│   Archive Tier           │
│                          │
│  • Cold data             │
│  • Rare access           │
│  • Lowest cost           │
│  • Restore required      │
└──────────────────────────┘

Cost and Performance Flexibility
```

**Object Storage Features:**

- Cost flexibility through multiple storage tiers

- Performance optimization based on access patterns

- Can move data between tiers based on usage

- Automatic lifecycle management policies available

**Use Cases:**

**1. Content Repository:**

- Highly available and durable content repository

- Store data, images, logs, and videos

- Unlimited scalability for growing content

**2. Application Log Data:**

- Store application log data for analysis

- Troubleshooting purposes

- Long-term log retention

**3. Unstructured and Semi-Structured Data:**

- Optimized for data without rigid schema

- Examples: Documents, media files, sensor data, social media content

**4. Big Data Primary Repository:**

- Use as primary data repository for big data workloads

- Store raw data, processed data, and analytics results

- Integration with big data processing tools

**5. Data Preservation:**

- Preserve data for longer periods of time

- Compliance and regulatory requirements

- Historical data retention

**Summary:**

- Unlimited capacity with high durability (99.9% availability)

- Objects from 0 bytes to 10 TiB

- Regional service with global accessibility

- Public and private (Service Gateway) access options

- Three storage tiers for cost optimization

- Ideal for unstructured data, big data, logs, and long-term archival

================================================================================

**Object Storage Resources:**

**Three Core Constructs:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Object Storage Resource Hierarchy                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

                    ┌──────────────────────┐
                    │     Namespace        │
                    │  (Top-level container│
                    │   Unique per tenant) │
                    └──────────┬───────────┘
                               │
                               │ Contains
                               │
                    ┌──────────▼───────────┐
                    │      Buckets         │
                    │  (Logical containers)│
                    │  Unique per region   │
                    └──────────┬───────────┘
                               │
                               │ Contains
                               │
                    ┌──────────▼───────────┐
                    │      Objects         │
                    │  (Data + Metadata)   │
                    └──────────────────────┘

              Flat Structure (No Hierarchy)
       (But can simulate directories with prefixes)
```

**1. Namespace:**

- Top-level container for all buckets and objects

- Each OCI tenant assigned one unique object storage namespace at account creation time

- Namespace name is globally unique across all OCI tenants

- Cannot be changed or deleted

**2. Object:**

- Data stored in object storage along with its metadata

- Consists of: Data content + Metadata (key-value pairs)

- Metadata includes system-defined and user-defined attributes

**3. Bucket:**

- Logical container for storing objects

- Bucket name must be unique within a region within each tenancy

**Bucket Naming Rules:**

```
Example Scenario:

Region: Mumbai (India West)
Bucket Name: "demo-bucket"

✓ Can create: "demo-bucket" in Mumbai region
✗ Cannot create: Another "demo-bucket" in Mumbai region (duplicate)
✓ Can create: "demo-bucket" in Hyderabad region (different region)

Conclusion: Same bucket name can exist in different regions,
            but NOT in the same region within a tenancy
```

**Flat Structure:**

- Within object storage namespace, buckets and objects exist in **flat structure/flat hierarchy**

- No directory tree structure

- All objects at same level within bucket

**Object Storage Endpoint Structure:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Storage Endpoint Anatomy                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

https://objectstorage.{region}.oraclecloud.com/n/{namespace}/b/{bucket}/o/{object}

Example:
https://objectstorage.ap-mumbai-1.oraclecloud.com/n/ociarchassociate/b/training/o/oci.jpg

Breaking it down:
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Service:    objectstorage                                    │
│  Region:     ap-mumbai-1                                      │
│  /n/         Namespace indicator                              │
│  Namespace:  ociarchassociate                                 │
│  /b/         Bucket indicator                                 │
│  Bucket:     training                                         │
│  /o/         Object indicator                                 │
│  Object:     oci.jpg                                          │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Simulating Directory Structure with Prefixes:**

- Can simulate directory structure by adding prefix string to object name

- Prefix includes one or more forward slashes (/)

- Not actual directories, just naming convention

**Prefix Example:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Using Prefixes for Directory Simulation                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Actual Storage (Flat):
┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Bucket: training                    │
│  ├─ training/oci/oci.jpg             │
│  ├─ training/aws/aws.jpg             │
│  └─ training/azure/azure.jpg         │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘

Visual in Console (Hierarchical):
┌──────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Bucket: training                    │
│  └─ training/                        │
│      ├─ oci/                         │
│      │   └─ oci.jpg                  │
│      ├─ aws/                         │
│      │   └─ aws.jpg                  │
│      └─ azure/                       │
│          └─ azure.jpg                │
└──────────────────────────────────────┘

Components:
- Prefix: "training/oci"
- Object Name: "oci.jpg"
- Full Object Name: "training/oci/oci.jpg"
```

**Benefits of Using Prefixes:**

**1. Bulk Operations:**

- Perform bulk deletes of all objects with same prefix using CLI or API

- Perform bulk downloads of all objects with same prefix using CLI or API

- Example: Delete all objects starting with "training/oci/"

**2. Hierarchical Display:**

- Console displays objects in hierarchical view using prefixes

- Makes navigation easier for users

- Organizes objects logically like traditional file systems

**3. Organization and Management:**

- Logical grouping of related objects

- Easier to search and filter objects

- Better organization for large number of objects

**Key Points:**

- Namespace is unique per OCI tenant, assigned at account creation

- Buckets must have unique names per region within tenancy

- Objects exist in flat structure within buckets

- Can simulate directories using prefix strings with forward slashes

- Prefixes enable bulk operations and hierarchical console display

- Endpoint structure: /n/{namespace}/b/{bucket}/o/{object}

================================================================================

**Object Storage Characteristics:**

**Strong Consistency:**

- Object storage always serves the most recent copy of data that was written to the system

- Read requests always return the latest committed write

- No eventual consistency delays

**High Durability:**

- Designed for eleven 9s (99.999999999%) durability

- Ensures data is protected against loss and corruption

**Redundancy Architecture:**

```
Multi-Availability Domain Region:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    Object Stored                            │
│                         │                                   │
│         ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐                   │
│         │               │               │                   │
│    ┌────▼────┐    ┌─────▼────┐    ┌────▼────┐              │
│    │   AD 1  │    │   AD 2   │    │   AD 3  │              │
│    │  Copy 1 │    │  Copy 2  │    │  Copy 3 │              │
│    └─────────┘    └──────────┘    └─────────┘              │
│                                                             │
│   Data redundantly stored across 3 Availability Domains    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Single Availability Domain Region:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    Object Stored                            │
│                         │                                   │
│         ┌───────────────┼───────────────┐                   │
│         │               │               │                   │
│    ┌────▼────┐    ┌─────▼────┐    ┌────▼────┐              │
│    │   FD 1  │    │   FD 2   │    │   FD 3  │              │
│    │  Copy 1 │    │  Copy 2  │    │  Copy 3 │              │
│    └─────────┘    └──────────┘    └─────────┘              │
│      (Within single Availability Domain)                    │
│                                                             │
│    Data redundantly stored across 3 Fault Domains          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

FD = Fault Domain
AD = Availability Domain
```

**Data Integrity and Reliability:**

- Active monitoring using checksums to ensure data integrity

- Corrupt data automatically detected and repaired

- Any loss in data redundancy is detected automatically

- Remediation performed without customer intervention or impact

- Self-healing infrastructure ensures continuous data protection

**Security Features:**

- All stored data is encrypted by default

- Tightly integrated with OCI Identity and Access Management (IAM) service

- Data uploaded and downloaded over SSL endpoints using HTTPS protocol

- Secure transmission and storage at all times

**Capacity and Scalability:**

- Store unlimited amount of data in object storage

- Create thousands of buckets per account

- Each bucket can host unlimited number of objects

- Objects can be as small as 0 bytes or as large as 10 TiB (tebibytes)

**Key Benefits:**

- Strong consistency ensures latest data always available

- Eleven 9s durability protects against data loss

- Triple redundancy (3 ADs or 3 FDs) provides high availability

- Automatic corruption detection and repair without manual intervention

- Default encryption and IAM integration ensure security

- HTTPS/SSL protocol for secure data transfer

- Unlimited storage with flexible object sizing (0 bytes to 10 TiB)

================================================================================

**Multipart Uploads:**

**Overview:**

- Multipart upload uploads data in parallel

- Faster and more efficient than uploading large object in single upload

- If multipart upload fails, only retry the failed part (not entire object)

- Eliminates need to restart entire object upload on failure

**When to Use Multipart Uploads:**

- **Recommendation:** Use multipart uploads for objects larger than 100 MiB (mebibytes)

- Provides better performance and reliability for large files

- Optimal for large datasets, backups, media files, and archive data

**Multipart Upload Constraints:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│            Multipart Upload Specifications                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Maximum Parts per Object:      10,000 parts
         │
         ▼
    ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
    │  Each Part Size Constraints:       │
    │                                    │
    │  Minimum:  10 MiB per part         │
    │  Maximum:  50 GiB per part         │
    └────────────────────────────────────┘
         │
         ▼
    Total Object Size Range:
    • Minimum: 10 MiB (single part)
    • Maximum: 500 TiB (10,000 parts × 50 GiB)

MiB = Mebibyte, GiB = Gibibyte, TiB = Tebibyte
```

**Key Features:**

**1. Flexible Upload Management:**

- Can pause uploads of individual parts

- Resume uploads when schedule and resources allow

- Provides control over upload timing and resource utilization

**2. Lifecycle Management:**

- Use object lifecycle policy rules to automatically delete uncommitted multipart uploads

- Can delete failed multipart uploads after specific number of days

- Prevents storage consumption by incomplete uploads

- Perform cleanup operation using OCI Console

**Two Methods for Multipart Uploads:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Multipart Upload Methods Comparison                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│  Method 1: API Approach      │  │  Method 2: CLI Approach      │
│                              │  │                              │
│  Manual Control & Steps:     │  │  Automated & Simplified:     │
│                              │  │                              │
│  • Manually split object     │  │  • No manual splitting       │
│    into parts                │  │  • Specify part size only    │
│                              │  │  • Auto-split by Object      │
│  • Initiate upload (get ID)  │  │    Storage                   │
│                              │  │                              │
│  • Upload each part          │  │  • No commit needed          │
│    (get E-tag per part)      │  │  • Auto-commit on complete   │
│                              │  │                              │
│  • Manually commit upload    │  │  • Set parallel upload count │
│    with part numbers & E-tags│  │    (optional)                │
│                              │  │                              │
│  ✓ Granular control          │  │  ✓ Simpler workflow          │
│  ✓ Programmatic integration  │  │  ✓ Faster setup              │
│  ✗ More complex workflow     │  │  ✗ Less granular control     │
└──────────────────────────────┘  └──────────────────────────────┘
```

**Method 1: Multipart Upload Using API:**

**Step-by-Step API Workflow:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              API Multipart Upload Process                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Step 1: Create Parts (Manual Split)
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Example: 200 MB Object                 │
│                                         │
│  Split into 10 parts × 20 MB each       │
│                                         │
│  ┌────┐ ┌────┐ ┌────┐     ┌────┐       │
│  │ P1 │ │ P2 │ │ P3 │ ... │P10 │       │
│  └────┘ └────┘ └────┘     └────┘       │
│  20 MB  20 MB  20 MB      20 MB        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Step 2: Initiate Upload
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Create Multipart Upload API Call       │
│                                         │
│  Object Storage Returns:                │
│  • Upload ID                            │
│  • Status: Active                       │
│                                         │
│  Upload remains active until            │
│  committed or aborted                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Step 3: Upload Object Parts
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Upload Part Request (for each part)    │
│                                         │
│  For each part uploaded, Object         │
│  Storage returns:                       │
│  • Part Number (1, 2, 3... 10)          │
│  • Entity Tag (E-tag) value             │
│                                         │
│  Store these values for commit step    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Step 4: Commit Upload
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Commit Multipart Upload Request        │
│                                         │
│  Submit:                                │
│  • Upload ID                            │
│  • Part numbers + E-tag values          │
│                                         │
│  Object Storage reconstructs object     │
│  from constituent parts                 │
│                                         │
│  Result: Complete object available      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**API Requirements:**

- Must track part numbers and corresponding E-tag values for each uploaded part

- Both part number and E-tag required when committing upload

- Commit step reconstructs object from all constituent parts

**Method 2: Multipart Upload Using CLI:**

**CLI Workflow (Simplified):**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              CLI Multipart Upload Process                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Single Command Upload
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  No manual splitting required           │
│                                         │
│  Just specify:                          │
│  • Source file                          │
│  • Part size (mandatory)                │
│  • Parallel upload count (optional)     │
│                                         │
│  Object Storage handles:                │
│  • Automatic splitting into parts       │
│  • Parallel upload execution            │
│  • Automatic commit on completion       │
│                                         │
│  Result: Complete object uploaded       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**CLI Command Format:**

```
oci os object put --bucket-name <bucket> --name <object-name> --file <file-path> --part-size <size-in-MiB> --parallel-upload-count <max-parallel-uploads>
```

**CLI Parameters:**

- **--bucket-name (mandatory):** Name of the bucket where object will be stored

- **--name (optional):** Name of the object in the bucket; if not specified, uses the filename from file path

- **--file (mandatory):** Path to the file to upload

- **--part-size (mandatory):** Size of each part in MiB (must be integer value)

- **--parallel-upload-count (optional):** Maximum number of parallel uploads allowed; controls how many parts upload simultaneously

**Key Differences from API Approach:**

- No need to manually split object into parts

- Specify part size, and Object Storage automatically splits object

- No manual commit step required after upload completes

- Simpler command-line interface for quick uploads

**Multipart Upload Benefits:**

**1. Performance:**

- Parallel upload of multiple parts significantly faster than single upload

- Better utilization of available network bandwidth

- Reduced total upload time for large objects

**2. Reliability:**

- Failed parts can be retried individually without restarting entire upload

- Reduces impact of transient network issues

- More resilient upload process for large files

**3. Flexibility:**

- Pause and resume uploads as needed

- Control resource utilization through parallel upload settings

- Manage upload timing based on network availability

**4. Storage Management:**

- Lifecycle policies automatically clean up failed or abandoned uploads

- Prevents unnecessary storage consumption

- Automated maintenance of storage buckets

**Use Cases:**

- Uploading large backup files (> 100 MiB)

- Media file uploads (videos, high-resolution images)

- Big data file ingestion

- Database backup and recovery files

- Archive data migration to cloud

- Any scenario where large files need efficient, reliable upload

================================================================================

**Managing Buckets and Objects:**

**Naming Requirements:**

- Bucket names and object names are **case sensitive**

- Case matters: "MyBucket" ≠ "mybucket" ≠ "MYBUCKET"

- Important for consistent access and management

**Bucket Structure Constraints:**

- **Cannot nest buckets** - buckets cannot contain other buckets

- Flat bucket hierarchy within namespace

- All buckets exist at same level within object storage namespace

**Object Size:**

- Objects can be up to **10 tebibytes (TiB)** in size

- Supports very large files for diverse use cases

**Object Modification Behavior:**

- Cannot edit or append data to existing object

- Cannot make partial modifications to object content

- **Must replace entire object** to make any changes

- Full object replacement required for any updates

**Key Management Features:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│         Bucket and Object Management Features                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────┐
│  Object Versioning       │        │ Pre-Authenticated        │
│                          │        │ Requests (PARs)          │
│  • Retain previous       │        │                          │
│    versions of objects   │        │ • Access without         │
│  • Version history       │        │   credentials            │
│  • Rollback capability   │        │ • Temporary access URLs  │
│  • Data protection       │        │ • Time-limited access    │
└──────────────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────┐
│  Retention Rules         │        │  Replication Policies    │
│                          │        │                          │
│  • Immutable storage     │        │ • Automatic replication  │
│  • Bucket-level rules    │        │ • Same or different      │
│  • Compliance support    │        │   region                 │
│  • Governance & legal    │        │ • Disaster recovery      │
└──────────────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────┐
│  Security Zones          │        │  Object Copy             │
│                          │        │                          │
│  • Oracle security       │        │ • Copy within region     │
│    principles            │        │ • Copy across regions    │
│  • Mandatory private     │        │ • Flexible data          │
│    buckets               │        │   distribution           │
└──────────────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────┐
│  Lifecycle Policies      │
│                          │
│  • Automated archiving   │
│  • Automated deletion    │
│  • Predefined schedule   │
│  • Cost optimization     │
└──────────────────────────┘
```

**1. Object Versioning:**

- Enables retention of previous versions of objects

- Maintains version history for objects

- Provides rollback capability to earlier versions

- Protects against accidental overwrites or deletions

**2. Pre-Authenticated Requests (PARs):**

- Provide way to access bucket or object without user credentials

- Generate temporary access URLs

- Time-limited access grants

- No need to share credentials with external users

- Useful for sharing objects with third parties securely

**3. Retention Rules:**

- Applied at bucket level

- Provide **immutable object storage** options

- Data written to object storage cannot be modified or deleted during retention period

- **Use Cases:**

  - Governance requirements
  
  - Regulatory compliance
  
  - Legal hold requirements
  
  - Industry-specific retention mandates

**4. Replication Policies:**

- Automatically replicate objects from one bucket to another

- **Replication Options:**

  - Same region (different buckets)
  
  - Different regions (cross-region replication)

- Use Cases:

  - Disaster recovery and business continuity
  
  - Geographic data distribution
  
  - Compliance with data residency requirements
  
  - Performance optimization for global users

**5. Security Zones:**

- Ensure cloud resources comply with Oracle security principles

- Enforce security best practices automatically

- **Security Zone Requirement:** Buckets must be private (not publicly accessible)

- Prevents accidental public exposure of sensitive data

- Built-in compliance with Oracle security standards

**6. Object Copy:**

- Copy objects to other buckets in same region

- Copy objects to buckets in different regions

- Flexible data distribution without replication policies

- On-demand copying for specific objects

- Useful for data migration and distribution scenarios

**7. Object Lifecycle Policies:**

- Automatically manage archiving and deletion of objects

- Operate according to predefined schedule

- **Automation Capabilities:**

  - Archive objects to lower-cost storage tiers after specific time
  
  - Delete objects automatically after retention period expires
  
  - Transition objects between storage tiers based on age

- **Benefits:**

  - Cost optimization by moving infrequently accessed data to cheaper tiers
  
  - Automated compliance with retention requirements
  
  - Reduced manual management overhead
  
  - Consistent policy enforcement

**Summary of Key Restrictions:**

- Bucket and object names are case sensitive

- Cannot nest buckets within other buckets

- Objects limited to 10 TiB maximum size

- Cannot edit or append to objects - must replace entire object

**Summary of Management Capabilities:**

- Versioning protects against data loss and enables rollback

- PARs enable secure temporary access without credential sharing

- Retention rules ensure compliance with governance and legal requirements

- Replication provides disaster recovery and geographic distribution

- Security zones enforce Oracle security best practices

- Object copy enables flexible on-demand data distribution

- Lifecycle policies automate storage management and cost optimization

================================================================================

**Object Storage Retention Rules:**

**Overview:**

- Protect data from accidental or malicious update, overwrite, or deletion

- Provides immutable WORM-compliant (Write Once, Read Many) storage options for object storage and archive storage

- Can be locked to prevent rule modification and data deletion or modification, even by administrators

- Configured at the bucket level

**Two Types of Retention Rules:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   Retention Rule Types                              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    1. Time-Bound Retention Rule                  │
│                                                                  │
│  • Specify duration during rule creation                        │
│  • Object modification and deletion prevented for duration      │
│  • Duration applied individually to each object based on        │
│    object's last modified timestamp                             │
│                                                                  │
│  Configuration:                                                 │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  • Retention Time Amount (e.g., 1, 12, 24)            │    │
│  │  • Retention Time Unit (days,  years)          │    │
│  │  • Optional: Retention Rule Lock (irreversible)       │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  2. Indefinite Retention Rule                    │
│                                                                  │
│  • No duration specified                                        │
│  • Object modification and deletion prevented until rule        │
│    is deleted                                                   │
│  • Remains in effect indefinitely                               │
│                                                                  │
│  Configuration:                                                 │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  • No Retention Time Amount required                  │    │
│  │  • Rule active until manually deleted                 │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Retention Rule Lock:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                  Retention Rule Lock Feature                        │
│                      (Time-Bound Rules Only)                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

CRITICAL: Irreversible Operation
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ⚠️  WARNING: Cannot be undone once locked                        │
│                                                                  │
│  • Not even tenancy administrator can delete a locked rule      │
│  • Mandatory 14-day delay before rule is permanently locked     │
│  • Delay allows testing, modification, or deletion of rule      │
│  • After lock is complete, rule CANNOT be modified or removed   │
│                                                                  │
│  Only Way to Delete Locked Rule:                                │
│  └──▶ Delete the entire bucket                                  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Lock Process Timeline:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Day 0: Enable Retention Rule Lock                              │
│           ↓                                                     │
│  Days 1-13: Testing Period                                      │
│           • Can modify rule                                     │
│           • Can delete rule                                     │
│           • Can remove lock                                     │
│           ↓                                                     │
│  Day 14: Lock Becomes Permanent                                 │
│           • Rule cannot be deleted                              │
│           • Rule cannot be modified                             │
│           • Only option: Delete bucket to remove rule           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Retention Duration for Time-Bound Rules:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│         How Duration is Applied to Objects                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Key Concept: Duration applied individually to each object based on 
             object's last modified timestamp (not creation time of rule)

Example Scenario:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Bucket Name: "OCI"                                              │
│  Retention Rule: Time-bound with 1-year duration                 │
│                                                                  │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  Object 1: "OCI-test"                                   │    │
│  │  Last Modified: 14 months ago                           │    │
│  │                                                         │    │
│  │  Result:                                                │    │
│  │  ✓ CAN be modified or deleted                          │    │
│  │                                                         │    │
│  │  Why: Last modified time (14 months) exceeds           │    │
│  │       retention duration (12 months)                    │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                                  │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  Object 2: "OCI-demo"                                   │    │
│  │  Last Modified: 3 months ago                            │    │
│  │                                                         │    │
│  │  Result:                                                │    │
│  │  ✗ CANNOT be modified or deleted for next 9 months     │    │
│  │                                                         │    │
│  │  Why: Last modified time (3 months) is less than       │    │
│  │       retention duration (12 months)                    │    │
│  │       Protected for remaining: 12 - 3 = 9 months        │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Calculation Formula:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  IF (Current Time - Last Modified Time) < Retention Duration    │
│  THEN: Object is protected                                      │
│                                                                 │
│  IF (Current Time - Last Modified Time) >= Retention Duration  │
│  THEN: Object can be modified or deleted                       │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Multiple Retention Rules:**

- Can create **multiple retention rules** for a single bucket

- **Rule Precedence:** Indefinite retention rule is applied **before** any time-bound rule is considered

- Most restrictive rule takes precedence for each object

**Three Primary Use Cases:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Retention Rule Use Cases                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Use Case 1: Regulatory Compliance                               │
│                                                                  │
│  Scenario:                                                       │
│  • Industry requires retaining certain data for defined period  │
│  • Retention period is fixed and mandated by regulations        │
│  • Cannot modify or delete data during retention period         │
│                                                                  │
│  Solution:                                                       │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  Time-Bound Retention Rule                            │    │
│  │  • Set duration matching regulatory requirement       │    │
│  │  • Enable Retention Rule Lock                         │    │
│  │  • Ensures compliance and immutability                │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Use Case 2: Data Governance                                     │
│                                                                  │
│  Scenario:                                                       │
│  • Need to protect data per internal business processes         │
│  • Retention period required but may change over time           │
│  • Flexibility needed to adjust retention requirements          │
│                                                                  │
│  Solution:                                                       │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  Time-Bound Retention Rule (WITHOUT Lock)            │    │
│  │  • Set initial retention duration                     │    │
│  │  • DO NOT lock the rule                              │    │
│  │  • Allows modification if business needs change       │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Use Case 3: Legal Hold                                          │
│                                                                  │
│  Scenario:                                                       │
│  • Preserve business data for potential or ongoing lawsuits     │
│  • No defined retention period                                  │
│  • Remains in effect until legal matter is resolved             │
│                                                                  │
│  Solution:                                                       │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  Indefinite Retention Rule                            │    │
│  │  • No duration specified                              │    │
│  │  • Data protected until rule is manually deleted      │    │
│  │  • Removed when legal matter concludes                │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Integration with Other Object Storage Features:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│      Retention Rules Interaction with Other Features               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Feature: Bucket Re-encryption                                   │
│                                                                  │
│  ✓ Retention rules DO NOT block bucket re-encryption            │
│  ✓ Can re-encrypt using Oracle managed keys                     │
│  ✓ Can re-encrypt using your own Vault Master encryption keys   │
│                                                                  │
│  Conclusion: Encryption and retention work independently        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Feature: Lifecycle Policies - Storage Tier Updates              │
│                                                                  │
│  ✓ Lifecycle policies CAN update storage tier of objects        │
│    protected by retention rules                                 │
│  ✓ Can move from Standard to Infrequent Access tier            │
│  ✓ Can move from Infrequent Access to Archive tier             │
│                                                                  │
│  Conclusion: Tier transitions allowed for protected objects     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Feature: Lifecycle Policies - Object Deletion                   │
│                                                                  │
│  ✗ Lifecycle management CANNOT remove objects protected by      │
│    active retention rules                                        │
│  ✗ Deletion rules are blocked by retention rules                │
│                                                                  │
│  Conclusion: Retention rules take precedence over lifecycle     │
│              deletion policies                                   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Feature: Replication Source Bucket                              │
│                                                                  │
│  ✓ CAN create retention rules on a replication source bucket    │
│  ✓ Retention rules apply to objects in source bucket            │
│  ✓ Replication continues to function normally                   │
│                                                                  │
│  Conclusion: Retention rules compatible with replication        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Feature: Versioning                                             │
│                                                                  │
│  ✗ CANNOT add retention rules to bucket with versioning enabled │
│  ✗ Retention rules and versioning are mutually exclusive        │
│                                                                  │
│  Must choose one:                                                │
│  • Object Versioning for version history, OR                    │
│  • Retention Rules for immutable storage                        │
│                                                                  │
│  Conclusion: Retention rules and versioning cannot coexist      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Summary Table:**

```
┌────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
│  Object Storage Feature        │  Works with Retention Rules? │
├────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Bucket Re-encryption          │         ✓ Yes                │
│  Lifecycle - Tier Updates      │         ✓ Yes                │
│  Lifecycle - Object Deletion   │         ✗ No (Blocked)       │
│  Replication Source Bucket     │         ✓ Yes                │
│  Object Versioning             │         ✗ No (Incompatible)  │
└────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key Benefits:**

- **Data Protection:** Prevents accidental or malicious data loss

- **Regulatory Compliance:** Meets industry-specific retention mandates (HIPAA, SOX, FINRA, etc.)

- **Legal Protection:** Supports legal hold requirements for litigation

- **Immutability:** WORM-compliant storage ensures data cannot be tampered with

- **Flexible Options:** Both time-bound and indefinite retention available

- **Administrator Safeguards:** Locked rules cannot be bypassed even by admins

**Important Limitations:**

- Retention rules configured at bucket level (not individual object level)

- Cannot use retention rules with versioning-enabled buckets

- Locked rules can only be removed by deleting entire bucket

- 14-day mandatory delay for rule locking provides safety period

================================================================================

**Object Copy:**

**Overview:**

- Using object copy, you can copy objects to other buckets in same region or to buckets in other regions

- Object Storage service handles copy requests **asynchronously**

- Provides flexible data distribution across buckets and regions

**Key Characteristics:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   Object Copy Behavior                              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Copy Capabilities:
✓ Copy to buckets in same region
✓ Copy to buckets in different regions
✓ Copy across namespaces (different tenancies)

Important Constraints:
✗ Objects CANNOT be copied directly from Archive Storage
✗ Copy operation does NOT automatically create destination buckets
✗ Destination bucket must exist before copy operation

When Object is Copied:
• Destination object receives a NEW E-Tag value
• Source object remains unchanged
• Copy is independent object in destination
```

**Archive Storage Limitation:**

- Objects cannot be copied directly from Archive Storage tier

- **Workaround Required:**

  - First restore archived object to Standard or Infrequent Access tier
  
  - Then perform copy operation
  
  - Archive restoration required before copying

**Destination Bucket Creation:**

- Copy operation does **NOT** automatically create destination buckets

- Destination bucket must be created manually before initiating copy

- Copy operation will fail if destination bucket does not exist

**E-Tag Behavior:**

- When object is copied, destination object receives a **new E-Tag value**

- E-Tag is unique identifier for object version

- Source and destination objects will have different E-Tags after copy

**Four Overwrite Rules:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Copy Overwrite Rules                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Rule 1: Overwrite Destination Object (DEFAULT)                  │
│                                                                  │
│  Behavior:                                                       │
│  • Always overwrites destination object if it exists            │
│  • No E-Tag validation performed                                │
│  • Copy operation succeeds regardless of destination state      │
│                                                                  │
│  When to Use:                                                    │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  • Do not want to limit copy operation by E-Tag       │    │
│  │  • Ensure latest version always copied                │    │
│  │  • Overwriting existing destination is acceptable     │    │
│  │  • General-purpose copy operations                    │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Rule 2: Do Not Overwrite Any Destination Object                 │
│                                                                  │
│  Behavior:                                                       │
│  • Copy operation fails if destination object already exists    │
│  • Prevents overwriting regardless of E-Tag value               │
│  • Preserves existing destination objects                       │
│                                                                  │
│  When to Use:                                                    │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  • Prevent accidental overwriting of existing copy    │    │
│  │  • Preserve existing destination objects              │    │
│  │  • Only copy if destination does not exist            │    │
│  │  • Protect important existing data                    │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Rule 3: Overwrite Destination Object Only If It Matches         │
│          Specified E-Tag                                         │
│                                                                  │
│  Behavior:                                                       │
│  • Copy succeeds ONLY if destination E-Tag matches specified    │
│    E-Tag value                                                  │
│  • Must provide E-Tag value when initiating copy request        │
│  • Copy fails if destination E-Tag does not match               │
│                                                                  │
│  When to Use:                                                    │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  • Prevent accidental overwriting of destination      │    │
│  │    object that was modified                           │    │
│  │  • Ensure destination object is expected version      │    │
│  │  • Conditional copy based on destination state        │    │
│  │  • Protect against unintended overwrites              │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                                  │
│  Example Scenario:                                               │
│  • You expect destination to have specific version              │
│  • Supply that version's E-Tag                                  │
│  • Copy proceeds only if E-Tag still matches                    │
│  • Prevents overwriting if destination was changed              │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Rule 4: Copy Object Only If Source Matches Specified E-Tag      │
│                                                                  │
│  Behavior:                                                       │
│  • Copy succeeds ONLY if source E-Tag matches specified         │
│    E-Tag value                                                  │
│  • Must provide E-Tag value when initiating copy request        │
│  • Copy fails if source E-Tag does not match                    │
│                                                                  │
│  When to Use:                                                    │
│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  • Ensure only specified version of object is copied  │    │
│  │  • Prevent copying wrong/modified version of source   │    │
│  │  • Copy specific object version only                  │    │
│  │  • Conditional copy based on source state             │    │
│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                                  │
│  Example Scenario:                                               │
│  • You want to copy specific version of source object           │
│  • Supply that version's E-Tag                                  │
│  • Copy proceeds only if source still has that E-Tag            │
│  • Prevents copying if source was modified/replaced             │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Overwrite Rules Comparison:**

```
┌────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────┬─────────────┐
│  Overwrite Rule                    │ Validates   │ Validates   │
│                                    │ Source      │ Destination │
├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────┤
│  1. Overwrite Destination          │     ✗       │     ✗       │
│     (Default)                      │             │             │
├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────┤
│  2. Do Not Overwrite Any           │     ✗       │ ✓ (Exists?) │
│     Destination Object             │             │             │
├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────┤
│  3. Overwrite If Destination       │     ✗       │ ✓ (E-Tag)   │
│     Matches E-Tag                  │             │             │
├────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────┼─────────────┤
│  4. Copy If Source Matches E-Tag   │ ✓ (E-Tag)   │     ✗       │
└────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────┴─────────────┘
```

**Copy Operation Configuration:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Copy Configuration Parameters                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Required Parameters:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  1. Destination Namespace                                        │
│     • Object storage namespace of destination bucket             │
│     • Can be same or different from source namespace             │
│                                                                  │
│  2. Destination Region                                           │
│     • Region where destination bucket is located                 │
│     • Can be same region or different region                     │
│                                                                  │
│  3. Destination Bucket                                           │
│     • Name of bucket where object will be copied                 │
│     • Bucket must exist before copy operation                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Optional Parameters:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  4. Destination Object Name (Optional)                           │
│     • Name for object in destination bucket                      │
│     • If not specified, uses source object name                  │
│     • Allows renaming object during copy                         │
│                                                                  │
│  5. Destination Storage Tier (Optional)                          │
│     • Storage tier for destination object                        │
│     • Options: Standard, Infrequent Access, Archive              │
│     • If not specified, uses destination bucket's default tier   │
│                                                                  │
│  6. Overwrite Rule (Optional)                                    │
│     • One of four overwrite rules described above                │
│     • Default: Overwrite Destination Object                      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Copy Operation Workflow:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Copy Process Flow                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Step 1: Verify Prerequisites
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ✓ Source object exists                 │
│  ✓ Source object not in Archive tier    │
│    (or already restored)                │
│  ✓ Destination bucket exists            │
│  ✓ Destination bucket is accessible     │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 2: Configure Copy Parameters
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Specify destination namespace        │
│  • Specify destination region           │
│  • Specify destination bucket           │
│  • Optionally specify object name       │
│  • Optionally specify storage tier      │
│  • Select overwrite rule                │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 3: Initiate Copy Request
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Submit copy request to Object        │
│    Storage service                      │
│  • Request handled asynchronously       │
│  • Receive copy request ID              │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 4: Object Storage Processes Copy
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Validate overwrite rule              │
│  • Copy object data to destination      │
│  • Generate new E-Tag for destination   │
│  • Apply destination storage tier       │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 5: Copy Completes
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Destination object available         │
│  • Source object unchanged              │
│  • New E-Tag assigned to destination    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Use Cases:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Copy Use Cases                                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Data Distribution
   • Distribute data across multiple regions for global access
   • Reduce latency by copying data closer to users
   • Replicate data to regional buckets

2. Data Migration
   • Migrate objects between buckets
   • Move data to different storage tiers
   • Reorganize data across namespaces

3. Backup and Recovery
   • Create backup copies in different regions
   • Copy critical data for disaster recovery
   • Maintain redundant copies

4. Testing and Development
   • Copy production data to test/dev buckets
   • Create isolated copies for experimentation
   • Test with production-like data

5. Compliance and Governance
   • Copy data to meet data residency requirements
   • Create compliant copies in specific regions
   • Maintain copies for regulatory purposes

6. Version-Specific Copying
   • Copy specific version of object using E-Tag
   • Ensure copying exact version needed
   • Prevent copying modified versions
```

**Key Differences: Object Copy vs. Replication:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┐
│  Feature                        │ Object Copy  │ Replication  │
├─────────────────────────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┤
│  Trigger                        │   Manual     │  Automatic   │
│  Scope                          │ Single object│ All objects  │
│  Timing                         │  On-demand   │  Continuous  │
│  Destination Control            │   Full       │   Limited    │
│  Destination Writability        │ Read-Write   │  Read-Only   │
│  Overwrite Rules                │     Yes      │      No      │
│  E-Tag Validation               │  Available   │      No      │
│  Use Case                       │ Selective    │   Automated  │
│                                 │   copying    │  mirroring   │
└─────────────────────────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────┘
```

**Important Considerations:**

- **Asynchronous Processing:** Copy requests handled asynchronously; large objects may take time

- **New E-Tag:** Destination object gets new E-Tag; source and destination E-Tags differ

- **Bucket Pre-existence:** Destination bucket must exist; copy does not create it

- **Archive Limitation:** Cannot copy directly from Archive tier; must restore first

- **Permissions Required:** Need appropriate IAM permissions for both source and destination

- **Storage Tier Flexibility:** Can specify different storage tier for destination object

- **Rename Capability:** Can rename object during copy by specifying different destination name

**Summary:**

- Object copy provides flexible, on-demand object duplication across buckets and regions

- Four overwrite rules offer granular control over copy behavior

- E-Tag validation enables version-specific and conditional copying

- Asynchronous processing handles large object copies efficiently

- Useful for data distribution, migration, backup, and compliance scenarios

================================================================================

**Object Storage Replication:**

**Overview:**

- Using replication policy, you can replicate objects in one bucket to another bucket in same or different region

- Enables automatic object copying between buckets for disaster recovery and compliance

- Asynchronous replication process ensures data availability across regions

**Replication Policy Behavior:**

**After Policy Creation:**

- Destination bucket becomes **read-only** (can only be updated by replication from source bucket)

- Objects uploaded to source bucket **after** policy creation are asynchronously replicated to destination bucket

- Objects uploaded to source bucket **before** policy creation are **NOT replicated**

- Objects deleted from source bucket **after** policy creation are automatically deleted from destination bucket

**Replication Example:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Storage Replication Architecture                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Mumbai Region                          Hyderabad Region
┌──────────────────────┐               ┌──────────────────────┐
│  Source Bucket 1     │──────────────▶│  Destination Bucket  │
│  (Read-Write)        │  Replication  │  (Read-Only)         │
│                      │     Policy    │                      │
│  • Upload objects    │               │  • Auto-updated      │
│  • Delete objects    │               │  • Cannot upload     │
│  • Modify objects    │               │    directly          │
└──────────────────────┘               └──────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────┐
│  Source Bucket 2     │──────┐
│  (Read-Write)        │      │  Can replicate to:
└──────────────────────┘      │  • Same region bucket
                              │  • Different region bucket
                              └─▶ Target bucket
```

**Benefits of Object Storage Replication:**

**1. Disaster Recovery:**

- Aids in disaster recovery efforts

- Ensures business continuity with replicated data copies

- Quick recovery from regional failures

**2. Data Residency and Compliance:**

- Addresses data residency requirements

- Meets compliance mandates for data location

- Satisfies regulatory requirements for data backup

**3. Protection from Regional Outages:**

- Provides protection from regional outages

- Data copied to another region ensures availability

- Reduces risk of data loss from localized disasters

**4. Reduced Latency:**

- Maintaining multiple copies of data in regional locations closer to user access helps reduce latency

- Improves user experience by serving data from geographically closer locations

- Better performance for globally distributed applications

**Creating Replication Policy:**

**Required Configuration:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Replication Policy Configuration                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Policy Name
   └─ Unique identifier for replication policy

2. Destination Region
   └─ Select target region for replication
      • Can be same region (different bucket)
      • Can be different region

3. Destination Bucket
   └─ Specify target bucket name
      ⚠️ Bucket must exist BEFORE creating policy
      ⚠️ Policy does NOT automatically create bucket
```

**Important Notes:**

- **Replication overwrites objects:** If destination bucket has objects with same name as objects in source bucket, they will be overwritten

- **Pre-existing objects not replicated:** Objects uploaded to source bucket before policy creation are NOT replicated

- **Destination bucket must exist:** Replication policy creation does NOT automatically create destination bucket - must create it manually first

**Replication Limitations and Considerations:**

**Bucket Requirements:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│         Replication Policy Constraints                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

✓ Supported Storage Tiers:
  • Source bucket: Standard OR Archive tier
  • Destination bucket: Standard OR Archive tier

✗ Policy Limits:
  • Maximum 1 replication policy per source bucket
  • Maximum 1 source for each replication destination bucket
  • Maximum 1 destination for each replication source bucket

✗ Chain Replication NOT Supported:
  • Destination bucket cannot be replication source
  • Cannot create replication chains (A → B → C)
```

**Destination Bucket Restrictions:**

**1. Read-Only After Policy Creation:**

- Destination bucket becomes read-only after replication policy is created

- Cannot upload objects directly to destination bucket

- Only way to add objects: Upload to source bucket → Automatic replication to destination

**2. Deletion Protection:**

- Cannot delete replication destination bucket while replication is active

- Must stop replication before deleting destination bucket

**3. Chain Replication Not Supported:**

- Destination bucket cannot also be replication source

- Prevents creating replication chains

**Stopping Replication:**

**Two Methods to Stop Replication:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Methods to Stop Replication                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Method 1: Delete Replication Policy from Source
┌────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Go to source bucket                 │
│  • Delete replication policy           │
│  • Destination bucket becomes writable │
│  • Can now upload to destination       │
└────────────────────────────────────────┘

Method 2: Make Destination Bucket Writable
┌────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Go to destination bucket            │
│  • Stop replication                    │
│  • Destination becomes writable        │
│  • Source policy automatically removed │
└────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**After Stopping Replication:**

- Destination bucket becomes writable again

- Can upload objects directly to former destination bucket

- Can delete the bucket if needed

- Previously replicated objects remain in destination bucket

**Replication Workflow:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Complete Replication Setup Workflow                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Step 1: Create Destination Bucket
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Manually create bucket in target     │
│    region (same or different)           │
│  • Select storage tier                  │
│  • Configure bucket settings            │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 2: Create Replication Policy
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • On source bucket, create policy      │
│  • Specify destination region & bucket  │
│  • Provide policy name                  │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 3: Automatic Replication Begins
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • New objects uploaded to source       │
│  • Asynchronously replicated            │
│  • Destination bucket becomes read-only │
│  • Deletions also replicated            │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Result: Active Replication
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Source: Read-Write                   │
│  • Destination: Read-Only (auto-update) │
│  • Objects synced automatically         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key Points to Remember:**

- Destination bucket must be created manually BEFORE creating replication policy

- Maximum 1 replication policy per source bucket

- After policy creation, destination bucket becomes read-only

- Objects uploaded BEFORE policy creation are NOT replicated

- Objects uploaded AFTER policy creation are asynchronously replicated

- Deletions from source are automatically replicated to destination

- Chain replication (A → B → C) is NOT supported

- To delete destination bucket, must first stop replication

- Both Standard and Archive tier buckets supported

- Replication can be same-region or cross-region

================================================================================

**Object Storage Logging:**

**Overview:**

- Object Storage can send access events to OCI Logging service for monitoring and auditing

- Provides visibility into bucket access patterns and operations

- Helps with security monitoring, compliance auditing, and troubleshooting

- Based on open source **Fluentd format** for standardized log processing

**Service Logs:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                   OCI Service Logs Overview                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

What are Service Logs?
• Logs emitted by OCI services (such as Object Storage)
• Automatically generated based on service activities
• Standardized format across OCI services
• Captured and managed by OCI Logging service

Log Format:
• Based on open source Fluentd format
• Industry-standard log collection format
• Supports structured log data
• Easy integration with log processing tools

Integration:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  OCI Logging Service                                             │
│         ↓                                                        │
│  OCI Logging Analytics Service                                  │
│  • Enrich log data with additional context                      │
│  • Visualize log data with dashboards and charts                │
│  • Search and analyze logs                                      │
│  • Create alerts based on log patterns                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Logging Configuration:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Storage Logging Levels                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

IMPORTANT: Logging is enabled at the BUCKET LEVEL
           (Not at namespace or object level)

Each bucket can have:
┌────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Log Category 1:                   │  Log Category 2:                   │
│  Read Access Events                │  Write Access Events               │
├────────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Captures:                         │  Captures:                         │
│  • Object retrieval operations     │  • Object upload operations        │
│  • Object listing operations       │  • Object modification operations  │
│  • Object metadata queries         │  • Object deletion operations      │
│                                    │                                    │
│  Request Actions:                  │  Request Actions:                  │
```
