│  ┌──────────────────────────────┐ │  ┌──────────────────────────────┐ │
│  │ • GET    (retrieve object)   │ │  │ • PUT    (upload/update)     │ │
│  │ • LIST   (list objects)      │ │  │ • POST   (create object)     │ │
│  │ • HEAD   (get metadata)      │ │  │ • DELETE (remove object)     │ │
│  └──────────────────────────────┘ │  └──────────────────────────────┘ │
└────────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────┘

Configuration Options:
✓ Can enable Read log only
✓ Can enable Write log only
✓ Can enable BOTH Read and Write logs
✓ Can disable both (no logging)
```

**Request Action Values:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Request Actions in Log Entries                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Read Access Events (request_action field values):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  GET                                                             │
│  • Retrieve object content                                      │
│  • Download object from bucket                                  │
│  • Access object data                                           │
│                                                                  │
│  LIST                                                            │
│  • List objects in bucket                                       │
│  • Query bucket contents                                        │
│  • Enumerate objects                                            │
│                                                                  │
│  HEAD                                                            │
│  • Retrieve object metadata                                     │
│  • Check object existence                                       │
│  • Get object properties without downloading content            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Write Access Events (request_action field values):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  PUT                                                             │
│  • Upload new object                                            │
│  • Update existing object (overwrite)                           │
│  • Modify object content                                        │
│                                                                  │
│  POST                                                            │
│  • Create new object                                            │
│  • Initiate multipart upload                                    │
│  • Complete multipart upload                                    │
│                                                                  │
│  DELETE                                                          │
│  • Remove object from bucket                                    │
│  • Delete object permanently                                    │
│  • Delete specific version (if versioning enabled)              │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Enabling Object Storage Logging:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Two Methods to Enable Logging                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Method 1: Enable from Object Storage Bucket
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Step 1: Navigate to Object Storage Bucket                       │
│          └─ Select the bucket you want to enable logging for     │
│                                                                   │
│  Step 2: Go to Resources → Logs                                  │
│          └─ Access logging configuration section                 │
│                                                                   │
│  Step 3: Enable Read and/or Write Logs                           │
│          ├─ Enable Read Access Events (optional)                 │
│          └─ Enable Write Access Events (optional)                │
│                                                                   │
│  Step 4: Configure Log Settings                                  │
│          ├─ Specify Log Group (required)                         │
│          │  • Select existing log group OR                       │
│          │  • Create new log group                               │
│          │                                                        │
│          └─ Specify Log Name (required)                          │
│             • Unique name for this log                            │
│             • Identifies logs from this bucket                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Method 2: Enable from Observability & Management
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Step 1: Navigate to Observability & Management                  │
│          └─ Access OCI Logging service                           │
│                                                                   │
│  Step 2: Create or Select Log Group                              │
│          └─ Organize logs by grouping related logs together      │
│                                                                   │
│  Step 3: Create Service Log                                      │
│          ├─ Select Service: Object Storage                       │
│          ├─ Select Resource: Specific bucket                     │
│          └─ Select Log Category:                                 │
│             • Read Access Events OR                              │
│             • Write Access Events                                │
│                                                                   │
│  Step 4: Configure Log Settings                                  │
│          ├─ Specify Log Group (required)                         │
│          └─ Specify Log Name (required)                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Log Configuration Requirements:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Required Configuration Parameters                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Log Group
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Logical container for organizing related logs          │
   │  • Can have multiple logs within a log group              │
   │  • Helps organize logs by environment, application, etc.  │
   │  • Must specify existing group or create new one          │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Log Name
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Unique identifier for specific log                     │
   │  • Helps distinguish logs from different buckets          │
   │  • Should be descriptive and meaningful                   │
   │  • Example: "production-bucket-read-logs"                 │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Log Category (automatically determined by selection)
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Read Access Events - for GET, LIST, HEAD operations   │
   │  • Write Access Events - for PUT, POST, DELETE operations │
   │  • Can enable both categories for complete coverage       │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Log Data Content:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Information Captured in Logs                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Log entries typically include:

Identity Information:
• User who performed the action (OCID or principal)
• Authentication method used
• Source IP address

Request Details:
• Request action (GET, PUT, DELETE, etc.)
• Object name/key
• Bucket name
• Request timestamp
• Request ID (for tracking and correlation)

Response Information:
• HTTP status code (200, 404, 403, etc.)
• Response time
• Bytes transferred
• Error messages (if applicable)

Resource Information:
• Compartment OCID
• Namespace
• Region
```

**Integration with OCI Logging Analytics:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           OCI Logging Analytics Integration                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Object Storage Logs
         ↓
   OCI Logging Service
         ↓
   OCI Logging Analytics Service
         ↓
   Enhanced Capabilities:

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  1. Log Enrichment                                               │
│     • Add context to log data                                   │
│     • Correlate logs from multiple sources                      │
│     • Associate logs with metadata                              │
│                                                                  │
│  2. Visualization                                                │
│     • Create dashboards for log data                            │
│     • Generate charts and graphs                                │
│     • Visual representation of access patterns                  │
│                                                                  │
│  3. Search and Analysis                                          │
│     • Powerful search capabilities across logs                  │
│     • Query log data using filters                              │
│     • Pattern recognition and anomaly detection                 │
│                                                                  │
│  4. Alerting                                                     │
│     • Set up alerts based on log patterns                       │
│     • Notify on suspicious activities                           │
│     • Automated responses to specific events                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Use Cases:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Storage Logging Use Cases                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Security Monitoring
   • Track who is accessing objects
   • Detect unauthorized access attempts
   • Monitor for unusual access patterns
   • Identify potential security breaches

2. Compliance Auditing
   • Maintain audit trail for regulatory compliance
   • Track data access for HIPAA, PCI-DSS, SOX, etc.
   • Generate compliance reports
   • Prove adherence to data governance policies

3. Troubleshooting
   • Diagnose application issues
   • Track failed access attempts
   • Identify performance bottlenecks
   • Debug integration problems

4. Usage Analytics
   • Understand access patterns
   • Identify frequently accessed objects
   • Optimize storage tier assignments
   • Plan capacity and performance improvements

5. Cost Optimization
   • Track data transfer patterns
   • Identify infrequently accessed data for tier changes
   • Monitor storage access costs
   • Optimize lifecycle policies

6. Access Pattern Analysis
   • Understand user behavior
   • Identify popular content
   • Optimize data placement
   • Improve application performance
```

**Best Practices:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Logging Best Practices                                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Enable Appropriate Log Categories
   ✓ Enable read logs for monitoring data access
   ✓ Enable write logs for tracking data modifications
   ✓ Consider both for comprehensive coverage

2. Organize Logs with Log Groups
   ✓ Use descriptive log group names
   ✓ Group logs by environment (dev, test, prod)
   ✓ Separate logs by application or project

3. Use Meaningful Log Names
   ✓ Include bucket name in log name
   ✓ Indicate log type (read/write)
   ✓ Add environment identifier
   ✓ Example: "prod-media-bucket-write-logs"

4. Integrate with Logging Analytics
   ✓ Enable Logging Analytics for advanced analysis
   ✓ Create custom dashboards
   ✓ Set up alerts for important events
   ✓ Use for security monitoring

5. Retention and Archival
   ✓ Configure appropriate log retention period
   ✓ Archive logs for long-term compliance
   ✓ Balance retention needs with storage costs

6. Monitor Log Volume
   ✓ Be aware that logging generates costs
   ✓ High-traffic buckets generate significant log volume
   ✓ Monitor logging costs as part of overall expenses
   ✓ Adjust logging based on actual needs

7. Security Considerations
   ✓ Restrict access to logs using IAM policies
   ✓ Logs contain sensitive access information
   ✓ Protect logs from unauthorized viewing
   ✓ Use encryption for log data
```

**Important Considerations:**

- **Bucket-Level Configuration:** Logging is configured per bucket, not per object or namespace

- **Separate Log Categories:** Read and write logs are separate; can enable independently

- **Asynchronous Logging:** Logs may not appear immediately; slight delay expected

- **Cost Implications:** Logging incurs costs based on log volume and retention

- **Fluentd Format:** Standard format enables integration with various log processing tools

- **No Impact on Performance:** Logging does not impact object storage performance

- **Granular Permissions:** Use IAM policies to control who can view logs

**Summary:**

- Object Storage logging provides comprehensive visibility into bucket access activities

- Two log categories: Read access events (GET, LIST, HEAD) and Write access events (PUT, POST, DELETE)

- Logging enabled at bucket level with required log group and log name

- Based on Fluentd format for standardized log processing

- Integrates with OCI Logging Analytics for enhanced analysis and visualization

- Essential for security monitoring, compliance auditing, and operational troubleshooting

- Can be enabled via Object Storage bucket configuration or Observability & Management section

================================================================================

**Object Storage Tiers:**

**Overview:**

- Storage tiers help maximize access performance where appropriate and minimize storage costs

- Every object uploaded to object storage is assigned to a storage tier

- Storage tier determines storage cost and associated retrieval fees

- Tier selection balances cost and performance based on access patterns

**Bucket Creation Storage Tier Options:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│         Two Storage Tier Options at Bucket Creation                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌────────────────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────────────────┐
│  Option 1:                     │    │  Option 2:                     │
│  Standard Storage Tier Bucket  │    │  Archive Storage Tier Bucket   │
│                                │    │                                │
│  Default: Standard             │    │  Default: Archive              │
│                                │    │                                │
│  Can contain MIXED objects:    │    │  Can ONLY contain:             │
│  ┌──────────────────────────┐ │    │  ┌──────────────────────────┐ │
│  │ • Standard tier objects  │ │    │  │ • Archive tier objects   │ │
│  │ • Infrequent Access      │ │    │  │   ONLY                   │ │
│  │   tier objects           │ │    │  │                          │ │
│  │ • Archive tier objects   │ │    │  │ No tier flexibility      │ │
│  └──────────────────────────┘ │    │  └──────────────────────────┘ │
│                                │    │                                │
│  ✓ Flexible tier assignment    │    │  ✗ Fixed to Archive tier only  │
│  ✓ Can change object tiers     │    │  ✗ Cannot change object tiers  │
│  ✓ Auto-tiering available      │    │  ✗ No auto-tiering             │
└────────────────────────────────┘    └────────────────────────────────┘

IMPORTANT: Cannot change bucket's default storage tier after creation
            (Tier assignment is fixed at bucket creation time)
```

**Storage Tier Assignment Rules:**

**For Standard Default Storage Tier Bucket:**

- Can explicitly assign any storage tier to objects during upload:

  - Standard tier
  
  - Infrequent Access tier
  
  - Archive tier

- Can change storage tier for individual objects after upload

- Flexibility to mix different tier objects in same bucket

**For Archive Default Storage Tier Bucket:**

- All objects must use Archive storage tier assignment

- No tier flexibility for objects

- Cannot assign Standard or Infrequent Access tier to objects

**Three Storage Tiers:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Storage Tier Comparison                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Tier 1: Standard (Hot Tier)                                       │
│                                                                    │
│  Access Pattern:    Frequent, immediate access required           │
│  Storage Cost:      Highest (but no retrieval fees)               │
│  Availability:      Immediate                                      │
│  Retrieval Fee:     None                                           │
│  Min Retention:     None                                           │
│  Auto-Tiering:      Available (optional feature)                   │
│  Default Tier:      Yes (when bucket created with Standard)       │
│                                                                    │
│  Use Cases:                                                        │
│  • Content repository (images, logs, videos)                       │
│  • Big data and Hadoop workloads                                   │
│  • Frequently accessed application data                            │
│  • Active databases and analytics                                  │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓ Less frequent access
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Tier 2: Infrequent Access / Cool Tier                            │
│                                                                    │
│  Access Pattern:    Infrequent access, immediate when needed      │
│  Storage Cost:      Lower than Standard                            │
│  Availability:      Immediate (no restore required)               │
│  Retrieval Fee:     Per GB data retrieval fee applies             │
│  Min Retention:     31 days (prorated charge if delete earlier)   │
│  Auto-Tiering:      Can be auto-tiering target                     │
│  Default Tier:      No                                             │
│                                                                    │
│  Important: Charged for full 31 days even if deleted earlier      │
│                                                                    │
│  Use Cases:                                                        │
│  • Backups for on-premises data                                    │
│  • Infrequently accessed data requiring quick retrieval           │
│  • Data archive with occasional access needs                       │
│  • Secondary copies for disaster recovery                          │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓ Rare access
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Tier 3: Archive (Cold Tier)                                      │
│                                                                    │
│  Access Pattern:    Rarely accessed, long-term retention          │
│  Storage Cost:      Lowest                                         │
│  Availability:      NOT immediate - restore required (~1 hour)    │
│  Retrieval Fee:     Restore fee applies                            │
│  Min Retention:     90 days (prorated charge if delete earlier)   │
│  Auto-Tiering:      Not applicable                                 │
│  Default Tier:      Yes (when bucket created with Archive)        │
│                                                                    │
│  Important: Objects must be restored before access                │
│             Restore time approximately 1 hour                      │
│                                                                    │
│  Use Cases:                                                        │
│  • Compliance and audit mandates                                   │
│  • Historical data storage                                         │
│  • Regulatory retention requirements                               │
│  • Long-term archival with rare access                             │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Cost Efficiency:  Standard > Infrequent Access > Archive
Access Speed:     Standard = Infrequent Access > Archive (restore time)
```

**Standard Storage Tier Details:**

**Characteristics:**

- Default storage tier for object storage

- Used for data requiring quick, immediate, and frequent access

- Highest storage cost compared to other tiers (no retrieval fees compensate)

- Immediate availability without any delays

**Auto-Tiering Feature:**

- Optional feature available when choosing Standard default storage tier during bucket creation

- Automatically moves objects to Infrequent Access tier based on access patterns

- Optimizes costs without manual intervention

- Monitors object access and transitions infrequently accessed objects automatically

**Use Cases:**

- Content repository for scalable data: images, logs, videos

- Data repository for Hadoop and big data workloads

- Active application data with frequent access requirements

- Real-time analytics and processing workloads

**Infrequent Access Tier (Cool Tier) Details:**

**Characteristics:**

- For data accessed infrequently but must be available immediately when needed

- Lower storage cost than Standard tier

- **Minimum storage retention period: 31 days**

- Immediate availability (no restore process required)

- Per GB data retrieval fee charged when accessing objects

**Cost Implications:**

- If you delete or overwrite objects before 31 days: Charged prorated cost of storing data for full 31 days

- Example: Delete object after 10 days → Still charged for 31 days of storage

- Retrieval fees apply each time object is accessed

**Use Cases:**

- Backups for on-premises data

- Disaster recovery secondary copies

- Infrequently accessed data requiring quick retrieval when needed

- Data with unpredictable access patterns but occasional access requirements

**Archive Storage Tier (Cold Tier) Details:**

**Characteristics:**

- For data that is rarely accessed but must be retained for long periods

- Provides lowest storage cost of all tiers

- **Minimum storage retention period: 90 days**

- Objects NOT immediately accessible - must be restored before access

- **Restore time: Approximately 1 hour**

**Archive Bucket Restrictions:**

- Archive storage tier buckets can ONLY contain objects with Archive tier assignment

- No flexibility to mix tiers in Archive bucket

- All objects inherit Archive tier properties

**Restore Process:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Archive Object Restore Workflow                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Step 1: Object in Archive Tier (Not Accessible)
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Archive Tier Object                │
│  • Lowest storage cost              │
│  • Not accessible                   │
│  • Must initiate restore            │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────┘
               │
               ▼ Restore Request Initiated
Step 2: Restore Process (~1 Hour)
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Restore in Progress                │
│  • Object being prepared            │
│  • Approximate restore time: 1 hour │
│  • Cannot access during restore     │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────┘
               │
               ▼ Restore Complete
Step 3: Object Restored to Standard Tier (Accessible)
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Standard Tier Object               │
│  • Immediate access available       │
│  • Restored = Returned to Standard  │
│  • Can read and download object     │
│  • Higher tier cost now applies     │
└─────────────────────────────────────┘

Key Point: Restoring objects returns them to Standard Tier for access
```

**Restore Behavior:**

- When you restore Archive tier objects, you are returning those objects to Standard tier

- After restore completes, object accessible like any Standard tier object

- Object remains in Standard tier after restore (incurs Standard tier costs)

- Restore time approximately 1 hour (not instant access)

**Cost Implications:**

- If you delete or overwrite objects before 90 days: Charged prorated cost of storing data for full 90 days

- Example: Delete object after 30 days → Still charged for 90 days of storage

- Restore operation may incur additional fees

**Use Cases:**

- Compliance and audit mandates with long-term retention requirements

- Historical data that is rarely accessed but legally required

- Regulatory retention policies (e.g., financial records, healthcare records)

- Long-term archival where access is exceptional rather than routine

**Storage Tier Decision Matrix:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              When to Use Each Storage Tier                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Choose Standard Tier When:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ✓ Data accessed frequently (daily or multiple times/day)│
│ ✓ Immediate access critical for operations              │
│ ✓ Performance is priority over cost                     │
│ ✓ Active workloads requiring low-latency access         │
│ ✓ Content delivery and streaming applications           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Choose Infrequent Access Tier When:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ✓ Data accessed less than once per month                │
│ ✓ Immediate access needed when accessed                 │
│ ✓ Can tolerate per-access retrieval fees                │
│ ✓ Data retained for at least 31 days                    │
│ ✓ Backup or secondary data with occasional access       │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Choose Archive Tier When:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ✓ Data accessed very rarely (annually or less)          │
│ ✓ Can tolerate ~1 hour restore time                     │
│ ✓ Cost is primary concern                               │
│ ✓ Data retained for at least 90 days                    │
│ ✓ Compliance/regulatory retention without active use    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key Takeaways:**

- Cannot change bucket's default storage tier after bucket creation

- Standard tier buckets offer flexibility - can contain objects of any tier

- Archive tier buckets restricted - can only contain Archive tier objects

- Standard tier: Highest cost, immediate access, no retrieval fees, frequent use

- Infrequent Access tier: Medium cost, immediate access, retrieval fees, 31-day minimum retention

- Archive tier: Lowest cost, restore required (~1 hour), 90-day minimum retention, rare access

- Minimum retention periods enforced through prorated charges if deleted early

- Auto-tiering available for Standard tier buckets to optimize costs automatically

- Restoring Archive objects returns them to Standard tier for access

================================================================================

**Auto-Tiering:**

**Overview:**

- Auto-tiering determines which storage tier your data should be in based on usage patterns

- Automatically optimizes storage costs without manual intervention

- Eliminates need to write complex lifecycle policy rules or deliberate over tier selection

- Enabled at bucket level for Standard storage tier buckets

**How Auto-Tiering Works:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Auto-Tiering Workflow                                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Initial State: Data in Standard Tier
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Standard Tier (Hot)                │
│  • Frequent access                  │
│  • Immediate availability           │
│  • Higher storage cost              │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────┘
               │
               │ Monitors access patterns
               │ Detects infrequent access
               │
               ▼ Auto-Transition
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Infrequent Access Tier (Cool)      │
│  • Access pattern: Infrequent       │
│  • Lower storage cost               │
│  • Prorated storage costs           │
│  • NO retrieval charges             │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────┘
               │
               │ Monitors access patterns
               │ Detects frequent access again
               │
               ▼ Auto-Transition Back
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Standard Tier (Hot)                │
│  • Access pattern: Frequent         │
│  • Immediate availability           │
│  • Optimized for performance        │
└─────────────────────────────────────┘

Intelligent Movement:
- Standard → Infrequent Access (when access decreases)
- Infrequent Access → Standard (when access increases)
```

**Key Features:**

**1. Bucket-Level Enablement:**

- Auto-tiering is enabled at bucket level (not object level)

- Once enabled, applies to all eligible objects in the bucket

- Available only for Standard storage tier buckets

**2. Access Pattern Monitoring:**

- Continuously monitors data access patterns for objects in bucket

- Automatically detects changes in access frequency

- No manual monitoring or analysis required

**3. Intelligent Object Movement:**

- Moves objects larger than **1 mebibyte (MiB)** from Standard to Infrequent Access tier

- Based on detected access patterns (infrequent access triggers movement)

- Automatically moves objects back to Standard tier when access patterns change (more frequent access)

**4. Object Size Threshold:**

- Only objects larger than 1 MiB are eligible for auto-tiering

- Objects smaller than 1 MiB remain in Standard tier

- Size threshold ensures cost optimization benefits outweigh transition overhead

**Cost Benefits:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Auto-Tiering Cost Advantages                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Traditional Approach vs Auto-Tiering:

┌────────────────────────────────┐    ┌────────────────────────────────┐
│  Manual Tier Management        │    │  Auto-Tiering                  │
│                                │    │                                │
│  • Complex lifecycle policies  │    │  • Automatic tier selection    │
│  • Risk of incorrect tier      │    │  • Intelligent pattern-based   │
│  • Time-consuming analysis     │    │  • No manual intervention      │
│  • Potential cost waste        │    │  • Optimized cost savings      │
│  • Retrieval fees on mistakes  │    │  • NO retrieval charges        │
└────────────────────────────────┘    └────────────────────────────────┘

Auto-Tiering Cost Features:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  1. NO additional cost for auto-tiering feature          │
│  2. NO retrieval charges when moving between tiers       │
│  3. Prorated storage costs (pay only for time in tier)   │
│  4. Automatic cost optimization without manual effort    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**No Additional Costs:**

- Auto-tiering feature itself has **no cost**

- No charges for enabling or using auto-tiering

- Instead, helps reduce overall storage costs

**No Retrieval Charges:**

- **Critical advantage:** No retrieval fees when auto-tiering moves objects between tiers

- Unlike manual tier changes to Infrequent Access tier, auto-tiering eliminates retrieval fees

- Significant cost savings compared to manual tier management

**Prorated Storage Costs:**

- Storage costs are prorated based on time spent in each tier

- Example: Object in Standard for 15 days, then Infrequent Access for 15 days = 50% Standard cost + 50% Infrequent Access cost

- Fair billing based on actual tier usage

**Storage Tier Comparison for Auto-Tiering:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│     Standard vs Infrequent Access for Auto-Tiering                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Standard Tier (Frequent Access):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Optimized for frequent, immediate access              │
│  • Higher storage cost                                   │
│  • NO retrieval charges                                  │
│  • NO minimum retention period                           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↕ Auto-Tiering moves objects based on access
Infrequent Access Tier (Infrequent Access):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Ideal for infrequently accessed data                  │
│  • Lower storage cost than Standard                      │
│  • Immediate availability when needed                    │
│  • 31-day minimum retention (manual)                     │
│  • Retrieval fees (manual tier changes)                  │
│  • NO retrieval fees (auto-tiering transitions)          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Key Difference: Auto-tiering eliminates retrieval fees
                that would apply to manual tier transitions
```

**Use Cases for Auto-Tiering:**

**1. New Application Data Storage:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Scenario: New Application with Unknown Access Patterns    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Challenge:
- Application just launched
- No historical access pattern data
- Uncertain which tier is appropriate
- Risk of choosing wrong tier and wasting costs

Auto-Tiering Solution:
- Enable auto-tiering on bucket
- Service monitors access patterns automatically
- Learns actual usage over time
- Optimizes tier placement based on real behavior
- No upfront tier decision required

Result: Optimal cost without guesswork
```

- When launching new applications with no established access patterns

- Uncertain whether data will be frequently or infrequently accessed

- Auto-tiering learns and adapts to actual usage patterns

- Eliminates guesswork in tier selection for new workloads

**2. Data Storage with Changing Access Patterns:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Scenario: Data with Variable Access Patterns              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Challenge:
- Access patterns change over time
- Seasonal variations (e.g., quarterly reports)
- Project-based access (active during project, idle after)
- Manual tier changes time-consuming and error-prone

Auto-Tiering Solution:
- Continuously monitors access patterns
- Detects changes automatically
- Moves to Infrequent Access when access decreases
- Moves back to Standard when access increases
- Adapts to seasonal or cyclical patterns

Result: Always in optimal tier without manual management
```

- Data with variable or unpredictable access patterns

- Seasonal access variations (busy periods vs quiet periods)

- Project-based data (active during project, dormant after completion)

- Auto-tiering adapts automatically to changing patterns

**Additional Use Cases:**

- **User-Generated Content:** Unknown access patterns for user uploads (some popular, some rarely accessed)

- **Analytics Data:** Historical data accessed frequently during analysis, then rarely afterward

- **Media Libraries:** Recent content accessed frequently, older content accessed occasionally

- **Log Data:** Recent logs accessed often for troubleshooting, older logs rarely accessed

**Benefits Summary:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Auto-Tiering Benefits                                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Cost Optimization:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Automatic cost reduction without manual effort        │
│  • No cost for auto-tiering feature itself               │
│  • Prorated storage costs (fair billing)                 │
│  • NO retrieval charges (major cost saver)               │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Time Savings:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Eliminates complex lifecycle policy creation          │
│  • No manual tier analysis required                      │
│  • No ongoing tier management overhead                   │
│  • Set-and-forget configuration                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Risk Reduction:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Eliminates risk of choosing wrong tier                │
│  • Adapts to changing access patterns automatically      │
│  • No missed optimization opportunities                  │
│  • Always in optimal tier for current usage              │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Simplicity:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Bucket-level enablement (simple configuration)        │
│  • Automatic operation (no manual triggers)              │
│  • Intelligent pattern detection                         │
│  • Seamless tier transitions                             │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key Takeaways:**

- Auto-tiering automatically determines optimal storage tier based on access patterns

- Enabled at bucket level for Standard storage tier buckets only

- Monitors access patterns and moves objects between Standard and Infrequent Access tiers

- Objects larger than 1 MiB eligible for auto-tiering

- No cost for auto-tiering feature - helps reduce costs instead

- No retrieval charges when auto-tiering moves objects (unlike manual tier changes)

- Prorated storage costs based on time in each tier

- Ideal for new applications with unknown access patterns

- Perfect for data with changing or variable access patterns

- Eliminates need for complex lifecycle policies and manual tier management

- Saves time, reduces costs, and minimizes risk of incorrect tier selection

================================================================================

**Securing Object Storage:**

**Overview:**

- Object Storage security requires a multi-layered approach combining access controls, encryption, monitoring, and best practices

- Security involves both initial setup tasks and routine ongoing activities

- OCI provides comprehensive security features to protect data at rest, in transit, and during access

- Proper security configuration essential for compliance, data protection, and risk mitigation

**Initial Security Tasks:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Initial Security Configuration Steps                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. IAM Policies for Access Control
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Use IAM policies to grant access to users and resources│
   │  • Follow principle of least privilege                    │
   │  • Define granular permissions for buckets and objects    │
   │  • Separate read and write permissions                    │
   │  • Use groups for efficient user management               │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Network Source Restrictions
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Restrict access to requests from allowed IP addresses  │
   │  • Use Network Source feature in IAM                      │
   │  • Define allowed CIDR blocks or IP ranges                │
   │  • Block unauthorized network access                      │
   │  • Limit exposure to specific networks                    │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Deletion Prevention
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Prevent accidental deletion of buckets and objects     │
   │  • Restrict BUCKET_DELETE permissions                     │
   │  • Restrict OBJECT_DELETE permissions                     │
   │  • Implement retention rules for critical data            │
   │  • Use versioning for recovery options                    │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

4. Custom Encryption Keys
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Create and manage custom encryption keys in Vault      │
   │  • Don't rely solely on default Oracle-managed keys       │
   │  • Custom keys provide better control                     │
   │  • Enable key rotation policies                           │
   │  • Manage key lifecycle independently                     │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

5. Secured Network Access
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Data in transit encrypted with TLS 1.2 by default      │
   │  • Between customer clients and public endpoints          │
   │  • Use Service Gateway for private VCN access             │
   │  • Avoid exposing buckets to public internet when         │
   │    possible                                               │
   │  • Configure security lists and NSGs appropriately        │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

6. Security Zones
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Create security zones for enhanced compliance          │
   │  • Ensures resources comply with Oracle security best     │
   │    practices                                              │
   │  • Automatic enforcement of security policies             │
   │  • Prevents configuration drift from secure baseline      │
   │  • Mandatory private buckets in security zones            │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**IAM Access Control Details:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              IAM Policy Best Practices for Object Storage           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Principle of Least Privilege:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Grant ONLY the minimum permissions required             │
│                                                          │
│  Instead of: "Allow group Developers to manage           │
│               object-family"                             │
│                                                          │
│  Use:        "Allow group Developers to read objects     │
│               in compartment Development where           │
│               target.bucket.name='dev-bucket'"           │
│                                                          │
│  • Specific compartment                                  │
│  • Specific bucket                                       │
│  • Specific permission (read, not manage)                │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Separate Read and Write Permissions:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Read Access Policy:                                     │
│  "Allow group Readers to read objects in compartment X"  │
│                                                          │
│  Write Access Policy:                                    │
│  "Allow group Writers to manage objects in compartment X"│
│                                                          │
│  Admin Access Policy:                                    │
│  "Allow group Admins to manage buckets in compartment X" │
│                                                          │
│  Benefit: Granular control prevents privilege escalation│
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Use Groups for Management:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Create groups based on job functions                  │
│  • Assign permissions to groups, not individual users    │
│  • Easier to manage and audit                            │
│  • Consistent permissions across team members            │
│  • Examples:                                             │
│    - Object-Storage-Admins                               │
│    - Object-Storage-Readers                              │
│    - Object-Storage-Writers                              │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Network Source Configuration:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Network Source Access Restrictions                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

What is Network Source?
• Feature in IAM that restricts access based on source IP address
• Adds network-level security on top of identity-based access
• Prevents access from unauthorized networks

Configuration Example:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Step 1: Create Network Source                           │
│          • Name: "Corporate-Network"                     │
│          • Allowed IPs: 203.0.113.0/24                   │
│          • Allowed IPs: 198.51.100.0/24                  │
│                                                          │
│  Step 2: Update IAM Policy                               │
│          "Allow group Employees to read objects          │
│           in compartment Production where                │
│           request.networkSource.name='Corporate-Network'"│
│                                                          │
│  Result: Access only from specified IP ranges            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Use Cases:
• Restrict access to corporate network only
• Block access from unknown locations
• Comply with geographic access restrictions
• Prevent data exfiltration from unauthorized locations
```

**Encryption and Key Management:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Encryption Strategy for Object Storage                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Two Encryption Options:
┌────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────┐
│  Oracle-Managed Keys (Default) │  Customer-Managed Keys (Vault) │
├────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┤
│  • Automatic key generation    │  • Full control over keys      │
│  • Managed by Oracle           │  • Create custom keys          │
│  • No additional cost          │  • Manage key lifecycle        │
│  • Simple setup                │  • Implement key rotation      │
│  • Less control                │  • Better compliance           │
│  • No key rotation control     │  • Additional cost             │
└────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────┘

Recommended Approach: Customer-Managed Keys
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Why Use Vault Service for Custom Keys?                  │
│                                                          │
│  ✓ Full control over encryption keys                    │
│  ✓ Implement custom key rotation policies               │
│  ✓ Meet compliance requirements for key management      │
│  ✓ Audit key usage through logging                      │
│  ✓ Revoke key access independently                      │
│  ✓ Bring Your Own Key (BYOK) support                    │
│  ✓ Hardware Security Module (HSM) protection            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Data Encryption Layers:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  1. Data at Rest                                         │
│     • All data encrypted by default                      │
│     • AES-256 encryption algorithm                       │
│     • Encrypted before writing to disk                   │
│                                                          │
│  2. Data in Transit                                      │
│     • TLS 1.2 encryption by default                      │
│     • Between client and Object Storage endpoints        │
│     • Secure HTTPS communication                         │
│                                                          │
│  3. Key Encryption                                       │
│     • Master encryption keys protected                   │
│     • Keys stored in OCI Vault (HSM-backed)              │
│     • Keys never exposed in plaintext                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Security Zones Configuration:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Security Zones for Object Storage                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

What are Security Zones?
• Compartments with enforced security policies
• Automatic compliance with Oracle security best practices
• Prevents creation of non-compliant resources

Security Zone Requirements for Object Storage:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Mandatory Requirements:                                 │
│  • Buckets MUST be private (not publicly accessible)     │
│  • Encryption required for all data                      │
│  • Logging must be enabled                               │
│  • Retention rules recommended                           │
│                                                          │
│  Benefits:                                               │
│  • Automatic enforcement - cannot disable                │
│  • Prevents accidental public exposure                   │
│  • Continuous compliance monitoring                      │
│  • Reduces security misconfigurations                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Creating Secure Buckets in Security Zones:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  1. Create or designate compartment as Security Zone     │
│  2. Attempt to create bucket in Security Zone            │
│  3. Public access option automatically disabled          │
│  4. Encryption automatically enabled                     │
│  5. Bucket created with security policies enforced       │
│                                                          │
│  Cannot Override: Security policies are mandatory        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Routine Security Tasks:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Ongoing Security Operations                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Encryption Key Rotation
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  Purpose:                                                  │
   │  • Limit amount of data encrypted by single key version   │
   │  • Reduce risk if key is compromised                      │
   │  • Meet compliance requirements for key rotation          │
   │                                                           │
   │  Process:                                                 │
   │  • Each master encryption key has key version             │
   │  • Rotate key → Vault generates new key version           │
   │  • New data encrypted with new key version                │
   │  • Old data remains encrypted with old key version        │
   │  • Can re-encrypt old data with new version if needed     │
   │                                                           │
   │  Best Practice:                                           │
   │  • Rotate keys periodically (e.g., annually, quarterly)   │
   │  • Automatic rotation policies available in Vault         │
   │  • Document rotation schedule                             │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Security Auditing
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  Audit Service:                                            │
   │  • Automatically records ALL API calls to OCI resources   │
   │  • Includes Object Storage operations                     │
   │  • Captures: Who, What, When, Where details               │
   │                                                           │
   │  What to Monitor:                                         │
   │  • Failed access attempts (authentication failures)       │
   │  • Unauthorized access attempts                           │
   │  • Deletion operations (bucket/object deletes)            │
   │  • Permission changes (IAM policy modifications)          │
   │  • Unusual access patterns or locations                   │
   │  • High-volume data downloads                             │
   │                                                           │
   │  Best Practice:                                           │
   │  • Review audit logs regularly                            │
   │  • Set up alerts for suspicious activities                │
   │  • Integrate with SIEM systems                            │
   │  • Maintain audit log retention for compliance            │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Object Versioning for Data Protection
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  Purpose:                                                  │
   │  • Minimize data loss from inadvertent deletes            │
   │  • Protect against malicious deletions                    │
   │  • Enable recovery of previous object versions            │
   │  • Maintain object history                                │
   │                                                           │
   │  How It Works:                                            │
   │  • When versioning enabled, deletes create delete markers │
   │  • Object not actually deleted, just hidden               │
   │  • Previous versions retained                             │
   │  • Can restore by removing delete marker                  │
   │                                                           │
   │  Strategy:                                                │
   │  • Enable versioning on critical buckets                  │
   │  • Use lifecycle policies to manage old versions          │
   │  • Balance retention needs with storage costs             │
   │  • Consider versioning for compliance requirements        │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

4. Restrict Delete Permissions
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  Principle:                                                │
   │  • Grant BUCKET_DELETE and OBJECT_DELETE permissions to   │
   │    MINIMUM set of IAM users or groups                     │
   │                                                           │
   │  Implementation:                                          │
   │  • Create separate "Admins" group for delete permissions  │
   │  • Most users should have read/write only                 │
   │  • Require approval process for deletions                 │
   │  • Use audit logs to track all delete operations          │
   │                                                           │
   │  Example Policy Structure:                                │
   │  • General Users: READ and WRITE objects                  │
   │  • Power Users: READ, WRITE, UPDATE objects               │
   │  • Administrators: MANAGE objects and buckets (includes   │
   │    delete)                                                │
   │                                                           │
   │  Protection Layers:                                       │
   │  • Limit who can delete                                   │
   │  • Enable versioning for recovery                         │
   │  • Configure retention rules for immutability             │
   │  • Monitor delete operations via audit logs               │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

5. Data Integrity Verification
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  MD5 Checksum Verification:                                │
   │  • Object Storage provides MD5 checksum for ALL objects   │
   │  • Checksum generated during upload                       │
   │  • Verifies object data integrity                         │
   │                                                           │
   │  Verification Process:                                    │
   │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
   │  │  Step 1: Calculate MD5 checksum of local file     │  │
   │  │          (before upload)                           │  │
   │  │          ↓                                         │  │
   │  │  Step 2: Upload file to Object Storage            │  │
   │  │          ↓                                         │  │
   │  │  Step 3: Retrieve checksum from Object Storage    │  │
   │  │          (from console or API response)            │  │
   │  │          ↓                                         │  │
   │  │  Step 4: Compare checksums                        │  │
   │  │          • Match = Integrity verified              │  │
   │  │          • Mismatch = Corruption detected          │  │
   │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
   │                                                           │
   │  When to Verify:                                          │
   │  • After uploading objects                                │
   │  • After copying or moving objects                        │
   │  • Before critical operations                             │
   │  • During data migration                                  │
   │  • Periodic integrity checks                              │
   │                                                           │
   │  Best Practice:                                           │
   │  • Always verify MD5 checksum after upload                │
   │  • Automate checksum verification in upload scripts       │
   │  • Store original checksums for later verification        │
   │  • Re-upload if checksum mismatch detected                │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

6. Retention Rules Configuration
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  Purpose:                                                  │
   │  • Protect data from modification or deletion             │
   │  • Provide WORM-compliant storage                         │
   │  • Meet regulatory compliance requirements                │
   │  • Prevent even administrators from deleting data         │
   │                                                           │
   │  Retention Rule Types:                                    │
   │  • Time-bound: Protect for specified duration             │
   │  • Indefinite: Protect until rule manually removed        │
   │  • Locked rules: Cannot be modified or deleted            │
   │                                                           │
   │  Use Cases:                                               │
   │  • Regulatory compliance (HIPAA, SOX, FINRA, etc.)        │
   │  • Legal hold requirements                                │
   │  • Data governance policies                               │
   │  • Protection against malicious deletion                  │
   │                                                           │
   │  Refer to "Object Storage Retention Rules" section for   │
   │  detailed configuration and usage information.            │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Comprehensive Security Architecture:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           Layered Security Model for Object Storage                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Layer 1: Network Security
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • TLS 1.2 encryption for data in transit                │
│  • Network source restrictions (IP allowlisting)         │
│  • Service Gateway for private VCN access                │
│  • Security lists and NSGs for traffic control           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Layer 2: Identity and Access Management
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • IAM policies with least privilege principle           │
│  • Group-based permission management                     │
│  • Separate read/write/admin permissions                 │
│  • Conditional policies based on network source          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Layer 3: Data Encryption
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Encryption at rest (AES-256)                          │
│  • Customer-managed keys in Vault service                │
│  • Automatic key rotation policies                       │
│  • HSM-backed key protection                             │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Layer 4: Data Protection
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Object versioning for recovery                        │
│  • Retention rules for immutability                      │
│  • Restricted delete permissions                         │
│  • MD5 checksum verification                             │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Layer 5: Monitoring and Auditing
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Audit service for API call logging                    │
│  • Object Storage access logging                         │
│  • Security alerts and notifications                     │
│  • Regular security audits                               │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ↓
Layer 6: Compliance and Governance
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Security zones for enforced compliance                │
│  • Retention rules for regulatory requirements           │
│  • Regular key rotation                                  │
│  • Documented security procedures                        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Security Best Practices Summary:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Object Storage Security Checklist                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Initial Setup:
☑ Configure IAM policies with least privilege
☑ Enable network source restrictions
☑ Create custom encryption keys in Vault
☑ Enable TLS 1.2 for data in transit (default)
☑ Configure security zones for critical compartments
☑ Restrict delete permissions to minimum users

Ongoing Operations:
☑ Rotate encryption keys periodically
☑ Review audit logs regularly
☑ Enable object versioning for critical buckets
☑ Verify MD5 checksums after uploads
☑ Configure retention rules for compliance
☑ Monitor for unusual access patterns
☑ Maintain updated IAM policies
☑ Document security procedures

Monitoring and Auditing:
☑ Enable Object Storage access logging
☑ Integrate audit logs with SIEM systems
☑ Set up alerts for suspicious activities
☑ Perform regular security assessments
☑ Track delete operations
☑ Review failed access attempts

Compliance:
☑ Implement retention rules for regulatory requirements
☑ Maintain audit trail for compliance reporting
☑ Document key rotation schedule
☑ Ensure encryption meets compliance standards
☑ Regularly review and update security policies
```

**Key Security Principles:**

- **Defense in Depth:** Multiple security layers provide comprehensive protection

- **Least Privilege:** Grant minimum permissions required for tasks

- **Encryption Everywhere:** Protect data at rest and in transit

- **Continuous Monitoring:** Regular auditing and monitoring detect threats

- **Automated Compliance:** Security zones enforce best practices automatically

- **Data Integrity:** MD5 checksums ensure data not corrupted

- **Access Control:** Network and identity-based restrictions limit exposure

- **Key Management:** Customer-managed keys provide control and compliance

- **Versioning and Retention:** Protect against accidental and malicious data loss

- **Regular Rotation:** Periodic key rotation reduces risk of compromise

**Summary:**

- Object Storage security requires comprehensive initial setup and ongoing routine maintenance

- Use IAM policies, network restrictions, and encryption for multi-layered security

- Customer-managed keys in Vault service provide better control than default keys

- TLS 1.2 encrypts data in transit by default between clients and endpoints

- Security zones automatically enforce Oracle security best practices

- Routine tasks include key rotation, security auditing, and integrity verification

- Object versioning and retention rules protect against data loss

- Restrict delete permissions to minimum set of users

- MD5 checksum verification ensures data integrity during transfers

- Comprehensive security approach combines technical controls with governance policies

================================================================================

**Pre-Authenticated Requests (PARs):**

**Overview:**

- Pre-Authenticated Requests (PARs) provide way to grant temporary access to Object Storage resources without requiring users to have OCI credentials

- Alternative to making buckets public (not recommended security practice)

- Generates unique URL that anyone can use to access specified bucket or object

- Enables secure sharing with external parties, partners, or systems

**Key Concept:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│         Pre-Authenticated Request Access Model                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Traditional Access (Requires Credentials):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  User → OCI Credentials (API Keys) → IAM Authentication  │
│         → Access Object Storage                          │
│                                                          │
│  Problem: External users need OCI accounts and keys      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Pre-Authenticated Request (No Credentials):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  User → PAR URL → Direct Access to Object Storage        │
│                                                          │
│  Benefit: No OCI credentials required                    │
│           Simple URL-based access                        │
│           Time-limited access                            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**How Pre-Authenticated Requests Work:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              PAR Creation and Usage Workflow                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Step 1: Creator Creates PAR
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  OCI User with permissions              │
│  • Navigates to bucket or object        │
│  • Creates Pre-Authenticated Request    │
│  • Specifies access type and scope      │
│  • Sets expiration date                 │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 2: Unique URL Generated
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Object Storage generates unique URL    │
│  • Cryptographically secure token       │
│  • Embedded permissions and expiration  │
│  • Cannot be guessed or derived         │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 3: URL Shared with Users
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Creator shares URL with intended users │
│  • Via email, documentation, etc.       │
│  • No need to share credentials         │
│  • Users can be external to OCI         │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 4: Users Access Resources
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Users access using standard tools      │
│  • Web browsers (for downloads)         │
│  • curl, wget (command line)            │
│  • HTTP clients (applications)          │
│  • No authentication required           │
└───────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                ↓
Step 5: PAR Expires (Automatic)
┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  After expiration date                  │
│  • URL becomes invalid                  │
│  • Access automatically revoked         │
│  • Security maintained                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**PAR Configuration Options:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Pre-Authenticated Request Parameters                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Target (Required)
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  Choose what to grant access to:                           │
   │                                                            │
   │  Option A: Entire Bucket                                   │
   │  • Access to ALL objects in the bucket                     │
   │  • Can list, read, or write all objects                    │
   │  • Useful for bulk operations                              │
   │                                                            │
   │  Option B: Specific Object                                 │
   │  • Access to ONE specific object only                      │
   │  • Most restrictive and secure                             │
   │  • Ideal for sharing single file                           │
   │                                                            │
   │  Option C: Objects with Prefix                             │
   │  • Access to objects matching specified prefix             │
   │  • Example: "reports/2024/" grants access to all           │
   │    objects starting with that prefix                       │
   │  • Useful for directory-like access                        │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Access Type (Required)
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  Three Access Type Options:                                │
   │                                                            │
   │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
   │  │  Permit Object Reads                             │     │
   │  │  • Download objects                              │     │
   │  │  • Read object content                           │     │
   │  │  • Get object metadata                           │     │
   │  │  • Most common for sharing files                 │     │
   │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
   │                                                            │
   │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
   │  │  Permit Object Writes                            │     │
   │  │  • Upload new objects                            │     │
   │  │  • Overwrite existing objects                    │     │
   │  │  • Useful for file submission/upload scenarios   │     │
   │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
   │                                                            │
   │  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐     │
   │  │  Permit Reads and Writes                         │     │
   │  │  • Both download AND upload                      │     │
   │  │  • Full read/write access (except delete)        │     │
   │  │  • Maximum flexibility for collaboration         │     │
   │  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘     │
   │                                                            │
   │  IMPORTANT: PARs CANNOT be used to DELETE objects or      │
   │             buckets (deletion not supported)               │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Expiration Date (Required)
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Must specify when PAR expires                           │
   │  • Required field - cannot create PAR without expiration   │
   │  • NO time limit restrictions                              │
   │  • Can set as far in future as needed                      │
   │  • After expiration, URL becomes invalid automatically     │
   │                                                            │
   │  Examples:                                                 │
   │  • 24 hours (temporary share)                              │
   │  • 7 days (week-long access)                               │
   │  • 1 year (long-term partner access)                       │
   │  • 10 years (extended access period)                       │
   │                                                            │
   │  Best Practice:                                            │
   │  • Set shortest expiration that meets requirements         │
   │  • Shorter durations = better security                     │
   │  • Can always create new PAR if needed                     │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

4. Name (Optional)
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Descriptive name for PAR (for your reference)           │
   │  • Helps identify purpose when managing multiple PARs      │
   │  • Example: "Q1-Reports-Partner-Access"                    │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Access Type Matrix:**

```
┌────────────────────────────┬────────┬────────┬─────────────┐
│  Operation                 │  Read  │ Write  │ Read+Write  │
├────────────────────────────┼────────┼────────┼─────────────┤
│  Download Object           │   ✓    │   ✗    │      ✓      │
│  View Object Metadata      │   ✓    │   ✗    │      ✓      │
│  List Objects (if bucket)  │   ✓    │   ✗    │      ✓      │
│  Upload Object             │   ✗    │   ✓    │      ✓      │
│  Overwrite Object          │   ✗    │   ✓    │      ✓      │
│  Delete Object             │   ✗    │   ✗    │      ✗      │
│  Delete Bucket             │   ✗    │   ✗    │      ✗      │
└────────────────────────────┴────────┴────────┴─────────────┘

Key Restriction: PARs CANNOT be used for deletion operations
```

**Important PAR Characteristics:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              PAR Behavior and Restrictions                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Cannot Edit PAR
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  ⚠️  Once created, PAR cannot be modified                   │
   │                                                            │
   │  If you need to change:                                    │
   │  • Access type (read to write, etc.)                       │
   │  • Enable object listing                                   │
   │  • Change expiration date                                  │
   │  • Modify target scope                                     │
   │                                                            │
   │  Solution: Must create NEW Pre-Authenticated Request       │
   │           (and revoke/delete old one if needed)            │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Creator Dependency
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  ⚠️  CRITICAL: PAR validity depends on creator's           │
   │      permissions                                           │
   │                                                            │
   │  Scenarios that break PAR:                                 │
   │  • Creator's OCI account is deleted                        │
   │  • Creator loses permissions to access bucket/object       │
   │  • Creator's IAM policies are revoked                      │
   │                                                            │
   │  Result: PAR stops working even if not expired             │
   │                                                            │
   │  Key Principle:                                            │
   │  Users have access to bucket/object ONLY as long as        │
   │  PAR creator maintains required permissions                │
   │                                                            │
   │  Best Practice:                                            │
   │  • Create PARs using service accounts (not personal)       │
   │  • Use dedicated IAM groups for PAR creators               │
   │  • Monitor creator account status                          │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. No Deletion Capability
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  ✗ PARs CANNOT be used to delete buckets or objects       │
   │                                                            │
   │  Available Operations:                                     │
   │  ✓ Read (download, list, view metadata)                   │
   │  ✓ Write (upload, overwrite)                              │
   │  ✗ Delete (not supported)                                 │
   │                                                            │
   │  Security Benefit:                                         │
   │  Prevents accidental or malicious deletion via shared URLs │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

4. Standard HTTP Tools Compatible
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  PAR URLs work with standard HTTP/HTTPS tools:             │
   │                                                            │
   │  • Web Browsers (Chrome, Firefox, Safari, etc.)           │
   │  • Command-line tools (curl, wget, HTTPie)                │
   │  • Programming libraries (Python requests, Java HTTP)      │
   │  • File transfer tools (supporting HTTP)                   │
   │  • REST API clients (Postman, Insomnia)                   │
   │                                                            │
   │  No special OCI tools or SDKs required                     │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

5. Unique URL Format
