- Read-Only Shareable does not require clustered file system (no concurrent writes possible)

- Must install and configure clustered file system before using volume as Read/Write Shareable

================================================================================

**Boot Volumes:**

**Overview:**

- Boot volumes are remote boot disks that serve as the OS disk for bare metal and virtual machine instances

- Provide higher durability, faster performance, and lower launch times compared to local boot disks

- Encrypted by default — cannot disable encryption

- Support both backup and clone operations

**Boot Volume Key Characteristics:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Boot Volume Features                                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Encryption (Default, Always On)
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Encrypted at rest by default                            │
   │  • AES-256 encryption                                      │
   │  • Cannot disable encryption                               │
   │  • Consistent with Block Volume encryption policy          │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Performance
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Faster performance than local boot disks                │
   │  • Lower instance launch times                             │
   │  • Performance level can be changed after instance created │
   │  • Available performance levels:                           │
   │    - Balanced                                              │
   │    - Higher Performance                                    │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Durability
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Higher durability for bare metal and VM instances       │
   │  • Remote storage — data persists independently of         │
   │    instance hardware                                       │
   │  • Redundantly replicated across storage servers           │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

4. Backup and Clone Support
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Backup operation: Point-in-time snapshot of boot volume │
   │  • Clone operation: Full copy of boot volume               │
   │  • Both operations supported for boot volumes              │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Boot Volume Sizes:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Boot Volume Size Options                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Default Boot Volume Sizes:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                          │
│  Linux:    46.6 GB  (default)                            │
│  Windows:  47 GB    (default)                            │
│                                                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Custom Size Options (at Launch):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Use selected image's default size, OR                 │
│  • Specify custom size from 50 GB up to 32 TB            │
│                                                          │
│  Range: 50 GB ──────────────────────────────▶ 32 TB     │
│                                                          │
│  • Custom size specified at instance launch time         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Size Summary Table:
┌──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
│  OS / Setting            │  Size                        │
├──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Linux Default           │  46.6 GB                     │
│  Windows Default         │  47 GB                       │
│  Minimum Custom Size     │  50 GB                       │
│  Maximum Custom Size     │  32 TB                       │
└──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘
```

**Preserving Boot Volume on Termination:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Boot Volume Preservation at Termination                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Default Behavior:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Terminate Instance → Boot volume deleted (default)      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

With Preserve Boot Volume Option:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Terminate Instance → Boot volume PRESERVED              │
│                       (data retained)                    │
│                            │                             │
│                            ▼                             │
│               Launch new instance using                  │
│               preserved boot volume                      │
│               (same OS/data, different shape)            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Key Use Case:
• Terminate old instance → Preserve boot volume
• Launch new instance with different shape
• Reuse existing OS configuration and data
• Enables scaling up or down without data loss
```

**Changing Boot Volume Performance:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Boot Volume Performance Levels                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Available After Instance Creation:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                          │
│  Balanced          ──▶  Standard performance level       │
│                         Good for most workloads          │
│                                                          │
│  Higher Performance ──▶ Elevated IOPS and throughput     │
│                         For performance-sensitive OS     │
│                         workloads                        │
│                                                          │
│  Note: Performance level can be changed at any time      │
│        after instance has been created                   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Boot Volume for Troubleshooting:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Boot Volume Troubleshooting Use Case                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Scenario: Instance not booting / OS issues
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                          │
│  Step 1: Detach boot volume from problem instance        │
│               ↓                                          │
│  Step 2: Attach detached boot volume to a healthy        │
│          instance AS A BLOCK VOLUME (data disk)          │
│               ↓                                          │
│  Step 3: Access and repair files on the attached volume  │
│          from within the healthy instance                │
│               ↓                                          │
│  Step 4: Detach from healthy instance                    │
│               ↓                                          │
│  Step 5: Reattach as boot volume to original instance    │
│          (or launch new instance from it)                │
│                                                          │
│  Key: Boot volume can be attached to another instance    │
│       as a block volume for diagnostics/repair           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Boot Volume vs Block Volume Summary:**

```
┌──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
│  Attribute                   │   Boot Volume        │   Block Volume       │
├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│  Purpose                     │  OS disk             │  Data disk           │
│  Default Size (Linux)        │  46.6 GB             │  1 TB                │
│  Default Size (Windows)      │  47 GB               │  1 TB                │
│  Min/Max Size                │  50 GB – 32 TB       │  50 GB – 32 TB       │
│  Encryption at Rest          │  Yes (default)       │  Yes (default)       │
│  Backup Support              │  Yes                 │  Yes                 │
│  Clone Support               │  Yes                 │  Yes                 │
│  Preserve on Termination     │  Optional            │  Yes (independent)   │
│  Can Detach and Reattach     │  Yes                 │  Yes                 │
│  Performance Tunable         │  Yes (post-creation) │  Yes                 │
└──────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
```

**Key Points:**

- Boot volumes are remote, encrypted (AES-256) boot disks for VM and bare metal instances

- Provide higher durability, faster performance, and lower launch times

- Both backup and clone operations are supported

- Performance level (Balanced or Higher Performance) can be changed after instance creation

- Default size: 46.6 GB for Linux, 47 GB for Windows; custom size: 50 GB to 32 TB

- Boot volume can be preserved when terminating an instance to reuse with a new instance

- For troubleshooting: detach boot volume from problem instance and attach to healthy instance as a block volume

================================================================================

**Block Volume Performance:**

**Overview:**

- Block Volume service uses NVMe-based storage infrastructure designed for consistency

- Offers flexible and elastic performance — adjustable at any time

- Default performance level at creation: Balanced

- Performance controlled via slider or Volume Performance Unit (VPU) control in console

- Four performance levels: Lower Cost, Balanced, Higher Performance, Ultra High Performance

**Volume Performance Units (VPUs):**

- VPUs (Volume Performance Units) are the unit used to purchase performance above the base level

- Purchased per GB per month

- More VPUs = higher IOPS and throughput

**Performance Levels Overview:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Block Volume Performance Levels                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Lower Cost → Balanced → Higher Performance → Ultra High Performance
  (0 VPU)    (10 VPU)      (20 VPU)           (30–120 VPU)

Performance increases ──────────────────────────────────────────────▶
Cost increases ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶
```

**1. Lower Cost:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Lower Cost Performance Level                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  IOPS:        2 IOPS per GB                                          │
│               Max: 3,000 IOPS per volume                             │
│                                                                      │
│  Throughput:  240 KBPS per GB                                        │
│               Max: 480 MBPS per volume                               │
│                                                                      │
│  VPU Cost:    0 VPUs — pay storage cost only (no VPU charge)        │
│                                                                      │
│  Availability: Block volumes ONLY (NOT available for boot volumes)  │
│                                                                      │
│  Recommended for:                                                    │
│  • Throughput-intensive workloads                                    │
│  • Streaming workloads                                               │
│  • Log processing                                                    │
│  • Data warehouses                                                   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**2. Balanced (Default):**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Balanced Performance Level (Default)                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  IOPS:        60 IOPS per GB (linear scaling)                        │
│               Max: 25,000 IOPS per volume                            │
│                                                                      │
│  Throughput:  480 KBPS per GB                                        │
│               Max: 480 MBPS per volume                               │
│                                                                      │
│  VPU Cost:    10 VPUs per GB per month                               │
│                                                                      │
│  Availability: Block volumes AND boot volumes                        │
│                                                                      │
│  Recommended for:                                                    │
│  • Most general-purpose workloads                                    │
│  • Good balance between performance and cost                         │
│  • Default for both block and boot volumes                           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**3. Higher Performance:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Higher Performance Level                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  IOPS:        75 IOPS per GB (linear scaling)                        │
│               Max: 50,000 IOPS per volume                            │
│                                                                      │
│  Throughput:  600 KBPS per GB                                        │
│               Max: 680 MBPS per volume                               │
│                                                                      │
│  VPU Cost:    20 VPUs per GB per month                               │
│                                                                      │
│  Availability: Block volumes AND boot volumes                        │
│                                                                      │
│  Recommended for:                                                    │
│  • Workloads with high I/O requirements                              │
│  • When Ultra High Performance not needed                            │
│  • High-performance application workloads                            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**4. Ultra High Performance:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Ultra High Performance Level                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  IOPS:        90–225 IOPS per GB (linear scaling)                    │
│               Max: scales with VPU selection (up to 225 IOPS/GB)    │
│                                                                      │
│  Throughput:  1,800 KBPS per GB                                      │
│               Max: 2,680 MBPS per volume                             │
│                                                                      │
│  VPU Cost:    30–120 VPUs per GB per month (purchasable range)       │
│                                                                      │
│  Availability: Block volumes ONLY (NOT available for boot volumes)  │
│                                                                      │
│  Recommended for:                                                    │
│  • Workloads with the highest I/O requirements                       │
│  • Best possible performance requirement                             │
│  • Most demanding database and OLTP workloads                        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Performance Levels Comparison Table:**

```
┌───────────────────┬────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
│  Attribute        │ Lower Cost │   Balanced   │    Higher        │   Ultra High         │
│                   │            │  (Default)   │  Performance     │   Performance        │
├───────────────────┼────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│  IOPS per GB      │  2         │  60          │  75              │  90–225              │
│  Max IOPS/volume  │  3,000     │  25,000      │  50,000          │  Scales with VPU     │
│  Throughput/GB    │  240 KBPS  │  480 KBPS    │  600 KBPS        │  1,800 KBPS          │
│  Max Throughput   │  480 MBPS  │  480 MBPS    │  680 MBPS        │  2,680 MBPS          │
│  VPU/GB/month     │  0         │  10          │  20              │  30–120              │
│  Block Volumes    │  Yes       │  Yes         │  Yes             │  Yes                 │
│  Boot Volumes     │  No        │  Yes         │  Yes             │  No                  │
│  Default Level    │  No        │  Yes         │  No              │  No                  │
└───────────────────┴────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
```

**Performance Scaling Visual:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              IOPS Scaling by Performance Level                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

            Lower Cost    Balanced    Higher Perf    Ultra High
IOPS/GB:       2            60            75          90–225
Max IOPS:    3,000        25,000        50,000        Highest

Low  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────▶ High
```

**Boot Volume vs Block Volume Performance Availability:**

```
┌───────────────────────────┬──────────────┬───────────────┐
│  Performance Level        │ Block Volume │  Boot Volume  │
├───────────────────────────┼──────────────┼───────────────┤
│  Lower Cost               │      ✓       │      ✗        │
│  Balanced                 │      ✓       │      ✓        │
│  Higher Performance       │      ✓       │      ✓        │
│  Ultra High Performance   │      ✓       │      ✗        │
└───────────────────────────┴──────────────┴───────────────┘

Boot volumes support Balanced and Higher Performance only.
Lower Cost and Ultra High Performance are block volumes only.
```

**Key Points:**

- Block Volume uses NVMe-based storage infrastructure for consistent, elastic performance

- Default performance level is Balanced for both block and boot volumes

- Performance can be changed at volume creation or at any point after creation

- Four performance levels: Lower Cost (0 VPU), Balanced (10 VPU), Higher Performance (20 VPU), Ultra High Performance (30–120 VPU)

- Lower Cost: 2 IOPS/GB, max 3,000 IOPS — block volumes only, no VPU cost, for throughput workloads (streaming, logs, data warehouse)

- Balanced: 60 IOPS/GB, max 25,000 IOPS — default, both block and boot, good for most workloads

- Higher Performance: 75 IOPS/GB, max 50,000 IOPS — both block and boot, for high I/O workloads

- Ultra High Performance: 90–225 IOPS/GB, max 2,680 MBPS throughput — block volumes only, 30–120 VPU, for highest I/O demands

- Lower Cost and Ultra High Performance are available for block volumes only (not boot volumes)

================================================================================

**Block Volume Dynamic Performance Scaling (Autotuning):**

**Overview:**

- Block Volume provides dynamic performance scaling with autotuning

- Enables the service to automatically adjust a volume's performance level to optimize performance

- Two types of autotuning: Performance-Based Autotuning and Detached-Volume Autotuning

- Both types can be enabled independently or together

**Two Types of Autotuning:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Dynamic Performance Scaling — Two Types                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Type 1: Performance-Based Autotuning                                │
│                                                                      │
│  • Adjusts volume performance BETWEEN a specified range              │
│  • Monitors volume performance metrics to decide adjustment          │
│  • You specify: Default VPUs/GB (floor) and Maximum VPUs/GB (ceiling)│
│  • Service scales up or down within that range based on load         │
│  • When disabled: performance returns to Default VPUs/GB setting     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Type 2: Detached-Volume Autotuning                                  │
│                                                                      │
│  • Adjusts performance based on whether volume is attached/detached  │
│  • When volume detached → automatically set to Lower Cost (0 VPU)    │
│  • When volume reattached → restored to Default VPUs/GB setting      │
│  • Applies to both block volumes and boot volumes                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Type 1 — Performance-Based Autotuning:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Performance-Based Autotuning Detail                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Configuration Parameters:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Default VPUs per GB                                     │
│  • Lowest performance level volume will be adjusted to   │
│  • Acts as floor for performance scaling                 │
│  • Cannot be set to 0 or 120                             │
│                                                          │
│  Maximum VPUs per GB                                     │
│  • Highest performance level volume will be adjusted to  │
│  • Acts as ceiling for performance scaling               │
│  • Must be at least 10 VPUs/GB HIGHER than Default VPU  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Scaling Range Visualization:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                          │
│  0 VPU                                         120 VPU  │
│  │──────────[Default VPU]══════[Maximum VPU]────│       │
│                    ↑                    ↑               │
│                  Floor               Ceiling            │
│                                                          │
│  Service auto-scales within [Default ↔ Maximum] range   │
│                                                          │
│  Constraints:                                            │
│  • Default VPU ≠ 0 and ≠ 120                            │
│  • Maximum VPU ≥ Default VPU + 10                       │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Metrics Used for Autotuning Decisions:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Block Volume monitors these metrics to determine load:  │
│                                                          │
│  1. Volume Throttled Operations                          │
│     • Total sum of all I/O operations throttled          │
│       during a given time interval                       │
│     • High throttling → service increases performance    │
│                                                          │
│  2. Volume Guaranteed VPUs per GB                        │
│     • Currently guaranteed VPU level                    │
│                                                          │
│  3. Volume Guaranteed IOPS                               │
│     • Currently guaranteed IOPS for the volume          │
│                                                          │
│  4. Volume Guaranteed Throughput                         │
│     • Currently guaranteed throughput for the volume    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Behavior When Disabled:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Turning off performance-based autotuning →              │
│  Volume performance reverts to Default VPUs/GB setting   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Type 2 — Detached-Volume Autotuning:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Detached-Volume Autotuning Detail                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Attach/Detach Behavior:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                          │
│  Volume DETACHED from instance                           │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Performance automatically set to Lower Cost (0 VPU)    │
│  (saves cost while volume is idle/unattached)            │
│                                                          │
│  Volume REATTACHED to instance                           │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Performance restored to Default VPUs/GB setting        │
│                                                          │
│  If Performance-Based Autotuning also enabled:           │
│  Further dynamic scaling takes effect after reattach     │
│                                                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Applies To:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ✓ Block volumes                                         │
│  ✓ Boot volumes                                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Cost Benefit:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Detached volumes cost reduced to 0 VPU automatically    │
│  No manual intervention required to reduce idle costs    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Combined Autotuning Workflow:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Combined Autotuning (Both Types Enabled)                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Volume attached, both autotuning types enabled:

DETACH event:
┌──────────┐     Detach     ┌───────────────────────────────┐
│  Volume  │ ─────────────▶ │  Lower Cost (0 VPU)           │
│ Running  │                │  Detached-volume autotune      │
└──────────┘                └───────────────────────────────┘

REATTACH event:
┌───────────────────────────────┐   Reattach   ┌──────────────────────┐
│  Lower Cost (0 VPU)           │ ────────────▶ │  Default VPUs/GB     │
│  Detached state               │              │  (from config)       │
└───────────────────────────────┘              └──────────┬───────────┘
                                                          │
                                                          ▼
                                               Performance-Based Autotune
                                               monitors metrics and scales
                                               between Default ↔ Maximum VPU
                                               based on workload demand
```

**Configuration Constraints Summary:**

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Performance-Based Autotuning Rules                                │
├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Default VPUs/GB   ≠ 0                                             │
│  Default VPUs/GB   ≠ 120                                           │
│  Maximum VPUs/GB   ≥ Default VPUs/GB + 10                          │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Example Valid Configuration:
  Default = 10 VPU → Maximum must be ≥ 20 VPU
  Default = 20 VPU → Maximum must be ≥ 30 VPU
  Default = 50 VPU → Maximum must be ≥ 60 VPU
```

**Key Points:**

- Dynamic performance scaling provides two autotuning types: performance-based and detached-volume

- Performance-Based Autotuning: automatically scales between a specified Default VPU (floor) and Maximum VPU (ceiling) based on monitored metrics

- Metrics used: Volume Throttled Operations, Guaranteed VPUs/GB, Guaranteed IOPS, Guaranteed Throughput

- Default VPUs/GB cannot be 0 or 120; Maximum VPUs/GB must be at least 10 higher than Default

- Disabling performance-based autotuning reverts volume to Default VPUs/GB

- Detached-Volume Autotuning: automatically sets performance to Lower Cost (0 VPU) when volume detached; restores to Default VPUs/GB when reattached

- Detached-volume autotuning applies to both block volumes and boot volumes

- Both types can be combined — detached-volume fires first, then performance-based takes effect after reattach

================================================================================

**Block Volume Resizing:**

**Overview:**

- Block volumes can be expanded (increased in size) at any time

- Size can ONLY be increased — decreasing volume size is NOT supported

- After any resize, the first backup taken on the resized volume will be a full backup (not incremental)

- Three methods to resize: Online resizing, Offline resizing, Restore/Clone to larger volume

**Critical Rule:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Volume Resize Golden Rule                              │
│                                                                     │
│  ✓ Can INCREASE volume size (e.g., 1 TB → 2 TB)                    │
│  ✗ CANNOT DECREASE volume size (e.g., 1 TB → 512 GB) — FORBIDDEN   │
│                                                                     │
│  Maximum size: 32 TB                                                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Method 1 — Online Resizing (Recommended):**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Online Resizing                                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

What It Is:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Expand volume size WITHOUT detaching from instance      │
│  • Volume stays attached and online during resize        │
│  • Preferred method — avoids application impact          │
│  • Recommended for production workloads                  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Pre-Requisite — Cross-Region Replication:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ⚠️  If cross-region replication is enabled:              │
│  1. Disable cross-region replication FIRST               │
│  2. Perform the resize                                   │
│  3. Re-enable cross-region replication after resize      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Steps After Online Resize:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                          │
│  Resize via OCI Console / CLI                            │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Run rescan commands inside the instance                 │
│  (to detect new volume size at OS level)                 │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Extend the partition manually                           │
│  (to use the additional space)                           │
│                                                          │
│  Note: Both rescan AND extend partition required         │
│        because volume remains attached during resize     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Method 2 — Offline Resizing (Detached Volume):**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Offline Resizing — Option A: Detach First              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Steps:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                          │
│  Step 1: Detach volume from instance                     │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Step 2: Expand the volume size (via console/CLI)        │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Step 3: Reattach volume to instance                     │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Step 4: Extend the partition manually                   │
│           (NO rescan commands needed — volume            │
│            was detached during resize)                   │
│                                                          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Advantage:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Simpler post-resize steps (no rescan required)        │
│  • Partition extension only                              │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Method 3 — Restore Backup to Larger Volume:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Offline Resizing — Option B: Restore from Backup       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Steps:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Take a volume backup                                  │
│  • While restoring backup, specify a larger target size  │
│  • A new, larger volume is created from the backup       │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Method 4 — Clone to Larger Volume:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Offline Resizing — Option C: Clone to Larger           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Steps:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Clone an existing volume                              │
│  • Specify a larger size for the new clone               │
│  • A new, larger volume is created as the clone          │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Post-Resize Steps Comparison:**

```
┌────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────┬─────────────────────┐
│  Step Required                 │  Online Resize       │  Offline Resize     │
│                                │  (Attached)          │  (Detached First)   │
├────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────┼─────────────────────┤
│  Detach volume before resize   │  No                  │  Yes                │
│  Run rescan commands (in OS)   │  Yes (required)      │  No (not needed)    │
│  Extend partition              │  Yes (required)      │  Yes (required)     │
└────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────┴─────────────────────┘
```

**All Resize Methods Summary:**

```
┌───────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Method                       │  Description                               │
├───────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Online Resize (Recommended)  │  Expand attached volume without detaching; │
│                               │  requires rescan + extend partition        │
│  Offline Resize (Detach)      │  Detach first, resize, reattach;           │
│                               │  requires extend partition only            │
│  Restore Backup to Larger     │  Restore existing backup specifying        │
│                               │  larger size at restore time               │
│  Clone to Larger              │  Clone existing volume at larger size      │
└───────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key Points:**

- Volume size can ONLY be increased — decreasing size is not supported

- Maximum volume size is 32 TB

- Online resizing is the recommended method — no detach required, no application impact

- If cross-region replication is enabled, must disable it before resizing, then re-enable after

- Online resize requires: rescan commands inside OS + extend partition manually

- Offline resize (detach first) only requires: extend partition (no rescan needed)

- After any resize, the first backup on the resized volume will be a full backup

- Three offline options: detach and expand, restore backup to larger size, clone to larger size

================================================================================

**Block Volume Backups:**

**Overview:**

- Point-in-time snapshots of Block Volume data

- Encrypted and stored in OCI Object Storage

- Can be restored as new volumes to any Availability Domain within the same region

- Source volume tags automatically included in the backup

- Provides disaster recovery within the same region, and cross-region DR when copied

**Backup Architecture:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Block Volume Backup and Restore Flow                   │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                         Same Region                                  │
│                                                                      │
│  Availability Domain 1          Object Storage         AD 2 / AD 3  │
│  ┌───────────────────┐              ┌───────────┐                    │
│  │  Block Volume     │──[Backup]──▶ │  Backup   │                    │
│  │  (AD 1)           │              │  stored   │                    │
│  └───────────────────┘              └─────┬─────┘                    │
│  ┌───────────────────┐                    │ [Restore]                │
│  │  Instance (AD 1)  │                    ▼                          │
│  │  (attached)       │             ┌─────────────────┐               │
│  └───────────────────┘             │  New Volume     │               │
│                                    │  (AD 2)         │               │
│                                    └────────┬────────┘               │
│                                             │ [Attach]               │
│                                    ┌────────▼────────┐               │
│                                    │  Instance (AD 2)│               │
│                                    └─────────────────┘               │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Cross-Region DR:
┌──────────────────────┐    [Copy Backup]    ┌──────────────────────┐
│   Source Region      │ ──────────────────▶ │  Destination Region  │
│   Object Storage     │                     │  Object Storage      │
│   (backup stored)    │                     │  (backup copied)     │
└──────────────────────┘                     └──────────────────────┘
Rebuild applications in destination region if source region has disaster
```

**Backup Initiation Methods:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Two Ways to Initiate a Backup                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  1. Manual Backup (On-Demand)                                        │
│                                                                      │
│  • One-off, ad-hoc backup triggered manually                         │
│  • You choose: Incremental OR Full backup                            │
│  • Can be taken when volume is attached or detached                  │
│  • Useful for pre-change snapshots (before patches, upgrades, etc.)  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  2. Policy-Based Backup (Scheduled)                                  │
│                                                                      │
│  • Defines a set backup schedule and retention period                │
│  • Two kinds of policies:                                            │
│    a) Oracle-Defined Policies (predefined, cannot modify)            │
│    b) User-Defined Policies   (custom, fully configurable)           │
│                                                                      │
│  • User-defined policies support scheduled cross-region backups      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Backup Types:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Two Volume Backup Types                                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Incremental Backup                                                  │
│                                                                      │
│  • Includes only changes since the LAST backup                       │
│  • Smaller size, faster to complete                                  │
│  • Depends on existence of a previous backup                         │
│                                                                      │
│  Special Case:                                                       │
│  If the FIRST backup on a volume is created as incremental,          │
│  it is effectively a FULL backup (no previous backup to diff from)   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Full Backup                                                         │
│                                                                      │
│  • Includes ALL data since the volume was created                    │
│  • Larger size, takes longer                                         │
│  • Self-contained — no dependency on previous backups                │
│                                                                      │
│  Automatic Full Backup Triggers:                                     │
│  • First backup ever on a new volume                                 │
│  • First backup after a volume has been resized                      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Oracle-Defined Backup Policies:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Oracle-Defined Backup Policies                         │
│              (Predefined — Cannot Be Modified)                      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  BRONZE Policy                                                       │
├────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Monthly Incremental   │  Runs on 1st day of month; retained 12 mos │
│  Yearly Incremental    │  Runs in early January; retained 5 years    │
└────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  SILVER Policy  (includes everything in Bronze, plus:)               │
├────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Weekly Incremental    │  Runs on Sunday; retained for 4 weeks       │
│  Monthly Incremental   │  (from Bronze) Retained 12 months           │
│  Yearly Incremental    │  (from Bronze) Retained 5 years             │
└────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  GOLD Policy  (includes everything in Silver + Bronze, plus:)        │
├────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Daily Incremental     │  Runs daily; retained for 7 days            │
│  Weekly Incremental    │  (from Silver) Retained 4 weeks             │
│  Monthly Incremental   │  (from Bronze) Retained 12 months           │
│  Yearly Incremental    │  (from Bronze) Retained 5 years             │
└────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Oracle-Defined Policy Comparison Table:**

```
┌──────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Policy          │  Backup Schedules and Retention                        │
├──────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Bronze          │  Monthly (1st of month, 12 months retention)           │
│                  │  Yearly  (early Jan, 5 years retention)                │
├──────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Silver          │  Weekly  (Sunday, 4 weeks retention)          ← added  │
│                  │  Monthly (1st of month, 12 months retention)           │
│                  │  Yearly  (early Jan, 5 years retention)                │
├──────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Gold            │  Daily   (daily, 7 days retention)            ← added  │
│                  │  Weekly  (Sunday, 4 weeks retention)                   │
│                  │  Monthly (1st of month, 12 months retention)           │
│                  │  Yearly  (early Jan, 5 years retention)                │
└──────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Each policy is cumulative — Gold includes all Silver and Bronze schedules.
```

**User-Defined Backup Policies:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              User-Defined Backup Policies                           │
│              (Custom — Fully Configurable)                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Policy Structure:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Backup Policy                                           │
│  └── One or more Schedules                               │
│       ├── Schedule 1: Daily, retain 7 days               │
│       ├── Schedule 2: Weekly, retain 4 weeks             │
│       └── Schedule 3: Monthly, retain 3 months           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Schedule Configuration Options:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Frequency:                                              │
│  • Daily                                                 │
│  • Weekly                                                │
│  • Monthly                                               │
│  • Yearly                                                │
│                                                          │
│  Retention Period:                                       │
│  • Defined in days, weeks, months, or years              │
│  • Set independently per schedule                        │
│                                                          │
│  Cross-Region Backups:                                   │
│  ✓ Can enable scheduled automated cross-region backups   │
│    via user-defined policies                             │
│    (Oracle-defined policies cannot do this)              │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Additional Backup Behaviors:**

- Backup can be taken whether volume is **attached** or **detached** from an instance

- Cross-region backup copy: manually copy backup to another region for cross-region DR

- First backup after volume resize is always a **full backup**

- First backup on a new volume created as incremental is effectively a **full backup**

- Source volume tags are automatically included in the backup

**Key Points:**

- Backups are encrypted and stored in OCI Object Storage

- Can restore to any Availability Domain within the same region

- Cross-region DR: copy backups to another region for region-wide disaster recovery

- Two initiation methods: Manual (on-demand, choose incremental/full) and Policy-based (scheduled)

- Two backup types: Incremental (changes since last backup) and Full (all data since creation)

- If first backup is set to incremental, it effectively becomes a full backup

- Three Oracle-defined policies: Bronze (monthly + yearly), Silver (adds weekly), Gold (adds daily)

- Oracle-defined policies cannot be modified

- User-defined policies support custom frequency, retention, and cross-region automated backups

- After resize, the first backup is always a full backup

================================================================================

**Block Volume Clones:**

**Overview:**

- Cloning creates a copy of an existing block volume without going through the backup and restore process

- A clone is a point-in-time, direct disk-to-disk deep copy of the source volume

- All data on the source volume at clone creation time is copied to the cloned volume

- Subsequent changes to the source volume are NOT reflected in the clone

**How Cloning Works:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Block Volume Clone Process                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Point in Time:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Source Volume (data at T=0)                             │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  File A, File B, File C, File D                 │    │
│  └──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘    │
│                         │ [Clone created at T=0]         │
└─────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────┘
                          │
              ┌───────────▼────────────┐
              │  Cloned Volume (T=0)   │
              │  File A, File B,       │
              │  File C, File D        │
              │  (identical snapshot)  │
              └────────────────────────┘

After Clone:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Source Volume (T=1, after changes)                      │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  File A (modified), File B, File C, File D,     │    │
│  │  File E (new)                                   │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
│                                                          │
│  Cloned Volume (still T=0 snapshot)                      │
│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
│  │  File A (original), File B, File C, File D      │    │
│  │  (NOT updated — changes not propagated)          │    │
│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Clone Availability and Background Copy:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Clone Operation Timeline                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Clone Initiated
      │
      ▼
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Clone operation occurs IMMEDIATELY                       │
│  State changes to "Available" immediately                 │
│  → Can attach and use the clone right away               │
└────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┘
                                 │
                                 ▼ (runs in background)
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Background data copy continues                          │
│  • Data copied from source to clone in background        │
│  • Can take up to 30 minutes depending on volume size    │
│  • Clone is usable during background copy                │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Clone Scope and Constraints:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Clone Scope Restrictions                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Clone created within:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ✓ Same Region                                           │
│  ✓ Same Availability Domain                              │
│  ✓ Same Tenancy                                          │
│                                                          │
│  Example:                                                │
│  Source Volume: AD 1, Phoenix Region, Tenancy X          │
│  Clone created: AD 1, Phoenix Region, Tenancy X          │
│  (same AD, same region, same tenant)                     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Cannot clone to:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ✗ Different Availability Domain                         │
│  ✗ Different Region                                      │
│  ✗ Different Tenancy                                     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Simultaneous Clone Limits:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Concurrent Clone Limits by Volume State                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Source Volume ATTACHED to an Instance                               │
│                                                                      │
│  • Must wait for first clone operation to complete                   │
│  • Only 1 clone can be created at a time                             │
│  • Sequential cloning only                                           │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Source Volume DETACHED from an Instance                             │
│                                                                      │
│  • Can create up to 10 clones simultaneously                         │
│  • Parallel cloning supported                                        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Summary:
┌──────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Volume State            │  Max Simultaneous Clones                │
├──────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Attached                │  1 (sequential only)                    │
│  Detached                │  10 (parallel)                          │
└──────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**What Can Be Cloned:**

- Individual block volumes

- Volume groups (clone an entire group at once)

**Clone vs Backup Comparison:**

```
┌──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
│  Attribute                   │       Clone          │       Backup         │
├──────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│  Method                      │  Disk-to-disk copy   │  Stored in Object    │
│                              │                      │  Storage             │
│  Speed to Usable             │  Immediate           │  Must restore first  │
│  Data Copy                   │  Background (≤30 min)│  Restore process     │
│  Cross-Region                │  No                  │  Yes (copy backup)   │
│  Cross-AD                    │  No                  │  Yes (restore to     │
│                              │                      │  any AD in region)   │
│  Cross-Tenancy               │  No                  │  No                  │
│  Point-in-Time               │  Yes                 │  Yes                 │
│  Changes After Copied        │  Not propagated      │  Not propagated      │
│  Volume Groups               │  Yes                 │  Yes                 │
└──────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘
```

**Use Cases:**

- **Environment Duplication:** Clone a production volume to quickly create an identical dev/test environment

- **Pre-change Snapshot:** Clone before making risky changes — instant rollback source

- **Parallel Testing:** Clone to multiple test instances simultaneously (up to 10 when detached)

- **Rapid Provisioning:** Faster than backup/restore when you need an immediate copy in the same AD

**Key Points:**

- Clone is a point-in-time, direct disk-to-disk deep copy of the source volume

- Clone operation is immediate — volume is usable as soon as state is "Available"

- Background data copy can take up to 30 minutes depending on volume size

- Changes to source after clone creation are NOT copied to the clone

- Clones are restricted to same region, same Availability Domain, and same tenancy

- Attached source: only 1 clone at a time (must wait for previous to complete)

- Detached source: up to 10 simultaneous clones

- Can clone both individual volumes and volume groups

================================================================================

**Block Volume Backup vs Clone — Comparison:**

**Overview:**

- Both backups and clones create point-in-time copies of block volume data

- Key differences: speed, cost, storage location, retention, and use cases

**Comprehensive Comparison Table:**

```
┌──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┐
│  Attribute                   │         Backup             │           Clone              │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Definition                  │  Point-in-time backup;     │  Point-in-time direct copy;  │
│                              │  restore as new volumes    │  single copy, no restore     │
│                              │                            │  process needed              │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Speed                       │  Minutes to hours          │  Seconds (immediately        │
│                              │                            │  available)                  │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Storage Location            │  Object Storage            │  Block Volume                │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Cost                        │  Lower (Object Storage     │  Higher (Block Volume        │
│                              │  pricing)                  │  pricing)                    │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Restore Multiple Volumes    │  Yes — restore backup      │  No — single copy each time  │
│                              │  as many new volumes       │                              │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Retention / Expiration      │  Policy backups expire     │  No expiration — clone is    │
│                              │  Manual backups don't      │  a regular volume            │
│                              │  expire                    │                              │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Retention Policy Support    │  Yes (Oracle-defined or    │  No (regular volume, no      │
│                              │  user-defined policies)    │  policy support)             │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Cross-Region                │  Yes (copy backup to       │  No (same region only)       │
│                              │  another region)           │                              │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Cross-AD (within region)    │  Yes (restore to any AD    │  No (same AD only)           │
│                              │  in same region)           │                              │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Volume Group Support        │  Yes                       │  Yes                         │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Data Unchanged Over Time    │  Yes — backup data is      │  No — clone is a live        │
│                              │  static                    │  volume that can be modified │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│  Primary Use Cases           │  Business continuity,      │  Rapid environment           │
│                              │  DR, compliance,           │  duplication, testing        │
│                              │  regulatory requirements   │                              │
└──────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┘
```

**Storage Location and Cost:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Storage Location Comparison                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Backup:                           Clone:
┌───────────────────────┐         ┌───────────────────────┐
│  Object Storage       │         │  Block Volume         │
│  • Lower cost tier    │         │  • Higher cost tier   │
│  • Designed for       │         │  • Full block volume  │
│    archival/backup    │         │    pricing applies    │
│  • Cheaper per GB     │         │  • More expensive     │
└───────────────────────┘         └───────────────────────┘

Conclusion: Backups cost less than clones for equivalent data size
```

**Retention and Expiration:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Retention / Expiration Behavior                        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Backup Expiration:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Policy-based backups:  EXPIRE (per retention settings)  │
│  Manual backups:        DO NOT expire                    │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Clone Expiration:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Clone is a regular block volume → NO expiration         │
│  Exists until manually deleted                           │
│  Billed as a normal block volume                         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Use Case Guide:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              When to Use Backup vs Clone                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Use BACKUP when:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ✓ Business continuity and disaster recovery needed      │
│  ✓ Cross-region DR is required                           │
│  ✓ Compliance / regulatory data retention required       │
│  ✓ Data must remain unchanged over time (static record)  │
│  ✓ Cost-efficient long-term retention preferred          │
│  ✓ Need to restore same backup to multiple volumes       │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Use CLONE when:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ✓ Need to rapidly duplicate an existing environment     │
│  ✓ Creating dev/test environment from production data    │
│  ✓ Need volume available immediately (seconds, not mins) │
│  ✓ No need for long-term retention or cross-region copy  │
│  ✓ Want to test changes without impacting source volume  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key Points:**

- Backup stores data in Object Storage (cheaper); Clone uses Block Volume (more expensive)

- Backup takes minutes to hours to complete; Clone is available in seconds

- Policy-based backups expire per retention settings; manual backups do not expire; clones never expire (regular volume)

- Backup can be restored to multiple new volumes; Clone creates a single copy each time

- Backup supports cross-region copy and cross-AD restore; Clone is restricted to same region, same AD, same tenancy

- Both support volume groups (boot + block volumes grouped together)

- Backup data remains static/unchanged over time — suitable for compliance requirements

- Clone is a live, modifiable volume — suitable for testing and environment duplication

================================================================================

**Block Volume Cross-Region Replication:**

**Overview:**

- Ongoing automatic asynchronous replication of block volumes to other regions

- Supports both boot volumes and block volumes

- Replication is asynchronous — not real-time, slight lag between source and replica

- Complementary to backups — NOT a replacement for backups

- Does not cause any downtime or performance impact on source volumes

**Backup vs Replication — Key Distinction:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Backup vs Cross-Region Replication                     │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Backup                      │  Cross-Region Replication              │
├──────────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Point-in-time snapshot      │  Current version of data (ongoing)     │
│  Static data                 │  Continuously updated replica          │
│  On-demand or scheduled      │  Automatic and continuous              │
│  Stored in Object Storage    │  Stored as block volume replica        │
└──────────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────┘

Both features are COMPLEMENTARY — use together for full data protection
```

**Replication Process:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Cross-Region Replication Flow                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Phase 1 — Initial Sync:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Source Region                     Destination Region    │
│  ┌──────────────────┐                                    │
│  │  Block Volume    │──[Initial Full Sync]──▶ Replica    │
│  │  (Source)        │                                    │
│  └──────────────────┘                                    │
│                                                          │
│  • Full data sync from source to replica                 │
│  • Can take hours depending on volume size               │
│    and amount of data written to volumes                 │
│  • No downtime on source during initial sync             │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Phase 2 — Continuous Replication (after initial sync):
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Source Region                     Destination Region    │
│  ┌──────────────────┐                                    │
│  │  Block Volume    │──[Async Ongoing]──▶ Replica        │
│  │  (changes)       │                                    │
│  └──────────────────┘                                    │
│                                                          │
│  • Asynchronous, ongoing replication                     │
│  • Replica stays close to current state of source        │
│  • No impact on source volume performance                │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Activating a Replica:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Creating a Volume from a Replica                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

To use replica data as a new volume:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Step 1: Activate the replica                            │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Step 2: Activation process clones the replica           │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Step 3: New volume created in destination region        │
│           ↓                                              │
│  Step 4: Attach new volume to instance in dest. region   │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Use Case: Primary region unavailable → activate replica → 
          new volume created → attach to DR instance → resume operations
```

**Limitations and Restrictions:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Cross-Region Replication Restrictions                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Cannot Resize with Replication Enabled
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  To resize a volume with replication enabled:              │
   │  Step 1: Disable replication (deletes the replica)         │
   │  Step 2: Resize the volume                                 │
   │  Step 3: Re-enable replication (starts from scratch)       │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Not Supported with Customer-Managed Vault Encryption Keys
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  ✗ Volumes encrypted with customer-managed Vault keys      │
   │    cannot use cross-region replication                     │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

3. Replication is Asynchronous
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Not real-time — some data lag expected                  │
   │  • Replica may not have the very latest writes             │
   │  • Not suitable as synchronous mirroring                   │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Destination Region Selection:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Region Pairing for Replication                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Source region determines available destination regions:

┌──────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Source Region       │  Available Destination Regions             │
├──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  UK West             │  UK South (London)                         │
│  US West (Phoenix)   │  US East (Ashburn), US West-2 (San Jose)   │
│  (varies by region)  │  Determined by Oracle based on region pair │
└──────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Prerequisite:
• Must be subscribed to the destination/target region before enabling replication
```

**Pricing:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Cross-Region Replication Costs                         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

1. Storage Cost (Destination Region)
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Billed for the volume replica in the destination region │
   │  • Billed at Block Storage Lower Cost pricing              │
   │  • Regardless of source volume's performance tier          │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

2. Network Cost
   ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  • Applicable network costs for data transfer              │
   │    between source and destination regions                  │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Enabling Cross-Region Replication:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Steps to Enable Replication                            │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Step 1: Verify subscription to target region
Step 2: Edit the volume properties in OCI Console
Step 3: Enable Cross-Region Replication toggle
Step 4: Select destination region
Step 5: Select availability domain (if applicable)
Step 6: Provide a name for the volume replica
Step 7: Save — initial sync begins automatically
```

**Use Cases:**

- **Disaster Recovery:** If primary region becomes unavailable, activate replica in destination region to restore operations

- **Migration:** Replicate data to a new region as part of a migration project

- **Business Expansion:** Expand into new geographies by replicating data to a new region

**Key Points:**

- Asynchronous, ongoing automatic replication to another region — supports block and boot volumes

- NOT a replacement for backups — backups provide point-in-time snapshots; replicas provide current data

- Initial sync can take hours; after that replication is continuous

- No downtime or performance impact on source volumes

- Cannot resize a volume while replication is enabled — must disable (deletes replica), resize, then re-enable

- Not supported for volumes encrypted with customer-managed Vault keys

- To use a replica: activate it → activation clones the replica → new volume created in destination region

- Replica billed at Lower Cost block storage pricing regardless of source volume's performance tier

- Additional network costs apply for data transfer between regions

- Must be subscribed to the destination region before enabling replication

- Source region determines which destination regions are available

================================================================================

**Block Volume Groups:**

**Overview:**

- OCI Block Volume service allows grouping multiple volumes together into a volume group

- A volume group can contain both boot volumes (OS/system disks) and block volumes (data disks)

- Used to create point-in-time, crash-consistent volume group backups and clones

- Available at **no additional charge**

**Volume Group Specifications:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Volume Group Limits                                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Parameter                 │  Limit                                  │
├────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Max Volumes per Group     │  32 volumes                             │
│  Max Combined Size         │  128 TB total                           │
│  Volume Types Supported    │  Boot volumes + Block volumes           │
│  Groups per Volume         │  1 (each volume in only one group)      │
│  Additional Cost           │  None (no charge for volume groups)     │
└────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────┘

Note: Volumes within a group can vary in size, but total combined
      size of all volumes must not exceed 128 TB
```

**Volume Group Use Case — Multi-Tier Application:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Enterprise Application Volume Group                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                   Volume Group                                  │
  │                                                                 │
  │  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌────────────────┐  │
  │  │   Web Tier      │  │   App Tier      │  │   DB Tier      │  │
  │  │  ┌──────────┐   │  │  ┌──────────┐   │  │  ┌──────────┐  │  │
  │  │  │ Boot Vol │   │  │  │ Boot Vol │   │  │  │ Boot Vol │  │  │
  │  │  └──────────┘   │  │  └──────────┘   │  │  └──────────┘  │  │
  │  │  ┌──────────┐   │  │  ┌──────────┐   │  │  ┌──────────┐  │  │
  │  │  │Block Vol │   │  │  │Block Vol │   │  │  │Block Vol │  │  │
  │  │  └──────────┘   │  │  └──────────┘   │  │  └──────────┘  │  │
  │  └─────────────────┘  └─────────────────┘  └────────────────┘  │
  │                                                                 │
  │  All volumes backed up together as one point-in-time,          │
  │  crash-consistent snapshot                                      │
  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Benefit: Entire multi-tier application state captured at the same instant
```

**Volume Group Backup and Clone:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Volume Group Backup and Clone                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Volume Group Backup:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Point-in-time, crash-consistent backup of all volumes │
│    in the group simultaneously                           │
│  • Can be performed manually (on-demand)                 │
│  • Can be scheduled automatically using backup policies  │
│  • Retention period configurable via backup policy       │
│  • Can restore entire group of volumes from backup       │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Volume Group Clone:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Point-in-time, crash-consistent clone of all volumes  │
│    in the group simultaneously                           │
│  • Clones entire group at once (not individual volumes)  │
│  • Same restrictions as individual volume clones         │
│    (same region, same AD, same tenancy)                  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Volume Group Lifecycle Rules:**

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              Volume Group Deletion and Membership Rules             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Rule 1: Deleting a Volume Group
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Deletes the GROUP only                                │
│  • Individual volumes in the group are NOT deleted       │
│  • Volumes continue to exist independently after group   │
│    is deleted                                            │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Rule 2: Deleting a Volume that is in a Group
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  • Cannot directly delete a volume that is part of a     │
│    volume group                                          │
```
